# SPORK architecture (final)

Status: final architecture for owner review, 2026-11-03 (round 10 fixed hardware with 4 TB drives, owner decision plan C (E26); round 9 plan scheduled by gates, not engineering capacity; round 8 System 1 index from the beginning; section 8; round 7 native record versioning applied, `08-native-versioning-design.md`; round 6 reconciliation with `02-retrieval-speed.md` applied; round 5 review applied; round 4 folded in the research, Appendix A).
Inputs: `docs/spork/00-research-findings.md` (owner directives: replication not sharding, retrieval speed first, set-oriented retrieval like Recon compare, a link-analysis platform on top), three architecture proposals (single-replicated-cluster, replicated-tiered-storage, pragmatic-delivery-first), three judges' verdicts, four research notes (`research/intel-platform-data-layer.md`, `research/system-one-decision-models.md`, `research/embedding-models.md`, `research/system-one-multimodal-index.md`), four engine workstreams already implemented in worktrees (`workstreams/geo.md`, `workstreams/partial-text.md`, `workstreams/fulltext-topk.md`, `workstreams/vector-scale.md`) plus the read benchmark (`workstreams/retrieval-bench.md`), and spot checks of the SPORK tree (ArcadeDB 26.11.1-SNAPSHOT, Ratis 3.3.1) and of Recon (`recon-next/src`).
Base: replicated-tiered-storage (highest combined judge score), with fatal flaws fixed and the best ideas from the other two grafted in. Section 10 lists every decision and what was rejected. Ordering rule throughout: retrieval speed (read p50/p99 and read throughput) outranks write throughput and storage efficiency, and every modality must answer "over this set" as well as "over the corpus".

---

## Executive summary

**Replication, not sharding (for hot data, owner decision of 2026-11-03, plan C).** All 4 nodes hold a full copy of every hot database that Recon queries interactively (the hot period partitions, `identity`, `recdir`, `ops`, `audit`), in one Raft group; warm sealed period partitions are held on 2 of 4 nodes outside the group (E26, 0.8). No record is ever split across nodes. Bulky payloads that are never queried in place (image originals, face crops, backups after W3d, WORM audit anchors, and warm ad text once E17 lands) live in an S3-compatible object store and are referenced by hash.

**Layout keeps every database small.**
- The corpus is split by period into `rec_<p>` (structured records), `txt_<p>` (deduplicated ad text, full-text, style and text vectors) and `media_<p>` (image and face metadata, perceptual hashes and image and face codes), each capped at 120 GB (`txt_<p>` at the period boundary, about 73 GB hot, so its aging rewrite fits n4's slot, 3.5).
- A small `identity` registry holds only identifiers that link 2 or more records, plus the ontology registry, targets, cases, missions, watches, resolution decisions and the decision log. A `recdir` directory maps each upstream RID to its period, so hydrating a record costs two index probes (about 4 cold reads of 16 KB pages, 64 KB, plus a walk of the resident series roots and bloom pages, 2.5), run on a 32-way read pool. The point-lookup indexes and the Record, `recdir` and TextBody buckets are created with 16 KB pages, because the engine reads whole pages and the stock 256 KB index pages would make a 2,000-id hydrate move 1.3 GB.
- Databases link to each other only through canonical keys, never through SPORK RIDs.
- Capping database size caps the free space a resync needs. It does not cap resync volume: until E2 is re-scoped (3.6), any resync triggered by a purged Raft log re-downloads every database on the node.
- **LSM compaction is scheduled, not automatic.** In this engine every compacted page the leader writes ships through the Raft log, and the stock policy rewrites any index over 10,240 pages in full every 10 mutable pages: about 320 GB a day today for the `recdir` index alone against a 15 to 25 GB/day ingest budget. E0 disables automatic full compaction on every SPORK database and the plugin runs one full compaction at a time, off peak, under the `transient-slot` lease (0.4). The Raft log line is 120 GB.

**Recon keeps its semantics, and retrieval is set-oriented.** Most of Recon's analysis runs over an arbitrary set of record ids, not a query. SPORK therefore serves a batch hydrate (`POST /records:hydrate`) and set-scoped analysis endpoints that take `{ids[]}` and reproduce Recon's rules (link clusters with `linkOn`, place-anchored convergence, face clustering within a set), alongside the corpus-wide pushdown queries. Dossier assembly, watch-scan scoring and mission lead generation stay in Recon's TypeScript over hydrate; SPORK supplies capped footprints (Recon's 300 per token, max 2,000), leases, persisted state and audit (7.1). Above that, an **ObjectSet service** materializes any selection (ids, identifier keys, a saved set, a face set, a filter, a search-around) as a roaring bitmap keyed by its definition hash, so Recon compare's four modes run as projections and pairwise intersections over bitmaps with Recon's own field keyers (4b.1; the n-way shared and distinct algebra is a SPORK extension), and the 15 read operations of the intel-platform contract (hydrate, typed expand with per-type caps, degrees, k-hop counts, shortest paths, common neighbours, set algebra, facets, aggregation, timeline, geo, induced subgraph, provenance, watch diff, kNN) each have a budget and a SPORK mapping (4b).

**The data model is an ontology.** Object types, property types and link types with two-sided names are registered data, every object, property value and link can carry markings, asserted values carry a provenance struct, and merges mint a winner key that owns post-merge writes (Gotham's rule) so they split back out cleanly (2.7). RID and canonical-key rules are unchanged. **History is the engine's, not the graph's (round 7).** `Identity` and `MEMBER_OF` are system-versioned types (`ALTER TYPE ... VERSIONED true`): the engine stamps `_txFrom` and copies each superseded record image into an internal `_VersionHistory` store in the same transaction, so a merge or split deletes and recreates membership edges, the live edge list is the current view with no `validTo IS NULL` filter on any traversal, and `history.of`, `history.asOf` and `history.edges` answer the as-of questions. Reads that do not ask for history pay nothing (2.6, 2.7, 0.4).

**Faces.** ROC is the face engine of record and the only verifier.
- Recon's ArcFace model (InsightFace buffalo_l / w600k_r50, licensed for non-commercial research) is retired and nothing it produced is kept. Face recall uses YuNet (MIT) detection and AuraFace-v1 (Apache-2.0) 512-d embeddings in a corpus-wide `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` posting-list index of representative faces (S-FACE, 4c): 157 GB of disk at mid 3x and 0.83 GB of heap (1.35M int8 512-d centroids at 612 B), because only centroids are resident. Nothing ROC-derived enters an ANN index, and every shortlist is ROC 1:1 verified.
- ROC templates are stored as immutable gallery segments on every node.
- Interactive 1:N search runs over a representative gallery held in RAM and split 4 ways.
- An exhaustive all-faces search runs as an async job, so victim identification never loses recall.

**System 1 multimodal index (4c).** Seven embedding spaces (scene S-IMG, near-duplicate S-PDQ, face S-FACE, text S-TEXT, style S-STYLE, tattoo S-TATTOO, case documents S-DOC) with Apache or MIT licensed models (SigLIP 2 or PE-Core, PDQ, AuraFace-v1 + YuNet, gte-multilingual-base + the OpenSearch sparse encoder, LUAR, PP-OCRv5), 1-bit or PQ scan codes with 4-bit or int8 rerank, posting lists in LSM pages that replicate through Raft (one index per space in each period database, sharing one global codebook per space), int8 centroids on heap. It costs 453 B per record at mid (410 GB at 3x) and 2.6 GB of heap at mid 3x (0.83 GB today), ships from the beginning (round 8, owner directive of 2026-11-03): S-PDQ and S-TEXT with Recon's Q1 move, S-FACE, S-IMG, S-TATTOO and S-DOC in Q2 in one image pass once ROC and the model licences have answered (round 9), every backfill after H1, behind an IVF recall gate in Phase 0 with a proposed fallback onto the existing indexes (8). It moves the second-drive order trigger to about month 18.8 (2028-05) at mid (round 7: 19.1 before the version history line); building it earlier does not move that date, because the trigger already counted every space from month 0. The heap figure is why S-STYLE moves onto the IVF index in Q2 (round 9; round 5 put it in Q4): with the style jvector graphs and every space's centroids resident the 8 GB vector slice would overflow at month 12, and with S-STYLE moved it is 1.7 GB at the Q2 exit. Structured constraints (time, geo, set, case) are allow-list bitmaps, never embedded. System 2 verifiers (ROC face and tattoo 1:1, ROC age band, deterministic OCR normalizers, a cross-encoder on at most 50 texts) sit behind every shortlist.

**System 1 decision layer (4d).** A pluggable decider (state plus typed questions in, probabilities out: Jev hosted, Perplexity Decider self-hosted under Apache-2.0, deterministic rules as the fallback) routes query intent, prunes partitions, scores ingest priority, runs the entity-resolution fast pass with an analyst middle band, reranks shortlists and drives prefetch. Every decision is logged with its outcome, thresholds are data, and hosted inference on CHT data is a constraint, so the self-hosted path is required before any hosted provider is enabled. Round 10: no GPU will exist to run the self-hosted model, so the deterministic rules are the self-hosted path, and a hosted provider is limited to states without personal data with owner sign-off.

**Fixed hardware (round 10, 0.8).** The owner directed that the hardware will not change except a 4 TB drive expansion: 4 nodes x 126 GB RAM, no GPU, no H2, no second drive, no burst host; each node's 2 TB drive is replaced by a 4 TB one. On 4 TB (a 3,000 GB ceiling) with full replicas, mid crosses the ceiling at month 13.2 (about 2027-12) with the whole design and reaches 140% at 3x. The recommended path, for the owner's decision: the 4 TB replacement on every node by about 2027-01-15, ideally before the initial formation; combination A from Q2 (valves 3, 1 and 5, scene and tattoo codes on the hot window only), which holds mid to about 2028-01; and E26, warm sealed partitions on 2 of 4 nodes, in production by about 2027-07, which holds mid to about 2029-01 at 95% of the ceiling at 3x (with an object store, about 2029-07). **The owner chose this, plan C, on 2026-11-03**, over a retention window, so "replicate, do not shard" now holds for hot data only. Still open: scene and tattoo search on recent data only (without it plan C still holds mid to about 2029-01, at 96%), OCR on the daily stream only, and whether an object store exists. Encoding is CPU-only: the day-one spaces backfill in 9.4 days on the 16 encode cores, faces and hot-window scene in about 35 more, and OCR runs on the daily stream only. The figures below that assume 3.84 TB drives, a second drive or H2 are kept for the record and superseded by 0.8.

**Capacity, mid face scenario (section 0 has the arithmetic).**

| | Today (faces, no S1 index yet) | At 3x (faces and the S1 index) |
|---|---|---|
| Per-node footprint | 1,417 GB, 91% of the 1,560 GB planning ceiling (1,239 GB face-free, 79%) | 4,185 GB, 93% of the 4,520 GB expanded ceiling, 55% of the two-drive 7,560 GB ceiling (round 7: 43 GB of version history inside `identity`) |
| Fits on 2 TB? | Yes, without S1 codes, until the face-free crossing about 2027-02; no S1 or face backfill runs before the 4 TB expansion | No, in any scenario on the stock engine |
| Fits on 4 TB (round 10, 3,000 GB)? | Yes | No with full replicas (140%); 95% with plan C, combination A and E26, decided (0.8) |

Disk expansion is the plan, and at mid it is already due:
- **Order now.** Mid is past the 1,248 GB order trigger with or without faces (1,417 GB with, 1,239 GB without; round 6 added the 24 GB hot-tier PDQ band index). P0a can confirm the order, not delay it.
- **Install the 4 TB expansion (round 10; H1 in earlier rounds) before any backfill and before the face-free footprint crosses 1,560 GB** at month 3.7 (about 2027-02) at mid, 1.3 (2026-12) at high, 5.4 (2027-04) at low. Neither the S1 first-spaces backfill nor the W8 face backfill runs on a node without it (face-free plus S-PDQ and S-TEXT would be 1,558 GB, 100%, at mid at the Q1 exit), so for the index to ship with Recon's Q1 move the expansion is on every node by about 2027-01-15 (round 10: the CPU backfill takes 9.4 days and starts by 2027-01-20). The pressure valves (1.3) exist only from Q2 (they need the aging rewrite and the gallery), so they are not a bridge for H1.
- **What to add (superseded by 0.8, round 10: the drives go to 4 TB and nothing else is added):** one 3.84 TB NVMe per node (LVM on LUKS, a LUKS PV per drive, 0.6), with the same XFS filesystem grown online. The ceiling then becomes 4,520 GB. With the S1 index and the version history, mid crosses the 3,616 GB second-drive order trigger at month 18.8 (2028-05) and reaches 93% at 3x, so the second 3.84 TB drive (7,560 GB ceiling) is ordered inside the horizon at mid; low does not reach the trigger inside the horizon (73% at 3x); high (6,482 GB at 3x) buys two drives per node now.
- **Backups never land on node disk.** Until W3d they go to an off-node NFS or object-store gateway mount on an encrypted volume with KMS-held keys, because stock auto-backup archives are plaintext (5.6). Sealed period databases are archived once at seal, after deletion batches and when the S1 code backfill completes on them; only `identity`, `ops`, `audit`, `recdir` and the current period keep 7 dailies, so the mount is about 2.0 TB today and 4.5 TB at 3x with the S1 codes (1.8 and 4.1 TB without them; round 7 added the version history to the daily `identity` archive) instead of 4.0 and 12.5 TB (0.4). Transient slots (admission, n4 build, backup staging, full compaction) are mutually exclusive under one lease. No re-architecture is needed in any case.

**RAM.** RAM is the firmer constraint and the budget is fixed.
- The database JVM gets 56 GB on n1 to n3, including 10 GB of GC headroom and a 23 GB page cache. On n4 it gets 43 GB, and a separate 16 GB build slice runs offline builds and enrichment (1.4). A 3 GB encoder sidecar (int8 ONNX on CPU; round 10: no GPU will be added) runs on every node. The 8 GB vector residency slice holds the style search cache (0.4 GB from Q1, round 8), the S1 centroids (2.6 GB at mid 3x: 7.1M centroids at 36 B of location plus an int8 code plus a 64 B graph share, 4c.2) and, until S-STYLE moves onto the IVF index in Q2, the style jvector graphs (5.9 GB): with the graphs resident the slice reaches 8.0 GB at month 12 and 9.3 GB at 3x, with S-STYLE on the index it is 3.0 GB at mid 3x (1.4). Nothing per item is resident, which is what lets corpus-wide image and face recall fit where a 346 GB ArcFace index could not; whether nprobe 64 over 200-item postings reaches 90% recall at 100 is a P0a gate, not an anchor (4c.3).
- ROC gets a 45 GB cap per node. After reserving the case/watch and tattoo galleries it holds at most about 205M interactive face templates cluster-wide, with enough slack to absorb a node loss.
- At mid, the interactive gallery crosses that limit about month 15.5 (2028-02). With the hardware fixed (round 10) the fix is ROC indexed search (Q-ROC1) or representatives restricted to clusters active in the last 3 years, with the exhaustive job keeping recall; 256 GB RAM is no longer an option.

**Delivery.**
- **Q1, train 1 (rounds 8 and 9):** Recon moves onto SPORK with zero engine changes on its own track (plugin, hydrate and set-scoped endpoints, ETL, initial formation by leader-shipped snapshots with raised bootstrap, snapshot-retry, stale-follower and compaction settings, an Operating Context with a role claim in the classic console and missions, audit by data class with per-view image grants, a shadow-diff harness against Recon's production `ApiSource`), and the 4 TB expansion is installed (round 10); beside it the System 1 track builds `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` (E18, from the spike already in a worktree), `HAMMING`, the encoder sidecar and PDQ dedup and backfills S-PDQ and S-TEXT, so the index ships with the move. Engineering time is not a planning constraint (owner, round 9): the date, about 2027-01-29, is set by the Phase 0 gates, the initial load and formation, the shadow-diff harness and H1 (8).
- **Already done in worktrees** (merge in Q2, zero Q1 dependency): geo kNN and metre radii with Cypher geo seeks (was E9), n-gram, phonetic, reversed and phone analyzers with automaton fuzzy seek (was E11), BM25 top-K scoring on primitive arrays (`arcadedb.fullText.topK`, a key that exists only in the partial-text worktree until the Q2 merge, 4x on 1M documents, round 5), parallel per-bucket vector search with heap-budgeted rebuild permits (was E7), and native record versioning (`ALTER TYPE x VERSIONED true`, the `_VersionHistory` store and the `history.*` functions; E25, round 7, 284 tests green in the `native-versioning` worktree).
- **Q2 onward:** engine and plugin work is ordered by retrieval speed, then by Recon impact: cold install, the ObjectSet service and the graph procedures (set algebra, typed expand, degrees, common neighbours, induced subgraph), S-FACE, Raft log preservation, S-STYLE onto the IVF index and the remaining spaces (S-IMG, S-TATTOO, S-DOC, once P0d answers), the decision layer, per-index readiness, the fingerprint-free reconcile skip, the BM25 global-statistics hook and the E8 block-max layout, `.vecgraph` shipping and the intel-platform write endpoints (links, assertions, annotations, markings, actions), all as Q2 releases (train 2); then (Q3, train 3) the listener role, forced installs off the apply thread, restore-then-join, replicated commit events, engine row filters and per-edge-type adjacency.
- Two engine items change an on-disk format. Round 8 narrows the rule that kept both off Recon's critical path: `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` (a new index type behind the closed `Schema.INDEX_TYPE` enum, Q1) is additive and may ride the Q1 milestone, though Recon's cutover never waits on it; the block-max posting layout for full-text (E8, Q2) rewrites a structure Recon reads and stays off every Recon path (8).

---

## 0. Assumptions and the budget model

Every figure below comes from one model, kept at `docs/spork/model/budget.py` (run it with `python3`). Workstream P0 replaces each (A) value with a measurement from a 10% rehearsal load plus a 1M-image face sample. After that, re-running the model takes about an hour. Month 0 is 2026-11.

### 0.1 Assumptions

| # | Assumption | Value |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | Records are top-level Record vertices (ads, encounters, profiles, posts) | 290M today, 900M at 3x |
| A2 | The 600 GB upstream figure excludes image binaries | (verify) |
| A3 | Growth to 3x is linear over 24 months | (900M - 290M) / 24 = 25.4M records/month. 200K ads/day organic gives only 73M/yr, so 3x implies new sources or backfills, about 835K records/day on average. Backfills arrive in steps, so triggers use measured footprint, not the calendar. |
| A4 | Hot tier (style vector index; until E17 the only difference from warm, since text stays on node in both tiers) | The newest 18 months of records, capped at 200M records, with a floor of 6 months. Today that is about 110M. The model conservatively treats all growth as hot and ages records out at today's organic rate (6.1M/month), so the tier reaches the 200M cap at about month 4.7. At 3x, 200M is about 8 months at 835K/day. |
| A5 | Raw ad text (title + body + reviews) | 1,200 B per record. 50% of texts are unique by **exact-byte** `textHash` (SHA-256 over title + body with no normalization, round 4: Recon's style vector counts case and punctuation, so normalized dedup would break stylometric parity). P0a measures the real fraction; every `txt` component scales with it, so each +0.1 adds 0.1 / 0.5 x (190.1 + 561.9) = 150 GB (3.7%) to the mid 3x total. |
| A6 | Image references per record, including records with none | low 1.5 / mid 3 / high 6 |
| A7 | Distinct images | 50% of image references (exact SHA-256 dedup) |
| A8 | Templatized faces per distinct image (detections that pass the ROC quality gate) | low 0.25 / mid 0.5 / high 0.8 |
| A9 | Representative ratio: interactive gallery templates per face | 1 representative per 2.5 faces, from blocking-based clustering (6.3). Up to 3 per cluster to cover age gaps. Measured in P0. |
| A10 | ROC template | 448 B (roc-021), opaque, version-locked. Assume 512 B per template in ROC RAM including overhead (Q-ROC3). Tattoo templates assumed 512 B (Q-ROC4). |
| A11 | ROC search cost | 0.23 us per template per core, linear (NIST). ROC search is capped at 16 of an assumed 32 cores per node (`roc-search` cgroup). ROC workers (DETECT, CLUSTER) run in a separate `roc-work` cgroup: 6 cores on n4, 2 on n1 to n3, so they never take search cores (6.3). |
| A12 | Disk | "2 TB" class enterprise NVMe = 1,920 GB. Minus a 60 GB OS partition and about 1.5% XFS + LUKS metadata (28 GB), that leaves 1,832 GB; plan with **1,800 GB usable**. |
| A13 | Network and CPU | At least 10 GbE between nodes, at least 32 physical cores per node |
| A14 | Object store | S3-compatible, outside the 4 nodes (9.2 Q4). Originals at mid 3x are 1.35B distinct images x 150 KB = 203 TB, which cannot live on 4 x 2 TB under any topology. |
| A15 | System 1 index (4c) | PDQ-cluster representatives are 30% of distinct images (S-IMG members); 200 items per IVF posting (1,000 for the 32 B PDQ codes), one global codebook per space shared by every partition's index, so a partition's posting holds 200 / partitions items (50 today, 17 at 3x); a resident centroid costs 36 B (the engine's 32 B location plus 4 B ordinal, `workstreams/vector-scale.md`) plus its int8 code plus a 64 B share of the degree-16 centroid graph: 356 B for the 256-d spaces, 612 B for S-FACE (512-d), 132 B for S-PDQ (round 5; the round-4 constant of 180 B was an inference that held only for 4-bit 256-d codes); SPANN boundary replication adds 30% of the posting (scan) side. Scan codes: 1-bit 256-d for S-IMG and S-TEXT (32 B + 8 B ordinal and RID + 17 B posting overhead = 57 B), PQ-64 for S-FACE (70 B). Rerank codes: 4-bit (128 B) for S-IMG and S-TEXT, int8 (512 B) for S-FACE (4-bit face codes are valve 5). Measured in P0a on the 1M-image sample. |
| A16 | Encoder throughput (4c) | One H100-class card, FP16 batched ONNX: tier A (YuNet + AuraFace + SigLIP 2 or PE-Core + text presence) 3.5 ms per image; PP-OCRv5 recognition 10 ms per text-bearing image, 35% of images; gte + sparse 1.2 ms per text. CPU int8 ONNX: about 2 scene images/s per core; PP-OCRv5 1.75 s per text-bearing image. Measured in P0a. |
| A17 | Version churn of the versioned types (round 7, 2.6) | 0.05 `Identity` vertices per record (about one per 12 shared identifiers) and one `MEMBER_OF` edge per shared identifier (0.61 per record at mid). 0.25 superseded versions per Identity and per MEMBER_OF edge per year, deliberately conservative: organic hard-key merges at 2 to 5% of new records, each moving about 5 memberships, give 0.06 to 0.1 moves per edge per year, with analyst splits, undo and decider-accepted merges on top. P0a measures the merge rate of the rehearsal load. |

### 0.2 Planning ceiling

Two rules bind the plan:
- **Raft free-space guard.** `HA_RAFT_STORAGE_MIN_FREE_SPACE_PERC` defaults to 20, and below that the node forces snapshots and logs warnings. So the steady-state footprint (everything except transient resync headroom) must stay at or below 80% of usable space.
- **Install-time reserve.** During a resync, at least 10% of the volume must stay free.

Ceiling = min(0.8 x usable + resync headroom, 0.9 x usable).

| Volume | Usable | Ceiling | Order trigger (80% of ceiling) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2 TB class | 1,800 GB | min(1,440 + 120, 1,620) = **1,560 GB** | **1,248 GB** |
| 2 TB + one 3.84 TB in the same VG | 1,800 + 3,840 x 0.985 = 5,582, round down to 5,500 GB | min(4,400 + 120, 4,950) = **4,520 GB** | 3,616 GB |
| 2 TB + two 3.84 TB (or one 7.68 TB) | 1,800 + 7,680 x 0.985 = 9,365, round down to 9,300 GB | min(7,440 + 120, 8,370) = **7,560 GB** | 6,048 GB |
| **4 TB (round 10, the plan of record): one 4 TB drive replacing the 2 TB**, planned at the 2 TB class's ratio | 0.9 x 4,000 = 3,600 GB | min(2,880 + 120, 3,240) = **3,000 GB** | 2,400 GB |
| (for comparison) A12's method on a 3.84 TB enterprise drive | (3,840 - 60) x 0.985 = 3,723, round down to 3,700 GB | min(2,960 + 120, 3,330) = 3,080 GB | 2,464 GB |

Round 10: the hardware is fixed except for the 4 TB replacement, confirmed by the owner as a replacement, not an addition (0.8), so the 4,520 and 7,560 GB rows are kept for the record only.

### 0.3 Faces implied by A1 and A6 to A9

Faces per record = A6 x A7 x A8. Low is 1.5 x 0.5 x 0.25 = 0.1875; mid is 3 x 0.5 x 0.5 = 0.75; high is 6 x 0.5 x 0.8 = 2.4.

| Scenario | Faces today | Faces 3x | Representatives today (/2.5) | Representatives 3x | All-face gallery today (x 448 B) | All-face gallery 3x | Representative gallery 3x |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| low | 290M x 0.1875 = 54M | 900M x 0.1875 = 169M | 22M | 68M | 24 GB | 76 GB | 30 GB |
| mid | 290M x 0.75 = 218M | 900M x 0.75 = 675M | 87M | 270M | 97 GB | 302 GB | 121 GB |
| high | 290M x 2.4 = 696M | 900M x 2.4 = 2.16B | 278M | 864M | 312 GB | 968 GB | 387 GB |

Tattoo templates: 2% of distinct images, 512 B each. Mid: 8.7M today (4.5 GB), 27M at 3x (13.8 GB).

### 0.4 Bytes per record (mid images; low and high differ only where noted)

The model is deliberately conservative. It assumes no engine compression work: no zstd dictionaries, no compact FTS postings, no page compression. Structured records are not compressed, because the engine has no page compression. P0a measures every line below.

**`rec_<p>` (every record), 1,595 B per record (low 1,484, high 1,817)**

| Component | B/record | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Record document | 637 | 300 B of properties (coded arrays, Description fields, Shanenet bitmask, keys, normalized stateCode) + 12 B lat/lon (3) + 96 B float32 style-vector copy (`style[24]` verbatim, 5.4; round 3: an INT8 copy would move pairs across Recon's 0.95 cosine threshold), + 12.5% slot reserve = 459. Plus denormalized display fields (2.1, round 2): city 12 + countryCode 2 + areaCodes[] 4 + display identifier arrays (phones, emails, socials, businessNames; 2.4 values x 22 B + 6 B header) 59 + style label 8 = 85, + 12.5% = 96. Plus the ontology contract (2.7, round 4): `_prov` struct {sourceId 2, collectedAt 8, assertedBy 2, confidence 1} = 13 + `markings` bitmask 8 + the (tenant, importedId) index amortized over analyst imports 4 = 25, + 12.5% = 28. Plus full-length upstream image hashes (2.1), 18 B per ref x 3 = 54 (low 27, high 108). |
| Local identifier vertices | 260 | 0.6 local vertices per record x about 430 B: vertex and key, gram, suffix and phonetic lists with their BY ITEM indexes (15 to 25 entries at 12 to 15 B each), handle-only index, Business grams3 |
| Lightweight `HAS_*` edges | 120 | Each Record and local vertex starts its edge list with a 64 B chunk (`GRAPH_EDGE_LIST_INITIAL_CHUNK_SIZE`), about 75 B with header and reserve, for 2.4 refs per record |
| `Record.imageHashes` BY ITEM index | 168 | 56 B x 3 refs, full-length hash (low 84, high 336) |
| Record indexes | 410 | record_id 45 + upstreamRid 30 + tsPosted 30 + tsCollected 30 + (website, tsPosted) 40 + textHash 35 + (placeId, tsPosted) 40 + (age, tsPosted) 40 + (views, tsPosted) 40 + filter composites 80 |

At 1,595 B a 120 GB `rec_<p>` holds about 75M records: about 4 periods today (3.9), 12 at 3x (12.0).

**Page sizes (schema v1, fixed at creation; round 6 reconciled with `02-retrieval-speed.md` C8 and C9).** ArcadeDB always reads a whole page (`PaginatedComponentFile` reads `getPhysicalSize()` bytes), and the stock defaults are 256 KB per LSM index page (`INDEX_DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE`, `LSMTreeIndexAbstract.DEF_PAGE_SIZE`), 64 KB per bucket page and 256 KB per external-property bucket page. Two engine facts set the SPORK sizes. First, a v0 bucket page spends 8,202 B on its header and a fixed 2,048-entry slot table whatever its size (`LocalBucket.contentHeaderSize`), so a 609 B Record row packs 13 per 16 KB page (49.9% record space, 97 GB of Record pages per 77M-record partition), 40 per 32 KB page (75%, 63 GB) and 94 per 64 KB page (87.5%, 54 GB); the slot count is on-disk format, so a 256-slot table is a format version (M20 in `02-retrieval-speed.md` 4.3), after which 16 KB pages hold 25 rows at 93.7% (50 GB). Second, every exact-key index is a HASH index (next paragraph), whose page size defaults to 4 KB for fixed-width keys (LONG, UUID: `record_id`, `upstreamRid`, `recdir` `upstreamRid`, `placeId`) and 16 KB for variable-width keys (STRING, BINARY: identifier `key`, `textHash`, `imageHashes BY ITEM`), the sizes the engine's own benchmark picked (`HashIndexBucket.DEF_PAGE_SIZE`, `DEF_VARIABLE_KEY_PAGE_SIZE`, issue #5712; maximum 64 KB). Schema v1 therefore uses: Record, TextBody, Target and AuditEntry buckets at 32 KB (from about 300 cold rows the bandwidth term of a hydrate beats the latency term, 64 KB would double it, and 16 KB v0 would cost +34 GB of Record pages per partition against 32 KB); `recdir` and identifier-vertex buckets at 16 KB (one tiny row per cold read, so bytes matter more than density); HASH point-lookup indexes at their engine defaults; LSM range composites, FULL_TEXT and GEOSPATIAL at 64 KB (`arcadedb.indexDefaultPageSize=65536` covers the plain LSM composites; FULL_TEXT and GEOSPATIAL ignore that setting and take `IndexBuilder.withPageSize(65536)`); TextBody's EXTERNAL `text` pages at 16 KB. Bucket page sizes need no Java: `CREATE [VERTEX|DOCUMENT] TYPE ... PAGESIZE n` reaches `TypeBuilder.withPageSize` (`CreateVertexTypeStatement.java:41-53`, `CreateDocumentTypeStatement.java`); `CREATE INDEX` has no PAGESIZE clause (`CreateIndexStatement.java:404`), so only the FULL_TEXT and GEOSPATIAL indexes go through the Java builder in the plugin's schema installer and the n4 build, and the HASH indexes are plain `UNIQUE_HASH` / `NOTUNIQUE_HASH` SQL. TextBody's paired external-property bucket does **not** take its page size from `TypeBuilder.withPageSize` (round 4: `LocalDocumentType` creates the `_ext` bucket with the database setting `EXTERNAL_PROPERTY_BUCKET_DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE`, and `LocalSchema.checkPairedExternalBucketPageSize` reads the same setting), so the installer and the n4 build set `arcadedb.externalPropertyBucketDefaultPageSize=16384` on each `txt_<p>` before the EXTERNAL `text` property is created. Existing files keep their page size, so changing this later means a rebuild; M20's 16 KB Record pages apply to partitions created after it lands. The byte lines above carry a 12.5% slot reserve and no page-level waste: the 25% header share of a 32 KB v0 page is about +180 B per record of disk over the 637 B Record line (+14 GB per partition) until M20, which P0a measures (`model/budget.py` prints the density table) and the lines absorb or not.

**Point lookups are HASH (round 6; replaces the round-4 series walk).** Round 4 budgeted LSM_TREE point-lookup indexes at 16 KB and found that a compacted LSM index is a chain of series of at most 1,024 data pages (`DEFAULT_MAX_DATA_PAGES_PER_SERIES`) and a point lookup visits every series newest to oldest (last data page plus one 8 KB bloom page per series) before the leaf: 1 + 2S + 2 cached touches, S = 519 for the `recdir` `upstreamRid` index today and 1,609 at 3x at 16 KB (130 and 402 at 64 KB), a resident bloom and series-root set of 1.2 GB today and 3.8 GB at 3x that had to live in the page cache, about 0.3 us of CPU per series (0.16 to 0.48 ms per probe), and a weekly full compaction of every point-lookup index through the Raft log (27 GB for `recdir` at 3x). Reconciliation with `02-retrieval-speed.md` C8 makes every exact-key index a HASH index (`UNIQUE_HASH` or `NOTUNIQUE_HASH`): the `identity` keys, `recdir` `upstreamRid`, each partition's `record_id`, `upstreamRid`, `textHash` and `imageHashes BY ITEM`, the local identifier `key`, `Place.placeId` and `TextBody.textHash`. A HASH lookup is page 0 (global depth, directory start), one directory page and one bucket page: 3 cached touches and 1 cold read, 2 with an overflow page, against 1 + 2S + 2 for LSM; no series, no bloom pages, no mutable tail, and `HashIndex.compact()` and `scheduleCompaction()` return false, so a HASH index never ships a compaction through Raft. Verified limits (`engine/src/main/java/com/arcadedb/index/hash/`): keys may be any scalar type including LONG, STRING, BINARY, UUID and RID (`HashIndexBucket.isSupportedKeyType`); unique and non-unique (format version 3 keeps the RIDs of a non-unique key in RID-list pages); `BY ITEM` is validated by the property type, not the index type (`TypeIndexBuilder`); no ordered iteration (`HashIndex.supportsOrderedIterations()` is false), so range scans stay on the LSM composites; an entry must fit one page (`entryTooLarge`: the longest SPORK key, `sh:<platform>:<handle>`, is under 100 B); growth is a bucket split (two pages rewritten plus directory entries) or a directory doubling (a copy of 2^d x 4 B, at most 32 MB for `recdir` at 3x), both ordinary page writes that replicate through the WAL like bucket pages (`HashIndexBucket extends PaginatedComponent`; ha-raft has no HASH-specific path), and the directory depth is bounded at 30. Resident set: the directory is 4 B per bucket page; at about 65% page load a 4 KB page holds about 125 LONG entries (8 B key + 10 B RID + 3 B slot) and a 16 KB page about 250 STRING keys, so `recdir` at 3x (870M keys, 7.2M pages) keeps a 2^23 x 4 B = 34 MB directory and every point-lookup directory together stays under 0.25 GB; with the LSM composites' page 0, tails and roots the pinned set is 0.44 GB today and 1.3 GB at 3x (`02-retrieval-speed.md` 3.1), against the 3.8 GB bloom set, so the resident-set reason for keeping hydrates off n4 (D52) is gone. Index bytes are about the same as the LSM lines above (a LONG entry at 21 B / 0.65 = 32 B against 30; a STRING key 57 against 50). Cold hydrate bytes with the reconciled sizes: an upstream RID costs `recdir` HASH 4 KB + `recdir` document 16 KB + partition `upstreamRid` HASH 4 KB + Record 32 KB = 56 KB, a `record_id` 4 + 32 = 36 KB; a 2,000-id hydrate moves 112 MB (72 MB by `record_id`; 80 and 40 MB after M20), against 131 MB in the round-4 16 KB LSM plan, 160 MB at a uniform 16 KB HASH page and 1.3 GB on stock pages. P0a verifies HASH `BY ITEM`, overflow-chain length under the duplicate-heavy `imageHashes` and `textHash` keys, and behaviour after a follower snapshot install (`02-retrieval-speed.md` risk 3); the fallback for a key HASH cannot serve is LSM_TREE at 256 KB pages (2 series after the seal compaction, 7 touches, a 256 KB cold leaf), never 16 KB.

**`txt_<p>` hot tier, 951 B per hot record**

| Component | B/record | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Title (inline) | 30 | about 60 B x 0.5. Search rows read it without touching the external page. |
| Text (EXTERNAL, LZ4, 16 KB external pages) | 273 | 1,200 x 0.5 unique / 2.2. `text = title + " " + body` is the only long property; the body is derived from it (title length), so nothing is stored twice. |
| FULL_TEXT (stock LSM postings) on one stored property | 480 | 0.8 x 600 B unique raw text. The index covers the single stored property `text`. A two-property index posts every token twice (field-prefixed with per-field tf, then unprefixed with global tf, `LSMTreeFullTextIndex.putWithStats`), which would cost 960 B. Field-qualified search is out of scope. |
| Style vector | 148 | per unique text: 96 B float property + 96 B vector stored inline in the graph file (`storeVectorsInGraph=true`, 1.4) + 24 B INT8 code + 64 B graph (degree 16) + 16 B location = 296, x 0.5 |
| textHash unique index | 20 | 40 x 0.5 |

**`txt_<p>` warm tier, 803 B per warm record**

| Component | B/record | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Title (inline) | 30 | about 60 B x 0.5 |
| Text (EXTERNAL, LZ4), kept on node | 273 | Round 3: the FULL_TEXT index is on the stored `text`, and engine index maintenance derives the keys to remove from the record's stored property values (`DocumentIndexer.deleteDocument` reads `index.getPropertyNames()`). There is no computed-key type index, and `ManualIndexBuilder` accepts only LSM_TREE and HASH, so dropping `text` would leave a deleted ad's postings in the index forever. The text leaves the node only with E17 (valve 4, 1.3). |
| FULL_TEXT, unpruned | 480 | Pruning terms present in more than 5% of the partition's texts would save 0.4 x 480 = 192 B. It is a valve (1.3), not counted, until P0 measures it. |
| textHash index | 20 | The raw style property is dropped: exact rescoring uses the float32 `styleVec` on Record. |

The engine never shrinks a bucket file (`LocalBucket` only defragments within a page), so a partition is not aged in place. When a `txt_<p>` leaves the hot tier it is rewritten offline on n4 into a new database without the vector index (and, after E17, without text), and admitted like a backfill under the aging protocol (3.5), which replays deletions made during the rewrite and switches the partition map to the new name. That costs one headroom slot and one partition transfer per node.

**`identity`, 263 B per record plus fixed parts plus the version history (round 7: 0 today, 42.9 GB at mid 3x)**

| Component | B/record | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Shared identifiers (vertex 150 incl. dayCells + key index 50 + MEMBER_OF 70) | 165 | 270 B x 0.61 shared per record. Shared = 0.235 non-image + 0.25 x 1.5 distinct images; low 0.42, high 0.985. Round 7: the MEMBER_OF edge loses `validFrom` and `validTo` (18 B) and gains the engine-owned `_txFrom` stamp (9 B); the 70 B line is kept and the 9 B stays as reserve. |
| CO_OCCURS aggregated edges | 42.5 | 0.5 per record x 85 B |
| Identity vertices + ResolutionDecisions | 20 | The Identity `_txFrom` (0.05 x 9 B) is inside this line. |
| FaceCluster vertices | 36 | 0.3 clusters per record x 120 B |
| Version history of `Identity` and `MEMBER_OF` (`_VersionHistory`, round 7) | 36 per record **per year** | Paid only by versioned types, and only on an update or delete: one full copy of the superseded record image plus `{rid, txFrom, txTo, out, in}` and one `rid` index entry (edges also an `out` and an `in` entry), nothing on create and nothing on a read. Identity entry: 113 B image + 37 B fields and header = 150 x 1.125 slot reserve = 171 + 30 B index entry = about 200 B. MEMBER_OF entry: 61 B image + 57 B fields and header = 118 x 1.125 = 132 + 3 x 30 B index entries = about 220 B. Per record per year at A17: Identity 0.05 x 0.25 x 200 = 2.5 B + MEMBER_OF 0.61 x 0.25 x 220 = 33.5 B = 36.0 B (low 26 at 0.42 shared, high 57 at 0.985). It accumulates from month 0 over every record then present: 10.5 GB per year at 290M records, 32.4 GB per year at 900M; 16.0 GB at month 12, 29.8 GB at month 18.8, **42.9 GB at month 24 (mid 3x)**, 0 today. For comparison the round-6 design kept every ended `MEMBER_OF` edge in the live edge lists at 0.61 x 0.25 x 70 B = 10.7 B per record per year, 13 GB at 3x, on the hot adjacency path of every traversal; the history store costs 3.4x the bytes and sits on no read path. The deletion saga (5.5 step 4) calls `history.purge` on the memberships and Identities of a deleted subject. If P0a measures churn above A17, slice E25d (delta images, 3.6) halves the image share of the line. |
| Fixed | | Place 8M x 300 B plus a UNIQUE `placeId` index = 2.6 GB. Case layer (Target with identifierKeys, Case, Evidence, Watch, Alert, Mission, learning state) 10 GB today, 40 GB at 3x. |

**`recdir`, 70 B per record.** One document `{upstreamRid LONG, period SHORT}` (about 40 B with header and reserve) plus a UNIQUE `upstreamRid` index (30 B). `record_id` needs no directory because SPORK assigns it with the period in its high bits (2.5).

**`media_<p>`, 480 B per record (plus 216 B while the partition is inside the hot window, round 6)**

| Component | B/record | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Image vertex + sha index + upstream-hash index | 255 | 1.5 distinct x 170 B: 32 B binary sha in record and index, phash, mime, w, h, firstSeen, counts (130) plus the (upstreamHash to sha) index (40) |
| Face vertex + key index, no template | 225 | 0.75 faces x 300 B: key, templateSha, clusterKey, bbox, quality, yaw, ageEstimate, minorFlag, segment, slot, representative, state (about 210), key index 60, envelope-encrypted slot pointer 28 (5.6) |
| PDQ Hamming band keys, hot window only | 216 while hot, 0 after | Round 6 (`06-stylometry-perceptual-hash.md` 3.1, open question 4): `Image.pdqBands` LIST OF LONG with a NOTUNIQUE `BY ITEM` LSM index, 8 bands of 32 bits (radius 15, re-encodes and resizes), about 18 B per band per distinct image x 1.5 = 216 B; kept only on the `media_<p>` partitions inside the hot window (165M distinct images today = 24 GB, 300M at the 200M cap = 43 GB) and on case databases, dropped when a partition leaves the window. Corpus-wide it would be 1.35B x 144 B = 194 GB at 3x next to S-PDQ's 54 GB and pHash with 2 bands 49 GB: neither is stored; the radius-31 corpus-wide near-dup is the S-PDQ IVF space (4c) and PDQ supersedes pHash. |

**`ops` and `audit`.** `ops` is 20 GB today, rising to 60 GB at 3x. It is bounded explicitly: (identifierKey, dayBucket) counters are kept for 120 days only (90-day baseline plus margin; about 2M rows/day x 120 days x 85 B = 20 GB at 3x pace), MediaJob rows are deleted on completion (peak about 10 GB during the face backfill), plus IngestCheckpoint, the DeletionOrder ledger, the gallery manifest, Presence (about 1 GB) and leases. `audit` is 1 GB today (the imported Recon chain) plus about 20 GB/yr.

**ROC gallery segments (on node, all faces), 336 B per record.** 0.75 faces x 448 B. Tattoo segments add 1.5 x 0.02 x 512 = 15 B per record.

**System 1 index (4c), 453 B per record at mid (low 232, high 1,023) plus 2 GB fixed; S-PDQ and S-TEXT from Q1, the other spaces from Q2 (rounds 8 and 9).** Postings and rerank codes live in `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` indexes on `Image`, `Face` and `TextBody`, so they sit inside `media_<p>` and `txt_<p>` and replicate through Raft like any LSM page. Mid, per record: S-IMG 0.3 x 1.5 distinct images x (57 posting + 128 rerank) = 83; S-PDQ 1.5 x (32 B hash + 8 B RID) = 60; S-FACE 0.3 representatives x (70 PQ-64 posting + 512 int8 rerank) = 175; S-TEXT 0.5 unique x (56 + 128) = 92; S-TATTOO 0.03 x 74 = 2; SPANN boundary replication 0.3 x (26 + 60 + 21 + 28 + 2) = 41. At 3x: 900M x 453 B = 408 GB + 2 GB S-DOC = 410 GB, by space S-IMG 75 (23 + 52), S-PDQ 54, S-FACE 157 (19 + 138), S-TEXT 83 (25 + 58), S-TATTOO 2, boundary 37, S-DOC 2. Today 133 GB. Valve 5 (4-bit face rerank, 256 B) saves 0.3 x 256 = 77 B per record, 69 GB at 3x. Heap is centroids only, costed per space (A15, round 5): S-IMG 2.02M x 356 B = 0.72 GB, S-TEXT 2.25M x 356 = 0.80, S-TATTOO 0.14M x 356 = 0.05, S-FACE 1.35M x 612 = 0.83, S-PDQ 1.35M x 132 = 0.18; 7.1M centroids and 2.57 GB at mid 3x (0.83 GB today, 5.34 GB at high 3x), about 1 B per indexed item (1.4). The per-partition centroid-to-posting directories (8 B per centroid, 57 MB per partition, 0.7 GB over 12 partitions at 3x) live in index pages and are read through the page cache, not held on heap. S-STYLE is the existing style index and is counted in `txt` hot; moving it onto the IVF index (Q2, round 9) adds 0.5M centroids x 124 B = 0.06 GB of heap and frees its 5.9 GB of jvector residency. Because the codes sit inside `media_<p>` and `txt_<p>`, every archive of those databases carries them: about 26 GB per sealed `media_<p>` and 7.5 GB per sealed `txt_<p>` at mid (backups below).

**Fixed per node, 190 GB.**
- OS-side binaries, logs, ROC SDK, ROC models and the encoder sidecar's int8 ONNX models (about 1 GB): 40
- Raft log + WAL: 120 = 2 x (25 + 27 + 8). Each LSM insert lands on the index tail page and shifts its pointer array, so the logged page delta is tens of KB per touched index (a HASH insert dirties one 4 or 16 KB bucket page): 15 to 25 GB/day of ingest deltas at 3x pace, not 8; ingest batches of 500 records or 30 s amortize tail-page deltas. Round 4 adds compaction: `LSMTreeIndexCompactor` runs only on the leader (`RaftReplicatedDatabase.runWithCompactionReplication` returns false on followers) and ships every compacted page as chunked SCHEMA_ENTRY WAL, so under the SPORK compaction policy below the line also holds one scheduled full compaction a day plus about 8 GB of incremental compactions. Round 6: the point-lookup indexes are HASH and never compact, so the largest scheduled rewrite is the current `txt_<p>` FULL_TEXT at 18.5 GB; the 27 GB term (the round-4 `recdir` LSM index) is kept as the reserve, so the line stays 120 GB. The line is 2 x the 24 h volume, measured in P0a with the policy applied.
- Vector and IVF build scratch: 10 (an IVF build is a streaming k-means on a sample plus one assignment pass over one period's codes, at most 77M x 60 B = 4.6 GB for S-PDQ)
- Ingest staging: 20

**LSM compaction policy (round 4 blocker).** With the defaults, `LSMTreeIndexCompactor.isFullCompactionDue` returns true once an index has 10 or more compacted series (`INDEX_COMPACTION_FULL_SERIES`), `compactFull` writes series of at most 1,024 data pages, so any index over 10,240 pages (160 MB at 16 KB, 2.6 GB at 256 KB) comes out of a full compaction with 10 or more series and is rewritten in full on every following round, and rounds are scheduled every 10 mutable pages (`INDEX_COMPACTION_MIN_PAGES_SCHEDULE`). The RAM fallback does not help: (series + 10) x page size is about 26 MB, under `INDEX_COMPACTION_RAM_MB` (300). At 200K records/day an LSM index the size of round 4's `recdir` index (8.7 GB, 30 B entries) fills 200K x 30 B / 16 KB = 366 mutable pages a day, 37 rounds, 37 x 8.7 = 319 GB/day through the Raft log; the current `txt_<p>` FULL_TEXT (13 GB today) adds 18 rewrites, 121 GB/day; at 3x pace a 27 GB index would be 153 rewrites = 4.1 TB/day. Every growing SPORK LSM index (the current partition's 27 composites and partial-search lists, the current FULL_TEXT, the `identity` composites and audit chains, the IVF postings) is over the threshold; the point-lookup indexes are HASH since round 6 and never compact. So, per SPORK database, E0 sets `arcadedb.indexCompactionFullSeriesThreshold=0` and `arcadedb.indexCompactionFullDeadEntriesRatio=0` (no automatic full compaction) and `arcadedb.indexCompactionMinPagesSchedule=64` on `identity` and the current period (fewer, larger incremental compactions, hence fewer series between full compactions, since each incremental round appends one series; sealed partitions have no mutable pages after their seal compaction, so the value is moot there and they keep the default 10; `recdir` has no LSM index). Full compactions are then plugin jobs: one index at a time, off peak, under the `transient-slot` lease (the old compacted file stays until its readers finish, at most 27 GB), weekly for the current FULL_TEXT (18.5 GB) and the `identity` LSM indexes (Target, Watch and Mission composites, the AuditEntry and AuditAccess chains, Evidence FULL_TEXT, the df table: about 3 GB at 3x), and once at seal and after every admission for every LSM index of the sealed or admitted partition (27 composites x 77M x 40 B = 83 GB per seal, one 3 GB index at a time, about once per 3.2 months at 3x pace, 6 GB/week averaged): about 28 GB/week, 4 GB/day on average at mid 3x, never more than one 18.5 GB rewrite in a day (round 6; round 4 had 80 GB/week with LSM point-lookup indexes, `recdir` at 27 GB the largest). **What the policy costs reads (round 6):** a live composite on the current partition gains one series per incremental round, 835K records x 40 B = 33 MB a day = 8 rounds of 64 pages at 3x pace, so it carries up to 56 series at the end of a week (13 at today's pace) plus up to 64 mutable pages: a range-cursor open there costs about 2 cached touches per series plus one binary search per mutable page, about 175 cached touches or 50 to 100 us against 30 us today, 200 cursor opens in P6 up to 20 ms on the live partition only (sealed partitions sit at M = 0 and about 23 series), which M2 (series directory and mutable-page fences) and M6 (cursor views) of `02-retrieval-speed.md` 4.3 remove and P0a measures; the current period's composites may be full-compacted twice a week (+27 GB/week of Raft) if P0a shows the week-end cursor open over budget. No point lookup pays any of it, since point lookups are HASH. Round 6 checked `02-retrieval-speed.md` revision 2's C5 variant (schedule 4, `fullSeriesThreshold=3` on `identity`) against this: schedule 4 makes 127 rounds a day and 892 series a week on a live composite for a saving of 60 cached binary searches per cursor open, and raises the incremental Raft traffic from about 8 to 14 GB/day (each round also rewrites the metadata, tail and root pages, about 192 KB per round against 256 KB of merged entries); `fullSeriesThreshold=3` on `identity` is a no-op for the tiny indexes that never reach 3 series and an unbounded automatic rewrite for the growing ones (the AuditEntry composites, 2.7 GB a year of entries, would be rewritten in full every 3 rounds = every 1.7 days, about 1.5 GB/day and rising), so both are rejected and C5 now carries this policy; C5's seal-time full compaction of every index of the sealed partition is adopted. Scheduled full compactions are suspended while any peer is dead (3.2 rule 6). Follower write load is the same bytes (SSD wear is budgeted at the ingest deltas plus 4 GB/day). P0a measures Raft bytes per compaction, series count per live composite and cursor-open time against it, P0c measures follower apply time for a 27 GB compaction (the reserve size; the largest scheduled one is 18.5 GB).
- Backups: 0. Before W3d, `AutoBackupConfig` defaults (run on `$leader`, `./backups` under the server root, every 60 minutes, 10 archives per database) would put full `.zip` archives on the leader's SPORK volume: one generation is about 491 GB at mid today at 2:1 and does not fit. WS1 therefore sets the backup directory to an off-node NFS or object-store gateway mount (outside `databases/`, so the `ATOMIC_MOVE` rule does not apply). **Mount sizing (round 4):** 7 daily archives of every database would need 7 x a generation = 3.4 TB today and 10.7 TB at mid 3x (3.9 and 12.2 TB once the S1 codes are inside the archives), not the 487 GB round 3 asked for. Instead, only `identity`, `ops`, `audit`, `recdir` and the current period group stay on the daily schedule (frequency 1,440 minutes, staggered off-peak, `maxFiles` 7 on the mount, no weekly copies before W3d): a daily set of (88.9 + 20 + 1 + 20.3 + 120 + 73 + 37) / 2 = 180 GB today, 337 GB at 3x. Sealed period databases are removed from the auto-backup schedule and archived by the plugin once at seal, again after each DeletionOrder batch that touched them, and again when the S1 code backfill (4c.4) or an IVF codebook version change completes on them (round 5: the third trigger, under the `transient-slot` lease; without it a sealed `txt_<p>` archived before its S1 backfill would be restored for its aging rewrite without its S-TEXT codes and a sealed `media_<p>` restored from its seal archive would lose about 26 GB of codes with no re-encode step); one copy plus one being rewritten, at most 60 GB: 310 GB today, 1,195 GB at 3x before the S1 codes. **With the S1 codes (round 5)** the current period group adds 75M x 453 B / 2 = 17 GB to each daily set and the sealed partitions add their codes at 2:1 (49 GB today, 187 GB at 3x). Mount = 8 x 197 + 371 + 60 = **2.0 TB today**, 8 x 375 + 1,403 + 60 = **4.5 TB at mid 3x** (9.2 Q13; round 7: the daily `identity` archive carries its version history, 161 GB at 2:1 at mid 3x instead of 140); 1.8 and 4.1 TB before the index exists (Q1, Q2). Stock auto-backup archives are plaintext (`BackupTask` never sets the integration `Backup` encryption key), so the mount must be an encrypted volume with KMS-held keys (5.6). After W3d, archives stream to the object store; if the SDK path must stage locally, staging is one archive at a time on the leader, the largest being `identity` (44 GB today, 161 GB at mid 3x including its version history, round 7).

**Transient slots (not in the steady state).** The leader's backfill admission (+180 GB, 3.5), n4's offline build (+180 GB: the restored source copy, the output and its archive, 162 GB for a `txt` aging rewrite at the period boundary, 3.5), the leader's backup staging (at most 161 GB) and a scheduled full index compaction (at most 27 GB) each fit the gap between the 1,440 GB steady ceiling and the 1,620 GB install reserve. They take one `ops` lease (`transient-slot`), so no two run together on any node, and none runs while a node is resyncing.

**Resync headroom, 120 GB.** It equals the database size cap. Databases larger than the cap (`identity` from about month 3.5 at mid) use cold install, E1 in 3.6, so headroom never exceeds the cap. Before E1 lands, headroom is max(120, size of `identity`).

### 0.5 Per-node footprint (GB)

Total = rec + txt hot + txt warm + identity (round 7: including the version history of `Identity` and `MEMBER_OF`, 0 today and 42.9 GB at mid 3x) + recdir + media (round 6: including the PDQ band index on the hot-window partitions, 23.8 GB today and 43.2 GB at 3x at mid) + ops + audit + gallery (faces + tattoo) + S1 index + fixed 190 + headroom 120. "Today" includes the face backfill; the S1 column is what the index would weigh at that size (rounds 8 and 9: its first spaces are built in Q1 and the rest in Q2, every backfill after H1), so the "no S1" total is the one the 2 TB ceiling is measured against.

| low today | 430.4 | 104.6 | 144.5 | 66.4 | 20.3 | 65.2 | 21.0 | 26.6 | 69.1 | **1,258.0** | 1,188.9 | 76% | 28% | 17% |
| mid today | 462.6 | 104.6 | 144.5 | 88.9 | 20.3 | 163.0 | 21.0 | 101.9 | 133.4 | **1,550.1** | 1,416.7 | 91% | 34% | 21% |
| high today | 526.9 | 104.6 | 144.5 | 141.3 | 20.3 | 404.2 | 21.0 | 320.7 | 298.7 | **2,292.1** | 1,993.5 | 128% | 51% | 30% |
| low 3x | 1,335.6 | 190.1 | 561.9 | 240.2 | 63.0 | 187.0 | 101.0 | 82.5 | 210.4 | **3,281.8** | 3,071.4 | 197% | 73% | 43% |
| mid 3x | 1,435.5 | 190.1 | 561.9 | 322.4 | 63.0 | 475.2 | 101.0 | 316.2 | 409.8 | **4,185.2** | 3,775.4 | 242% | 93% | 55% |
| high 3x | 1,635.3 | 190.1 | 561.9 | 509.3 | 63.0 | 1,193.4 | 101.0 | 995.3 | 922.6 | **6,482.1** | 5,559.4 | 356% | 143% | 86% |

Spot checks of the arithmetic:
- **rec, mid 3x:** 900M x 1,595 B = 1,435.5 GB.
- **txt hot, 3x:** 200M x 950.7 B = 190.1 GB.
- **txt warm, 3x:** (900M - 200M) x 802.7 B = 561.9 GB.
- **identity, mid 3x:** 900M x 263.2 B = 236.9 GB, plus 2.6 + 40 = 279.5 GB, plus the version history (round 7): record-months over 24 months = 290M x 24 + 25.4M x 24 x 24 / 2 = 14.3 billion, / 12 x 36.0 B = 42.9 GB, so 322.4 GB. The 3x rows are the only ones that move in round 7: history is 0 at month 0.
- **media, mid 3x:** 900M x 480 B = 432 GB, plus the hot-window PDQ band index 200M x 1.5 x 144 B = 43.2 GB (round 6) = 475.2 GB.
- **gallery, mid 3x:** 675M x 448 B = 302.4 GB, plus 27M tattoos x 512 B = 13.8 GB.
- **S1 index, mid 3x:** 900M x 453 B = 407.8 GB + 2 GB S-DOC = 409.8 GB.
- **mid today total without S1:** 1,004.8 (databases, of which 23.8 the hot-window PDQ band index) + 101.9 (gallery) + 190 (fixed) + 120 (headroom) = 1,416.7 GB; with S1, + 133.4 = 1,550.1 GB.
- **face-free mid today:** 1,416.7 - 101.9 (gallery) - 75.7 (Face rows 290M x 0.75 x 300 B = 65.3 plus FaceCluster 290M x 0.3 x 120 B = 10.4) = 1,239 GB.

For comparison, the database part alone (excluding gallery, S1, fixed and headroom) is 1,005 GB at mid today (929 GB before faces) against the upstream 600 GB, and 3,149 GB at 3x against the upstream 1.8 TB. SPORK stores more than upstream because of the per-partition gram and suffix lists, the extra composites, the full-text index (whose stored `text` stays on node), the provenance and markings, and the face metadata. Dedup and tiering keep it from growing faster than that.

### 0.6 Disk trigger and dates

At mid, the footprint grows (3,775.4 - 1,416.7) / 24 = 98.3 GB per month on average without the S1 index and (4,185.2 - 1,550.1) / 24 = 109.8 GB per month with it, faster in the first 5 months while the hot tier fills to its cap (round 7: the version history adds about 1.8 GB per month on average, 1.3 in the first year and 2.2 in the second, because it grows with the record count).

**The 2 TB ceiling (H1) binds twice** (round 4): the face gallery and face metadata do not exist until the W8 backfill in Q2, and the pressure valves cannot run before Q2 either (valve 2 needs the gallery, valves 1 and 3 need the WS7 aging rewrite, valve 1 also a P0 recall measurement). So the install date is the earlier of (a) the start of the face backfill, which must not begin on any node without H1, and (b) the face-free crossing of 1,560 GB.

| Scenario | Order disks at 1,248 GB | Face-free today | Face-free crosses 1,560 GB | With faces, crosses 1,560 GB |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| low | face-free month 1.4 (with faces 0.8): about 2026-12 | 1,143 GB | month 5.4 (about 2027-04) | month 4.6 |
| mid | already crossed with or without faces (1,417 / 1,239 GB today): **order now** | 1,239 GB | **month 3.7 (about 2027-02)** | month 1.4 |
| high | now | 1,431 GB | month 1.3 (about 2026-12) | now (1,994 GB) |

So H1 is installed in Q1, and the face backfill waits for it on every node. The round-3 claim that the valves stretch the crossing is withdrawn. Rounds 8 and 9: the S1 first-spaces backfill waits for H1 too; without H1 the face-free footprint plus S-PDQ and S-TEXT (178.4 B per record at mid) would be 1,558 GB at the Q1 exit and cross 1,560 GB at month 3.0 at mid (0.5 high, 4.7 low; `budget.py`), so for the index to ship with Recon's Q1 move H1 is on every node by about 2027-01-18. The table's mid and low figures are the model's (round 7's text kept 3.8, 5.5 and 1.5 from round 6).

**Superseded by 0.8 (round 10: no 3.84 TB addition and no second drive; the drives are replaced by 4 TB ones, a 3,000 GB ceiling).** **After one 3.84 TB per node (4,520 GB ceiling, 3,616 GB order trigger)** the S1 index (from Q1, round 8) is part of the footprint; the model counts every space at full size from month 0, so the dates below do not depend on the quarter each space is built in. With it, mid crosses 3,616 GB at month 18.8 (about 2028-05; round 7: 19.1 before the version history, which is 29.8 GB by then) and reaches 4,185 GB (93%) at 3x; the 4,520 GB ceiling itself falls at month 27.1, past the horizon. Without the S1 index the trigger would have been month 22.4. Low crosses 3,616 GB at month 28.0, past the horizon (73% at 3x). High crosses 3,616 GB at month 7.5 and 4,520 GB at month 12.7, which is why high buys two drives now (7,560 GB ceiling, 86% at 3x). **New expansion trigger:** at mid, order the second 3.84 TB drive per node when the measured footprint reaches 3,616 GB (projected 2028-05) and install it before 4,520 GB; alternatively buy the two drives together in Q1, which the owner may prefer for one maintenance window (9.2 Q2). Valve 5 (4-bit face rerank codes, 69 GB at 3x) moves the mid trigger by about 69 / 110 = 0.6 months and is not a substitute.

- **What to buy:** one 3.84 TB NVMe per node at low and mid (a second at month 18.8 at mid), two at high (H1). Disk layering is **LVM on LUKS** (round 4): each drive is its own LUKS container and physical volume, unlocked at boot from a KMS-held key through the initramfs (clevis/tang or a TPM-sealed keyfile), so the online sequence is `cryptsetup luksFormat` the new drive, `cryptsetup open`, `pvcreate` on the mapped device, `vgextend`, `lvextend`, `xfs_growfs` on the single SPORK filesystem. Nothing moves between mount points, so this is not a re-architecture (see 3.2 for why separate mounts are forbidden). The cost of a linear LV across two drives is that either drive's failure is a full-node loss with a 4 to 6.5 hour rebuild (3.3); mirrored PVs would halve usable space and are not affordable at these sizes, so the exposure is accepted and listed in R9.
- **Rollout:** one node at a time. `HA_LOG_PURGE_GAP` is only the minimum distance between purges, not a retention window: with a snapshot every 5 minutes and `HA_LOG_PURGE_UPTO_SNAPSHOT=true`, retention drops to about 5 minutes after each purge. `HA_LOG_PURGE_UPTO_SNAPSHOT=false` is set **permanently from day 1** (E0), not toggled per window: the setting is read only when Ratis properties and the `StateMachineUpdater` are built, so a toggle would need two rolling restarts of every voter, each under 5-minute retention. With it false the leader keeps the log back to the slowest follower, so a node down for the disk install catches up from the log (3.2 rule 6 has the dead-peer rule). From Q2, E0.5 (3.6) sets Ratis `purgePreservationLogNum` to about 24 h of entries and the setting returns to true as the end state. Scheduled full compactions are suspended during the install window so the log grows at the ingest rate only (25 to 33 GB/day).
- **Pressure valves** (1.3) are available from Q2 and buy about 1.5 to 3.5 months against the 4,520 GB trigger (177 GB at month 6, 392 GB at month 24, valve 5 included). They are a bridge for procurement, not a substitute.

### 0.7 RAM model (126 GB per node)

- **ROC slice:** a 45 GB cap: 4 GB process overhead, 1 GB for the case/watch gallery (up to 2M templates, on every node), 5 GB for this node's tattoo quarter after failover (27M x 512 B / 3 at mid 3x, rounded up), and 35 GB of face templates. That is 35 GB / 512 B = 68.4M face templates per node.
- **Steady state:** each node holds at most 75% of that, so the 3 survivors can absorb a lost node's quarter. Failover-safe cluster capacity = 3 x 68.4M = **205M interactive face templates**, about 51.3M per node in steady state.
- **When representatives exceed 205M:**
  - mid: about month 15.5 (R = 684M, 0.75 x 684M / 2.5 = 205M), about 2028-02
  - high: today (278M)
  - low: never (68M at 3x)
- **H2 (256 GB RAM per node):** raises the ROC cap to 150 GB per node. Face templates = (150 - 10) GB / 512 B = 273M per node, and failover-safe capacity = 3 x 273M = 820M. That covers mid 3x representatives (270M) and even mid 3x all faces (675M).

### 0.8 Fixed hardware: 4 nodes, 126 GB RAM, no GPU, 4 TB drives (round 10)

The owner directed on 2026-11-03: "we need to do as much as we can with the hardware we already have. Expect hardware to not change", then "Let's plan on expanding the hard drives to 4 TB", and confirmed that each node's 2 TB drive is replaced by a 4 TB one, with nothing added on top. So for the whole horizon each node has 126 GB of RAM, its existing CPUs, no GPU and one 4 TB drive. That voids H1 as a 3.84 TB addition (replaced by the 4 TB replacement), H2 (256 GB RAM), the second-drive order, a GPU per node, the GPU burst host and the inference box for the self-hosted decider. Whether an S3-compatible object store or an off-node backup mount already exists is unconfirmed (9.2 Q4, Q13), so every option below is shown with and without one. Every figure is from the round-10 section of `budget.py`.

**The 4 TB ceiling (0.2's rule).** Planned at 3,600 GB usable, the 2 TB class's ratio (1,800 of 2,000), so the ceiling is min(0.8 x 3,600 + 120, 0.9 x 3,600) = min(3,000, 3,240) = **3,000 GB** and its 80% trigger 2,400 GB. A12's method on a 3.84 TB enterprise drive would give (3,840 - 60) x 0.985 = 3,723, planned at 3,700 and a 3,080 GB ceiling, so 3,000 is the conservative reading. A replacement (not an addition) keeps one drive per node, so R9's two-drive exposure does not arise; it means each node is rebuilt onto its new drive, which costs nothing if the drives are in before the initial formation (6.1) and one empty-node replacement per node (3.3, drilled in P0c) if they arrive after it.

**When the 4 TB replacement must land.** Before any backfill (no S1 or face code is written on 2 TB: at mid the face-free footprint plus the first spaces is 1,558 GB, 100% of 1,560, at the Q1 exit) and before the face-free footprint crosses 1,560 GB at month 3.7 (2027-02-21) at mid. For the first spaces to ship with Recon's Q1 move, their 9.4-day CPU backfill has to start by 2027-01-20, so the replacement is done on every node by about 2027-01-15, best before the initial formation.

**Runway with full replicas** (first crossing of each ceiling; the S1 lines count every space at full size from month 0):

| Scenario | Footprint | 2 TB (1,560) | 4 TB (3,000) | 3x, % of 3,000 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| low | faces + every S1 space | month 3.5 (2027-02) | month 20.7 (2028-07) | 3,282 GB, 109% |
| low | faces + first spaces | month 3.9 (2027-02) | month 21.6 (2028-08) | 3,197 GB, 107% |
| low | faces, no S1 | month 4.6 (2027-03) | month 23.1 (2028-10) | 3,071 GB, 102% |
| low | face-free, no S1 | month 5.4 (2027-04) | month 25.0 (2028-12) | 2,930 GB, 98% |
| mid | faces + every S1 space | month 0.1 (2026-11) | **month 13.2 (2027-12)** | 4,185 GB, 140% |
| mid | faces + first spaces | month 0.9 (2026-11) | month 14.9 (2028-01) | 3,936 GB, 131% |
| mid | faces, no S1 | month 1.4 (2026-12) | month 16.1 (2028-03) | 3,775 GB, 126% |
| mid | face-free, no S1 | month 3.7 (2027-02) | month 21.3 (2028-08) | 3,224 GB, 107% |
| high | faces + every S1 space | now | month 3.9 (2027-02) | 6,482 GB, 216% |
| high | faces + first spaces | now | month 5.8 (2027-04) | 5,790 GB, 193% |
| high | faces, no S1 | now | month 6.6 (2027-05) | 5,559 GB, 185% |
| high | face-free, no S1 | month 1.3 (2026-12) | month 15.8 (2028-02) | 3,812 GB, 127% |

So on 4 TB with full replicas, mid holds about 13 months with the whole design (to about 2027-12), and no scenario reaches 3x with faces. High also exceeds the ROC RAM cap today (278M representatives against 205M), so high is not workable on this hardware without both a retention window and a face-scope cut.

**Options, ranked by retrieval speed kept per GB saved** (GB per node at mid, months 6 / 12 / 24 = 3x):

| # | Option | Saves (GB) | Needs | Retrieval cost | Directive bent |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Valve 3: hot cap 200M to 150M | 7 / 7 / 7 | WS7 | style similarity scoped to the newest 150M records instead of 200M | none |
| 2 | Valve 5: S-FACE rerank int8 to 4-bit | 34 / 46 / 69 | E18 | small shortlist recall loss, measured in P0a; ROC verifies every shortlist | none |
| 3 | Valve 1: warm FTS pruning | 47 / 76 / 134 | WS7, the stopword analyzer | pruned terms match warm text only through the posted-window path | none |
| 4 | S-IMG and S-TATTOO on the hot window only | 23 / 37 / 66 | none | no scene or tattoo similarity over warm images (near-duplicate S-PDQ and S-TEXT stay corpus-wide) | multimodal from day one, for scene search on warm data |
| 5 | Valve 4: warm text off node | 63 / 103 / 183 | E17 and an object store | warm text rows fetched from the store (tens of ms per page) | none |
| 6 | Valve 2: non-representative gallery off node | 89 / 120 / 181 | an object store | the exhaustive face job streams segments: minutes instead of about 40 s | none |
| 7 | Fewer copies: warm partitions on 2 of 4 nodes (RF2) | 404 / 658 / 1,166 | engine work (E26, below) | warm reads served by 2 nodes not 4: half the warm read throughput, a second node in every corpus-wide warm query, degraded on a node loss | **replicate, do not shard** |
| 8 | Retention: warm partitions off node | 808 / 1,316 / 2,332 | an object store (restore on demand to n4) or upstream RedLight as the source (9.2 Q8) | warm data leaves interactive retrieval: minutes to restore, or upstream's own API without SPORK's features | retrieval speed first; set-oriented retrieval over the corpus |

Not recommended: dropping the S-FACE rerank entirely (138 GB at 3x; ROC would rescore a longer shortlist on the interactive path) and dropping the S-TEXT rerank on warm partitions (45 GB; the one-bit scan alone gave 0.763 recall@10 in the spike). Warm bytes per node at mid 3x are 2,332 GB: rec 1,116 + txt warm 562 + media 336 + S1 317.

**Fewer copies, checked in code.** `ha-raft` runs one Raft group per cluster: `RaftHAServer` derives the group id from the cluster name and makes every configured peer a member, and every transaction and schema entry is applied on every peer through `ArcadeStateMachine.databaseFor`. A database a peer does not hold is a critical apply error (the bootstrap path's own comment: an unusable, unreopenable database "reach[es] applyTransaction's critical-error halt"). `HA_AUTO_ACQUIRE_DATABASES=false` only stops a joining node from pulling databases it has never seen; it does not exempt it from applies. Ratis itself can host several groups per server (`RaftLogCacheEviction` already iterates `server.getGroupIds()`), but a second group would need either 2 voters, which cannot lose a member and keep writing, or 3, which saves only a quarter. The shape that fits is therefore **E26: warm sealed partitions leave the Raft group** as read-only node-local databases placed by the plugin on 2 nodes (one of n2 and n3, one of n1 and n4, about 4.7 warm partitions per node at 3x), since between deletion batches they take no writes and deletions and aging already run as an offline rewrite on n4 followed by an admission (3.5), which would target the 2 holders instead of the group. Whether a server running `ha-raft` can also serve a non-replicated local database is not verified (`ServerDatabase.isReplicated()` exists per database; nothing was found that exempts a database from the group), so E26 starts with a spike in Phase 0 and is sized by it.

**Combinations against the 4 TB ceiling** (faces + every S1 space; savings that overlap are not counted twice):

| Combination | Owner decisions | mid crosses 3,000 | mid 3x | low | high | mid at the 2,400 trigger |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| none | none | month 13.2 (2027-12) | 140% | month 20.7 (2028-07) | month 3.9 (2027-02) | month 7.6 (2027-06) |
| **A**: valves 3, 1, 5 and S-IMG/S-TATTOO on the hot window | scene search on warm data | month 14.9 (2028-01) | 130% | month 22.9 (2028-09) | month 5.0 (2027-04) | month 8.9 (2027-07) |
| **B** = A + valves 2 and 4 | the object store must exist | month 17.9 (2028-04) | 118% | month 26.0 (2029-01) | month 7.4 (2027-06) | month 11.0 (2027-10) |
| **C** = A + RF2 warm partitions (E26), **decided** | replicate, do not shard (now for hot data only) | **month 26.6 (2029-01)** | **95%** | month 40.6 (2030-03) | month 9.9 (2027-08) | month 16.8 (2028-03) |
| **D** = A + retention | warm data off interactive retrieval; a store or upstream as the source | beyond month 60 | 59% | beyond month 60 | month 27.6 (2029-02) | month 50.3 (2031-01) |
| **E** = C + valves 2 and 4 | both of C's, and the store must exist | month 32.1 (2029-07) | 86% | month 45.6 (2030-08) | month 14.8 (2028-01) | month 20.8 (2028-07) |

A retention window sized for mid 3x after A keeps the newest 627M of 900M records on node (70%) and moves the oldest 273M off (909 GB over the ceiling at 3,331 B per warm record).

**Recommended (mid), and what the owner decided.** The owner chose **plan C** on 2026-11-03 (items 2 and 3, over item 5); item 4 (it depends on whether an object store exists) and option 4 inside A are still open.
1. The 4 TB replacement on every node by about 2027-01-15, ideally before the initial formation, and in any case before any backfill (hard deadline 2027-02-21).
2. **A from Q2** (valves 3 and 1 land with WS7, valve 5 and S-IMG/S-TATTOO on the hot window with the Q2 spaces): mid holds to about 2028-01 (month 14.9). Its one directive cost is scene and tattoo similarity on warm data.
3. **C as the path to 3x (decided, plan C)**: E26 built in train 3 and in production before A's trigger (2,400 GB at about 2027-07), so mid holds to about 2029-01 (month 26.6) and is at 95% of the ceiling at 3x. "Replicate, do not shard" now holds for hot data only: hot partitions, `identity`, `recdir`, `ops` and `audit` stay on all 4 nodes. Option 4 inside A (scene and tattoo on the hot window) is still open; without it plan C holds mid to month 26.0 (2029-01) at 96% at 3x, and E26's deadline moves only to month 8.6 (still about 2027-07; `budget.py`).
4. **If an object store exists, E** (C plus valves 2 and 4): mid holds to about 2029-07 (86% at 3x).
5. **Not chosen: D** (retention) was the alternative that reaches 3x at mid, and it needs an object store or upstream RedLight as the source of the warm data.
6. **High** is not workable on this hardware with faces: even E crosses at month 14.8 and ROC is over its RAM cap today. If P0b measures high, the choice is D plus a face-scope cut (representatives restricted to clusters active in the last 3 years, the exhaustive job for the rest).

**RAM on 126 GB.** The ROC interactive gallery passes its 205M failover-safe cap at month 15.5 (2028-02) at mid, today at high, never at low; with H2 void the fixes are ROC indexed 1:N (Q-ROC1) or restricting representatives to clusters active in the last 3 years, with the exhaustive job keeping recall. The vector slice is fine (3.0 GB at mid 3x after S-STYLE moves in Q2). The self-hosted decider (49 GiB in FP16) has no card to run on, so the decision layer runs the rules fallback, plus a hosted provider only on states without personal data and with owner sign-off (9.2 Q23, R26, R30).

**CPU-only encoding on the 16 encode cores** (planning figures measured in P0a with A16: PDQ 10 ms per image, gte-multilingual-base int8 60 ms per unique text, YuNet 5 ms per image, AuraFace-v1 int8 0.25 core-s per face, the scene model 0.5 core-s per image, PP-OCRv5 1.75 s per text-bearing image; ROC DETECT 0.1 core-s per image on the 12 `roc-work` cores):

| Backfill at mid today | Core-seconds | Days on 16 cores | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-PDQ, every distinct image (435M) | 4.3M | 3.1 | Q1, train 1 |
| S-TEXT, unique texts (145M) | 8.7M | 6.3 | Q1, train 1 |
| S-FACE: YuNet on every image, AuraFace on the 87M representatives | 23.9M | 17.3 | Q2, after P0d and the ROC face backfill (ROC DETECT 42 days on the `roc-work` cores, in parallel) |
| S-IMG on hot-window representatives (110M of 290M records) | 24.7M | 17.9 | Q2, after P0d (option 4) |
| S-IMG on every PDQ-cluster representative | 65.2M | 47.2 | not needed under option 4 |
| S-IMG on every distinct image | 217.5M | 157.3 | not planned |
| OCR on every text-bearing image (152M) | 266.4M | 192.7 | not planned: daily stream only, case- and target-linked images first |

The day-one spaces take 9.4 days; with S-FACE and hot-window S-IMG the corpus backfill is about 45 days of the encode cores across Q1 and Q2. The daily stream fits: today PDQ, text, YuNet, AuraFace on representatives and the scene model on representatives take 1.2 h of the 16 cores, plus 3.2 h of OCR on every text-bearing image; at the 3x pace the same set takes 5.1 h, and OCR on every text-bearing image would add 13.3 h, so OCR at 3x covers case- and target-linked images only (10%, +1.3 h) unless the owner adds a GPU after all.

## 1. Topology

### 1.1 Nodes and roles (4 single servers)

```
             Recon (Bun/TS) -> gateway (signs X-Spork-Context) -> L7 balancer
                                                     |
   +-----------------+-----------------+-----------------+------------------+
   | n1 VOTER        | n2 VOTER        | n3 VOTER        | n4 LISTENER      |
   | priority 10     | priority 5      | priority 5      | (Q1: replica,    |
   | writes, ingest, | interactive     | interactive     |  priority 0)     |
   | audit appends,  | reads           | reads           | analytics, batch |
   | leased jobs     |                 |                 | offline builds   |
   | light reads     |                 |                 | (16 GB slice),   |
   |                 |                 |                 | ROC workers      |
   | roc-agent       | roc-agent       | roc-agent       | roc-agent        |
   +-----------------+-----------------+-----------------+------------------+
        one Raft group (Ratis, mTLS), every database replicated on every node
                                                     |
       S3 object store: originals, crops, sealed gallery segment copies,
       backups (after W3d), WORM anchors, warm text (after E17)
```

**One Raft group.** `RaftHAServer` builds a single `RaftGroup` from the cluster name. Splitting the corpus into databases does not create more groups. Every database has replication factor 4 (`HA_AUTO_ACQUIRE_DATABASES=true`, the compiled-in default, stated for explicitness).

**Target shape: 3 voters + 1 non-voting listener.**
- Commits need 2 of 3 acknowledgements instead of 3 of 4.
- Fault tolerance is unchanged: 4 voters also survive only 1 loss.
- n4 can rebuild, resync or run nightly graph jobs for hours without slowing commits or using up the failure budget.
- If a voter dies for good, n4 is promoted by a membership change.

**Q1 shape (no engine work).**
- `HA_SERVER_ROLE` accepts only `any` and `replica`, and `replica` only sets priority 0 (`RaftHAServer` around line 683). So Q1 runs 4 voters with n4 as `replica`, and commits need 3 of 4.
- E4 (3.6) adds a `listener` role using Ratis `RaftPeerRole.LISTENER`, which Ratis 3.3.1 defines.

**ROC on every node.**
- Each node runs a `roc-agent` sidecar. It owns the `roc-serve` processes, holds the node's quarter of the interactive gallery in RAM, and holds all gallery segments on disk.
- ROC search is cgroup-limited to 16 cores (`roc-search`), so a face search cannot starve database reads. ROC workers (DETECT, CLUSTER) run in a separate `roc-work` cgroup (6 cores on n4, 2 on n1 to n3), so templatizing never slows the interactive fan-out (4, 6.3).

**Snapshot and resync settings.**
- `HA_SNAPSHOT_MAX_CONCURRENT=3` (default 2): one slot per follower, so no follower is ever refused with 503 while the other two pull. The semaphore is sized once when `SnapshotHttpHandler` is built, so the value is permanent, not toggled per window. Round 3 rejected 1: follower retries back off at base x 2^(attempt-1) with no jitter (`SnapshotInstaller.downloadWithRetry`), so followers refused together retry together and collide, and the last follower of a 120 GB admission waited 9.5 to 10.8 minutes, set by the backoff schedule rather than the link (3.5). E0 still sets `HA_SNAPSHOT_INSTALL_RETRIES=6` and `HA_SNAPSHOT_INSTALL_RETRY_BASE_MS=30000` (defaults 3 and 5,000 ms, about 35 s of total backoff) so a transient transfer error does not quarantine a database; total backoff is 31.5 minutes.
- `HA_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 120 s) also bounds the reconcile listing fetch, and that listing makes the leader compute a full-content SHA-256 of every database on every request (`BootstrapFingerprint`, no cache). E0 sets it to 3,600,000 (1 h) today and 5,400,000 (1.5 h) once databases pass 2 TB per node: at least 1.5 x the full-hash time P0c measures. The cost is that a cold cluster start waits that long for an absent peer before falling back to majority. E2 removes fingerprints from the listing (3.6).
- Monitoring never calls `GET /api/v1/cluster?presence=true`: it fans the same full-hash RPC out to every peer (`GetClusterHandler`).
- `HA_SNAPSHOT_COMPRESSION_LEVEL=0` on a dedicated 10 GbE link. The default level 1 DEFLATE runs on one HTTP worker thread per transfer, which caps resync speed. P0 measures level 0 against level 1.
- Raft log retention: `HA_LOG_PURGE_UPTO_SNAPSHOT=false` permanently until E0.5; see 0.6 and 3.2 rule 6 (`HA_LOG_PURGE_GAP` is not a retention window).
