- **Stale-follower recovery (round 4).** `HealthMonitor.checkStaleFollower` calls `recoverFromPersistentLag` once a follower's applied index has lagged the commit index by `HA_STALE_FOLLOWER_LAG_THRESHOLD` (default 10,000) entries for `HA_STALE_FOLLOWER_RECOVERY_DURATION_MS` (default 60,000), and recovery is `downloadAllDatabasesFrom`: every database on that follower, 1,005 GB today and 3,149 GB at mid 3x. The forced install of a backfill or aging admission runs on the apply thread (`ArcadeStateMachine.installLeaderCopy`) and sets neither `snapshotDownloadInProgress` nor `catchingUp`, the only exemptions `isFollowerLaggingBeyond` knows, so during a 6 to 10 minute admission stall (3.5) the lag check sees a stopped follower. Unbatched MediaJob claims and completions alone are (1.25M + 626K) x 2 / 86,400 = 43 entries/s at 3x pace, which passes 10,000 in 230 s, inside the stall. E0 therefore sets `HA_STALE_FOLLOWER_RECOVERY_DURATION_MS=1800000` (30 minutes, above the worst admission stall plus margin), workers batch claims and completions 50 per transaction (0.9 entries/s), and the P0c gate asserts that no follower logs "Persistent follower lag ... triggering snapshot recovery" during an admission (R18).
- **LSM compaction (round 4, confirmed in round 6).** Per database: `arcadedb.indexCompactionFullSeriesThreshold=0`, `arcadedb.indexCompactionFullDeadEntriesRatio=0`, `arcadedb.indexCompactionMinPagesSchedule=64` on `identity` and the current period (`recdir` has only a HASH index); full compactions are leased plugin jobs (0.4). `02-retrieval-speed.md` C5 now states the same policy.
- **Vector settings are database settings.** `arcadedb.vectorIndex.searchCacheSize=500000` is set per `txt_*` database (`ALTER DATABASE` in the schema installer, also for n4 builds and `_w<n>` rewrites); it is not an index metadata key (`LSMVectorIndexMetadata.USER_METADATA_KEYS` does not list it and `IndexMetadata` refuses unknown keys). `storeVectorsInGraph=true` stays per-index metadata. `arcadedb.externalPropertyBucketDefaultPageSize=16384` is set on each `txt_<p>` before the EXTERNAL `text` property is created (0.4).

### 1.2 Databases (identical set on every node)

The engine never queries across databases, so the split follows graph locality. Data that must be traversed together sits in one database, and data naturally bounded by time is split into periods. The SPORK plugin composes results in process against local copies. That is a local fan-out, not scatter-gather.

| Database | Contents | Size cap | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `identity` | Shared Identifier vertices (only identifiers that link 2 or more records): Phone, Email, Handle, Name, Business, ImageHash, Url. Also FaceCluster, Place (geo index), Identity, MEMBER_OF, CO_OCCURS (aggregated), soft edges, ResolutionDecision, Target (board), Case, CaseMember, Evidence, WikiRevision, Binding, Watch, Alert, Detector, LegalHold, RetentionPolicy, Mission, CommandKey, MindMapNote, RankerModel, Feedback, BoardLabel. The ontology registry (ObjectType, PropertyType, LinkType, Marking, 2.7), saved ObjectSet definitions and snapshots (4b), the decision layer's DecisionLog, OutcomeFeedback, ThresholdPolicy and AnalystQueue (4d). Also the plugin's term document-frequency table, its AuditEntry chain and Outbox. | Grows with shared identifiers: 89 GB mid today, 322 GB at 3x (of which 43 GB the version history of `Identity` and `MEMBER_OF`, round 7). Crosses 120 GB about month 3.5 at mid; cold install (E1) above that, or split into `identity` + `identity_img` if E1 slips (3.2). | Every analyst-facing governed write sits on one chain and one transaction boundary. The board has a single home. |
| `rec_<p>` | Record (all structured fields, `_prov`, `markings`), partition-local identifier vertices for every identifier on the period's records (shared or not), lightweight `HAS_*` edges, image-hash BY ITEM index, gram and phonetic lists, record indexes, the (tenant, importedId) index, AuditEntry, Outbox | 120 GB, about 75M records at 1,595 B | About 4 periods today, 12 at 3x. A period is sealed when full. |
| `txt_<p>` | TextBody (deduplicated by exact-byte textHash): inline title and stored `text` (title + body, EXTERNAL LZ4, on node in both tiers until E17), FULL_TEXT on `text`, style vectors (hot only), the S-TEXT `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` codes (4c, from Q1, round 8), AuditEntry. Warm partitions are produced by an offline rewrite under the aging protocol and named `txt_<p>_w<n>` (0.4, 3.5). | The `rec` period boundary: about 73 GB hot (75M x 951 B), 60 GB warm, plus 7 GB of S-TEXT codes. Round 4: not 120 GB, because the aging rewrite needs a restored source copy next to its output and archive on n4 (3.5): 72 + 60 + 30 = 162 GB fits the 180 GB slot, 120 + 101 + 50 = 271 GB would not. | Physical redaction boundary: only the console role has a grant on `txt_*` (5.4) |
| `media_<p>` | Image (content sha, upstream hash, pHash and the 32 B PDQ hash, `pdqBands` with its BY ITEM index on hot-window partitions only (`06-stylometry-perceptual-hash.md` 3.1), dims, firstSeen, `novelty` scalar), Face (bbox, quality, age band, minorFlag, gallery segment and slot, templateSha, clusterKey, representative), Tattoo, one `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` index each for S-IMG, S-PDQ, S-FACE and S-TATTOO sharing the space's global codebook (4c.2, S-PDQ from Q1, the others from Q2; round 9), AuditEntry | 120 GB (mid about 36 GB of metadata plus 26 GB of S1 codes per 75M-record period, plus 16 GB of PDQ band keys while the partition is inside the hot window, round 6) | Same period boundaries as `rec_<p>` (first seen). A corpus-wide S1 probe scans this period's index as one of `partitions` x nprobe posting reads (4c.3). |
| `recdir` | Record directory: `{upstreamRid, period}` with a UNIQUE HASH `upstreamRid` index (4 KB pages, round 6) | 20 GB today, 63 GB at 3x (70 B per record) | Makes a hydrate two index probes per upstream RID (2.5) |
| `ops` | MediaJob and enrichment queues (rows deleted on completion), IngestCheckpoint, DeletionOrder retention ledger, ROC gallery manifest with tombstones, leases, Presence table (120-day rolling), (identifierKey, dayBucket) counters (120 days), mission and watch run leases and schedules (`nextRunAt`), the `transient-slot` lease (0.4), the compaction schedule, the ObjectSet LRU manifest (4b) | 20 GB today, 60 GB at 3x (bounded, 0.4) | |
| `audit` | Cross-database chain anchors, verification checkpoints, rotated chain archives | 1 GB today + about 20 GB/yr | |

Two things live on node disk but in no database:
- **ROC gallery segment files** (`/var/lib/spork/roc/segments`, the same filesystem).
- **ROC process state.**

**Canonical keys across databases, never RIDs.** Each database references another only by canonical key (2.3): Record to identifier by identifier key, Record to image by the upstream image hash (mapped to the content sha in `media_<p>`), Face to image by `imageSha`, Record to text by `textHash`, Record to place by `placeId` (a LONG hash of the place key). This fixes the RID-link flaw. Offline rebuilds, re-imports, restores from older backups and backfill builds of any single database never break links.

### 1.3 Disk budget and pressure valves

Section 0.5 gives the full per-node footprint. Everything (databases root, Raft storage, ROC segments, staging) sits on one XFS filesystem on one LVM logical volume.

At mid the 1,248 GB order trigger is already reached, so disks are ordered now (H1). One saving is already in the base design (round 3 moved warm text off node from the base design to valve 4, because the FULL_TEXT index needs the stored `text` to remove a deleted ad's postings, 0.4):
- **E1 cold install.** Keeps headroom at 120 GB while `identity` grows past it. At mid 3x it saves 322.4 - 120 = 202 GB compared with the stock engine (round 7: `identity` includes 42.9 GB of version history at 3x).

These valves exist only from Q2 (round 4) and bridge the second expansion, not H1:

1. **Warm FTS pruning** (not counted). Saves 0.4 x 480 B per warm record: 700M x 192 = 134 GB at 3x. It needs the stopword analyzer (4, Full-text: resolved by convention, not index metadata) and is applied only in the offline rewrite of a sealed partition (WS7, Q2). Pruned terms then match only through the hot tier and the posted-window path. P0 measures the saving and the recall cost.
2. **Non-representative gallery segments off node.** The exhaustive job streams them from the object store, so it takes minutes instead of about 40 s. Saves 0.6 of the all-face gallery: 181 GB at mid 3x, 0.6 x 0.75 x 448 = 202 B per record before that. Exists only after the W8 face backfill.
3. **Lower the hot cap from 200M to 150M records.** 50M x (951 - 803) = 7 GB. Needs the aging rewrite (WS7).
4. **Warm text off node (needs E17, built only if disk needs it).** With a plugin-keyed FULL_TEXT index (E17, 3.6) the offline rewrite moves `text` to the object store (12 B pointer) and the deletion saga removes postings from the object-store copy before deleting it: 273 - 12 = 261 B per warm record, 47 GB today and 183 GB at 3x. Not available before E17, so not in the dates below.
5. **4-bit face rerank codes (4c).** S-FACE rerank at 256 B instead of int8 512 B: 0.3 x 256 = 77 B per record, 69 GB at mid 3x, at a recall cost measured in P0a against ROC-verified pairs.

At mid, valves 1, 2, 3 and 5 total 177 GB at month 6, 249 GB at month 12 and 392 GB at month 24. They do not make 3x fit on 2 TB (mid 3x minus every valve is still 3,793 GB) and they are not available for the H1 date (0.6). Disk expansion is the plan of record.

The 384-d jvector text-embedding index of the base design stays removed: its float property and graph would cost about 103 GB at 3x and one 8M-bucket rebuild about 21.6 GB of heap (1.4). The text leg lives instead in the S-TEXT `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` space (4c): 92 B per record of disk and only centroids on heap.

Gallery replication factor 2 (each node keeps 2 of 4 quarters) is **not** a valve. It is partial replication, against the owner's direction, and is listed in section 10 as rejected unless the owner signs off.

### 1.4 RAM budget per node (126 GB)

| Slice | n1 to n3 | n4 | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| OS, kernel, agents | 6 | 6 | |
| Server JVM heap (ZGC generational) | 56 (`-Xmx56g`) | 43 (`-Xmx43g`) | Same today and at 3x |
| of which page cache (`arcadedb.maxPageRAM`) | 23 | 4 | On heap, no mmap. Round 6: the point-lookup indexes are HASH, so the resident index tops are HASH page 0 and directory pages (under 0.25 GB) plus the LSM composites' page 0, series tails and roots: 0.44 GB today, 1.3 GB at 3x (`02-retrieval-speed.md` 3.1), not the 3.8 GB bloom and series-root set of round 4; n4's 4 GB holds that set, so n4 takes the dossier and set-scoped hydrates as `02-retrieval-speed.md` routes them (P9c, P11), at 2.8% of its cache per 2,000-id hydrate. |
| of which vector residency | 8 | 8 | Style: 100M hot unique texts x (24 B INT8 code + about 35 B location; `VectorLocationIndex` retains about 32 B per location plus a reverse entry) = 5.9 GB from month 4.7 (the hot cap), 3.2 GB today. Search cache set in E0 to 0.5M vectors through the database setting `arcadedb.vectorIndex.searchCacheSize` (round 4: not index metadata; the default 0 auto-sizes to the whole id range, up to 25% of available heap per index): 16 buckets x 0.5M x 104 B = 0.8 GB, halved to 0.25M (0.4 GB) when the S1 first spaces ship in Q1 (WS15a, round 8; round 7: Q3). S1 centroids (4c, round 5: 36 B location and ordinal + int8 code + 64 B graph share per centroid, costed per space in 0.4): 7.1M centroids = 2.57 GB at mid 3x, 0.83 GB today, 5.34 GB at high 3x. **The slice does not hold both the style graphs and the S1 centroids:** 5.9 + 0.8 + 2.6 = 9.3 GB at mid 3x, over 8 GB from month 6.5 with the 0.8 GB cache and from month 12 (2027-11) with it halved. So S-STYLE moves onto the IVF index in Q2 (0.5M style centroids x 124 B = 0.06 GB; exact rescoring stays on the float32 `styleVec`; round 9: with the centroids of the spaces actually built, the slice is 5.7 GB at the Q1 exit with the style graphs still resident and 1.7 GB at the Q2 exit after the move, `budget.py`), after which the slice is 0.4 + 2.6 + 0.06 = 3.0 GB at mid 3x and 5.8 GB at high 3x, a 5 GB margin at mid. The PDQ posting size of 1,000 (1.35M centroids instead of 6.75M) saves 0.7 GB. Every jvector index is created with `storeVectorsInGraph=true`, so a graph built in the running JVM is reloaded as `OnDiskGraphIndex` instead of staying on heap (the inline vectors are counted in 0.4). |
| of which vector rebuild transient (one permit) | 7 | 7 | One 24-d bucket of at most 8M: graph about 0.5 KB per node at degree 16 = 4 GB, plus build cache 8M x (96 + 64) B = 1.3 GB. `VECTOR_INDEX_MAX_CONCURRENT_REBUILDS=1` |
| of which query and OLAP working memory | 8 | 16 | **Full-text (round 5):** the BM25 top-K path (`FullTextTopK`, done in the partial-text worktree, switch `arcadedb.fullText.topK`, database scope, default true; round 6: the key exists only in that worktree and is absent from `GlobalConfiguration` in the main tree until the Q2 merge, so no day-1 configuration file carries it) reads each term's postings once into primitive arrays (RID long, tf, dl: about 16 B per posting, about 80 ns per posting), so an eligible query costs about 16 B x the summed df of its terms. The general path (`FullTextQueryExecutor`) still builds a separate `HashMap<RID, AtomicInteger>` (about 100 B per entry) for every MUST term before intersecting and collects every SHOULD term in full. The plugin therefore gates on the **sum** of df over the query's terms (from its df table) with two bounds: on the top-K path at most 12% of the partition's unique texts (about 40M per period, so 4.8M postings, 77 MB per partition); on the general path, which serves phrases, wildcard, prefix, fuzzy and regexp expansions, `NOT`, minimum-should-match, queries without a limit and **any RID allow-list pushdown** (the set-scoped full-text of 4b and 4c), at most 2% (0.8M candidates, 80 MB per partition). Partitions run in parallel only while the summed heap across them stays under 0.3 GB; otherwise sequentially, newest first. Queries over the per-partition bound are refused on n1 to n3 or sent to n4, which runs them sequentially (a 30% df term is about 0.2 GB per partition on the top-K path, 1.2 GB on the general path). **n4 components job:** a streaming union-find in the plugin, not a `GraphAnalyticalView` (2.6): sorted RID `long[]` 5.12 + parent `int[]` 2.56 + size `int[]` 2.56 = 10.2 GB at 3x (640M vertices), released after the job. A GAV CSR would need about 26.6 GB steady at 3x (positions 5.12, forward and backward offsets per edge type 10.24, neighbors 3.6, BFS reorder permutations 5.12, one algorithm array 2.56) and over 36 GB at build peak, more than this slice. |
| of which GC headroom | 10 | 8 | ZGC needs about 15 to 20% of the heap free to avoid allocation stalls (8 / 43 = 19%). Page reads allocate on heap: with the schema v1 page sizes a 2,000-id hydrate allocates about 112 MB, not 1.3 GB (2.5). |
| JVM native (metaspace, code cache, thread stacks, GC metadata) | 3 | 3 | |
| Direct memory (Netty, gRPC, Ratis log cache) | 4 | 4 | |
| ROC process (cap) | 45 | 45 | 4 overhead + 1 case/watch gallery + 5 tattoo quarter + 35 face templates. Steady state about 26 GB of face templates (51.3M x 512 B). After absorbing a failed node's share: 68.4M x 512 B = 35 GB. |
| Encoder sidecar (4c) | 3 | 3 | ONNX Runtime, int8 CPU models (YuNet, AuraFace-v1, SigLIP 2 B or PE-Core B, gte-multilingual-base, OpenSearch sparse encoder, LUAR, PP-OCRv5 mobile: about 1 GB of weights) plus batch buffers and the probe-embedding cache, in an `encode` cgroup of 4 cores (2 on n1). Serves probe encoding for interactive queries on n2 and n3 and ingest encoding on n4 (and n2, n3 while an n4 build runs). A GPU per node or a GPU burst host is owner question 22; on CPU the daily stream fits today (2.6 h of tier A plus 3.2 h of OCR on 16 cores cluster-wide) and not at 3x pace (10.9 + 13.3 h). The DB JVM never loads CUDA. |
| Build slice (n4 only) | 0 | 16 | A separate JVM for embedded `GraphBatch` builds (backfills and every hot-to-warm `txt` rewrite, 3.5) and the transform step: `-Xmx12g` with `arcadedb.maxPageRAM` 4 GB, plus 2 GB native and direct. **Every build runs `withWAL(true)` (round 5):** the GraphBatch Javadoc recommends WAL off for imports, but the read-bench worktree measured that `withWAL(false)` retains about 8 KB of heap per record until the JVM is exhausted (a 24 GB heap died at `close()` on 5M records; 12 GB filled at 2M, and this slice would fill at 1.5M), the suspect being the page publication path when the commit has no WAL ticket (`TransactionContext.commit2ndPhase` to `PageManager.publishPages`). With WAL on the same loads peak and collect normally (`workstreams/retrieval-bench.md`). Its identifier-count pass uses an external sort on disk, not the 8 GB off-heap map of the initial load (6.1). The stateless enrichment workers (6.2) run here as a 2 GB process; while a build runs they pause on n4 and run on n2 and n3 out of their OS file cache. At 3x pace a period seals about every 3.2 months, so builds recur. |
| OS file cache (EXTERNAL text, `.vecgraph`, cold LSM pages) | 9 | 6 | Round 4: 12 to 9 on n1 to n3 to fund the encoder sidecar |
| **Sum** | 6 + 56 + 3 + 4 + 45 + 3 + 9 = **126** | 6 + 43 + 3 + 4 + 45 + 3 + 16 + 6 = **126** | Heap: 23 + 8 + 7 + 8 + 10 = 56 on n1 to n3; 4 + 8 + 7 + 16 + 8 = 43 on n4 (OLAP 19 to 16 funds n4's sidecar; the union-find needs 10.2 GB) |

There is no per-item-resident face index. At mid 3x, ArcFace INT8 in jvector would have been 675M x 512 B = 346 GB of disk and 36 to 108 B per vector of heap (the location index alone is 29 GB at 900M, `workstreams/vector-scale.md`), so it did not fit at all. The S-FACE `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` space (4c) indexes 270M representatives at 3x for 157 GB of disk and 1.35M centroids x 612 B = 0.83 GB of heap (int8 512-d centroid codes), and ROC 1:1 verifies every shortlist.

The page cache covers about 23 / 3,149 = 0.73% of database bytes at mid 3x. Reads that do not ask for history touch no history page and carry no as-of predicate (round 7): the `_VersionHistory` store is a separate internal type with its own buckets and indexes, written only on an update or delete of a versioned record, so it competes for the cache only when `history.*` is called. Interactive latency therefore depends on NVMe random reads (about 100 us) with the index tops cached (HASH page 0 and directories, LSM composite tails and roots: 0.44 GB today, 1.3 GB at 3x, round 6), and on bytes moved, because the engine reads whole pages into the on-heap cache. With the schema v1 page sizes (0.4) a 2,000-id hydrate moves about 112 MB, 0.5% of a 23 GB cache and 2.8% of n4's 4 GB; with the stock 256 KB index and 64 KB bucket pages it would move 1.3 GB, evicting 5.7% of n1 to n3's cache and a third of n4's per call. Interactive hydrates go to n2 and n3; dossier footprints and set-scoped geo and graph calls (P9c, P11) go to n4, whose cache holds no interactive hot month to protect (round 6, D52). Section 4 states latencies for cold rows as reads at 32-way and bytes over 5 to 6.5 GB/s (NVMe through dm-crypt). SATA SSD would miss the section 4 targets by 3x to 5x.

**RAM breakpoints**

| Component | Breaks at | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| ROC interactive gallery | More than 205M representatives: mid about month 15.5 (2028-02), high today | ROC indexed 1:N (Q-ROC1); round 10: H2 (256 GB per node) is void on fixed hardware. Meanwhile restrict representatives to clusters active in the last 3 years. The exhaustive job covers the rest. |
| Vector residency | More than 8 GB: with the style jvector graphs resident, month 6.5 at mid (0.8 GB cache) or month 12 (cache halved), 9.3 GB at mid 3x; S1 centroids alone are 2.6 GB at mid 3x and 5.3 GB at high 3x | S-STYLE onto the IVF index in Q2 (round 9; frees 5.9 GB; slice 3.0 GB at mid 3x, 5.8 at high); then an off-heap location index and codes (`workstreams/vector-scale.md`, deferred), 4-bit centroid codes (saves 1.2 GB at mid 3x) or larger postings (fewer centroids) at a recall cost measured by the P0a nprobe gate |
| Encoder sidecar on CPU | Daily encode exceeds the off-peak window at about 1M distinct images/day (10.9 h tier A + 13.3 h OCR at 3x pace on 16 cores) | GPU per node or a GPU burst host (9.2 Q22); OCR only on text-bearing images flagged by the detector |
| n4 components union-find | More than 16 GB working memory: at 16 B per vertex, about 1B registry vertices (beyond 3x) | Hub and stop identifiers are already excluded at the scan. Then an external-sort union-find on disk. Never a named GAV (2.6). |

### 1.5 Network

- Raft and snapshot traffic run on a dedicated 10/25 GbE VLAN with Raft mTLS (`HA_TLS_*`).
- **Full node acquire at mid 3x** (3,063 GB of databases) at 0.6 to 1.0 GB/s with compression level 0 takes 51 to 85 minutes of transfer. Before that, the leader's reconcile listing hashes every database end to end (1 to 2 GB/s, single-threaded): 8 to 16 minutes today (1,005 GB), 27 to 52 minutes at mid 3x, on n1, which also runs ingest. Until E2 (3.6), every resync caused by a purged log pays both, not just the changed databases.
- **ROC segments** (316 GB including tattoos) come from peers or the object store in 5 to 9 minutes.

### 1.6 If "4 clusters" means 4 multi-node clusters

Adding nodes to a fully replicated cluster never raises the per-node disk ceiling. Each option changes something else:

- **Option A: one large SPORK cluster on the same site.** For example 12 nodes: 3 or 5 voters (5 tolerates 2 failures), the rest listeners (after E4).
  - Read throughput scales with node count.
  - ROC RAM scales too: failover-safe capacity = 11 x 68.4M = 752M templates, which covers mid 3x all faces (675M) interactively.
  - Disk per node is unchanged, so H1 still applies.
- **Option B: functional placement (recommended if the nodes exist).** Each physical cluster is its own Raft group and fully replicates a subset of databases. No record is sharded, and every database is still replicated within its cluster. The SPORK router holds a placement map. Canonical-key links (1.2) make joins across clusters work without engine changes.

  | Cluster | Holds | Per-node data at mid 3x |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1, core | `identity`, `recdir`, `ops`, `audit`, hot `rec_*` and `txt_*` (with S-TEXT) | 322.4 + 63 + 60 + 41 + 200M x 1,595 B (319) + 190.1 + 200M x 92 B (18) = 1,014 GB; with fixed 190 + headroom 120 about 1,324 GB, under the 1,560 ceiling |
  | 2, history | warm `rec_*` and `txt_*`, all `media_*` (with S-IMG, S-PDQ, S-FACE, S-TATTOO) | 700M x 1,595 B (1,116) + 562 + 432 + 900M x 361 B of S1 codes (325) = 2,435 GB; about 2,745 GB with fixed + headroom, so this cluster needs H1 (61% of 4,520) |
  | 3, ROC gallery tier | All segments, all ROC RAM | Interactive capacity grows with cluster 3's RAM |
  | 4, DR, analytics and staging | Listeners of cluster 1 in a second site (after E4), backups, a 10% staging corpus | |

  A sealed period moves from cluster 1 to cluster 2 by backup and restore, then is dropped on cluster 1.
- **Different sites.** Do not stretch a voting Raft group across a WAN unless RTT is under about 5 ms, because every commit pays the RTT.
  - **Before E4:** DR is backups in the object store plus Outbox replay (RPO minutes to hours).
  - **After E4:** DR uses Ratis listeners (RPO seconds).
  - Asynchronous log shipping between independent clusters does not exist and is not planned for year 1.

---

## 2. Identity-centric data model

The vertex and edge types below are instances of the ontology contract in 2.7: every type here is registered as an ObjectType or LinkType, every property as a PropertyType, and the RID and canonical-key rules (2.3, 2.5) are unchanged by that registration.

### 2.1 Vertex types

| Type | Database | Key (UNIQUE HASH unless noted; round 6, `02-retrieval-speed.md` C8) | Main properties |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Identifier` (abstract): `Phone`, `Email`, `Handle`, `Name`, `Business`, `ImageHash`, `Url` | `identity` (shared) and `rec_<p>` (local) | canonical key (2.3) | identity copy: firstSeen, lastSeen, recordCount, periodMask (a bit per period), topCells, dayCells, componentId, hub, stop, holdCount, retentionClass. Local copy: kind-specific fields plus gram and phonetic lists. |
| `Phone` extras | | `ph:<10 digits>` | digits, rev (reversed digits), areaCode, exchange, carrier, cc, inferredAreaCode, grams4[] (local), digitSuffixes[] (local) |
| `Email` extras | | `em:<lowercase>` | domain, local, grams3[] |
| `Handle` extras | | `sh:<platform>:<handle>` | platform, handle (with a non-unique `handle` index on local and registry copies, for bare-handle intake, 7.2), grams3[] |
| `Name` extras | | `nm:<normalized full name>` | first, middle, last, aliases[], phonetic[] (Double Metaphone, Beider-Morse), tokens[], grams3[] |
| `Business` extras | | `bz:<lowercase alnum>` | businessName, erotic, opened, grams3[] (local) |
| `ImageHash` | | `ih:<upstream hash, verbatim>` | The upstream `File.hash` (or `sha256`) string exactly as Recon uses it, never truncated or re-keyed, so exact search, pivot, detector values and the image proxy round trip still work. Stored as 32 binary bytes when it is 64 hex, reversible to the exact string. Promoted only when an image appears in 2 or more records. P0a confirms the upstream algorithm. |
| `FaceCluster` | `identity` | `fk:<algo@ver>:<ulid>` | representative face keys (up to 3), memberFaceCount, minorFlag, firstSeen, lastSeen |
| `Place` | `identity` | `pl:<cc>:<state>:<city>[:<geohash7>]`; `placeId` LONG hash with its own UNIQUE HASH index (4 KB pages) | point (GEOSPATIAL), city, cityNorm (indexed, for city-only filters), state, county, zip, country, generalLocation |
| `Identity` | `identity` | `idn_<ulid>` | kind (person, persona, org, unknown), status (active, superseded), confidence, createdByDecision, displayName and the other analyst-assertable properties (2.7). **Versioned type** (round 7, `ALTER TYPE Identity VERSIONED true`): the engine stamps `_txFrom` (epoch microseconds, engine-owned) and keeps every superseded image in `_VersionHistory`, so `history.of(identity)` replays its states and `history.edges(identity, t)` its memberships as of `t` (2.6). |
| `Target` | `identity` | Recon's `tgt_` id, migrated verbatim as an opaque value and never recomputed. New ids are minted with Recon's `boardId` rule (sha256 of `type:normalizeAnchor(value)`, `ops/board.ts`), not `spork-keys`, so re-promoting an anchor upserts the same target. | Every `BoardTarget` field: anchor `{type, value}` verbatim, label, priority, assessment, disposition, assignee (indexed; Assigned requires one), caseRef (Recon's single value, set once with `??=`; also the first entry of caseIds[]), sources[] (the tokens the dossier and refreshes re-resolve), snapshot (the metadata-only engine Target document: features for ranker labels, places for the overview heat map, identifiers, topSignals, nextAction, confidence), history, notes, tags[], origin (intake, watch, manual), createdAt, updatedAt, revision (OCC). SPORK adds anchorKey (spork-keys canonical, for joins), identifierKeys[] (canonical keys of the anchor and every snapshot identifier, BY ITEM index, for deconfliction; round 4: a 7-digit phone contributes both its raw-digit key `ph:<7>` and the city-inferred `ph:<10>`, because board snapshots carry no city and Recon's `identKey` keeps 7 digits, so deconfliction stays a superset of Recon's matches), the board search grams (4), tenantId, caseIds[] (SPORK case membership), markings[] and `subjectDeleted` (set by the deletion saga, 5.5). |
| `Case`, `CaseMember`, `Evidence`, `WikiRevision`, `Binding`, `Detector`, `LegalHold`, `RetentionPolicy` | `identity` | case-scoped keys | tenantId, caseId, revision |
| `Watch` | `identity` | UNIQUE normalized source-set key (409 on a duplicate) | sources[], threshold, baseline (anchor to score map), lastScan, tenantId, createdOc (the creator's analyst, caseRef and purpose, under which every scan runs, 5.1) |
| `Alert` | `identity` | `al_<ulid>` (Recon's alert id) | watchKey, watchName, kind (threshold, new, escalated), anchor, score, delta, families[], command (the one-click drill Recon renders), at. Capped at 500 per tenant, cleared in bulk with audit; removing a watch deletes its alerts in the same audited transaction, as Recon's `removeWatch` does (round 4). |
| `Mission`, `CommandKey`, `MindMapNote` | `identity` | Mission id (UNIQUE, updatedAt index); CommandKey request key (UNIQUE) | Mission: revision (OCC), leads, iterations, events, hermesCaseId, automation (enabled, intervalMinutes, nextRunAt, consecutiveFailures), createdOc (as on Watch). CommandKey: missionId, fingerprint (a mismatch returns 409). Lead `sourceRefs` hold `{provider, recordId, origin, textHash}` and never the title (round 4: Recon's `targeting.ts` writes `title: record.title || id` today; WS4 changes it to emit the `textHash` that the ops-role hydrate projection now carries, the missions UI resolves titles at view time through the console role, and the WS10 import of legacy missions replaces every `sourceRefs[].title` with its `textHash` before the document lands in `identity`). |
| `RankerModel`, `Feedback`, `BoardLabel` | `identity` | model version; anchorKey; targetId | Recon's learned ranker weights (n, pos), analyst feedback verdicts, and the label ledger that keeps a repeated assessment toggle from training twice. Updated in the board PATCH transaction with audit. |
| `ResolutionDecision` | `identity` | `rd_<ulid>` | kind (merge, split, link, unlink), inputs[], outputs[], winnerKey (the fresh Identity key minted by a merge, 2.6), rule + version, actor, opContext, rationale, supersedes, revertedBy, deciderLogId (4d, when a decision model proposed it) |
| `ObjectType`, `PropertyType`, `LinkType`, `Marking` | `identity` | apiName (UNIQUE) | The ontology registry (2.7): ObjectType {apiName, title, primaryKeyProperty, titleProperty, interfaces[], prominent, defaultMarkings[], retentionClass}; PropertyType {objectType, apiName, baseType, searchable, markings[], pii, displayMask}; LinkType {apiName, sideA {objectType, apiName, display}, sideB {...}, cardinality, directed, propertyTypes[], transformId}; Marking {name, kind, parent}. Replicated schema data read by the plugin's query builders, the exporter and the link-analysis front end. |
| `ObjectSetDef`, `ObjectSetSnapshot` | `identity` | definition hash (UNIQUE); (defHash, at) | A saved selection as its algebra tree (4b) and, for watches, its materialized member keys at a point in time (roaring bitmap, envelope-encrypted) so a re-evaluation diff is a symmetric difference. tenantId, caseId, markings[], createdOc. |
| `DecisionLog`, `OutcomeFeedback`, `ThresholdPolicy`, `AnalystQueueItem` | `identity` | `dl_<ulid>`; decisionLogId; (question, riskLevel); `aq_<ulid>` | The decision layer's tables (4d): every decider call with model version, question hash, state hash, probabilities, confidence, threshold, action, latency and cost; the later outcome joined to it; the threshold per question and risk level as data; and the middle band routed to an analyst. |
| `Assertion` | `identity` | (subjectKey, propertyType) UNIQUE | Narrowed in round 7. One document per asserted property of a subject whose type is **not** versioned (a `Record` field, a shared identifier's display value): subjectKey, propertyType, value, `_prov` {sourceId, collectedAt, assertedBy, confidence}, markings[], state. Those subjects are rewritten by ingest and sync constantly, so versioning their types would version every upstream update; the assertion sits beside the ingested value and the read path overlays it. `Assertion` is itself a versioned type, so a correction updates `value` and `_prov` in place and `history.of(assertion)` replays every value; the round-4 `supersedes` chain is gone. Assertions on versioned subjects (`Identity`) need no Assertion document: the property is updated and the engine keeps the history (2.7). Analyst-rate, not budgeted. |
| `Record` | `rec_<p>` | `record_id` UUID (SPORK-assigned, period in the high bits); `upstreamRid` LONG (UNIQUE) | source, aggregator, website, urlHash, tsPosted, tsCollected, tsRemoved, articleType, views, age, coded arrays (category, services, rates, payment, clientele), Description fields, shanenet bitmask, stateCode (normalized at ingest), placeId, city, countryCode, areaCodes[], lat, lon, geoCell, textHash, imageHashes[] (upstream hashes, full length), display identifier arrays phones[], emails[], socials[], businessNames[] (Recon's display values, denormalized at ingest so a hydrate or search row reads no edge or local vertex), style (Recon's fingerprint label, for compare) and styleVec (exact float32 copy of `style[24]`, 96 B, 5.4), both computed **per record from the exact `article_body` bytes at ingest, before dedup**, by a golden-vector port of Recon's `identity.ts` `styleVector` and `stylometricFingerprint` (round 4: case and punctuation are features, so no normalization anywhere on that path), `_prov` (2.7), markings (bitmask), importedId (analyst imports, with a (tenant, importedId) index, 2.5), priorityScore (4d, ingest-time), retentionClass, holdCount, batchId. The denormalized fields cost 96 B per record and the ontology fields 28 B (0.4). Bucket created with 32 KB pages (`CREATE VERTEX TYPE Record ... PAGESIZE 32768`; 16 KB once M20's 256-slot format exists); `record_id` and `upstreamRid` UNIQUE HASH and `textHash` and `imageHashes BY ITEM` NOTUNIQUE HASH indexes at the engine default page sizes (4 KB for the fixed-width keys, 16 KB for BINARY and STRING, 0.4). |
| `TextBody` | `txt_<p>` | `textHash` = SHA-256 over the exact title + body bytes, **no normalization** (round 4; A5), stored as sha256/128, UNIQUE HASH at 16 KB pages; bucket 32 KB | title (inline), text (stored title + body, EXTERNAL LZ4 with 16 KB external pages, the only FULL_TEXT property; the body is derived from it by title length, and it stays on node in both tiers until E17 replaces it with textRef), style[24] (hot only, the jvector index property; identical for every record sharing the hash because the bytes are identical), textCode (S-TEXT 1-bit and 4-bit codes, 4c, from Q1), sparseTerms (OpenSearch learned-sparse weights for `LSM_SPARSE_VECTOR`, optional, 4c), lang |
| `Image` | `media_<p>` | `sha` (content SHA-256, used for dedup and ROC) | upstreamHash (indexed, maps Recon's hash to the content sha), phash64, pdq (32 B PDQ hash, S-PDQ, 4c), sceneCode (S-IMG codes on PDQ-cluster representatives only), pdqClusterRep (sha of the representative), novelty (distance to the nearest S-IMG centroid at ingest, a structured scalar detectors filter on), ocrText (deterministically normalized identifiers extracted by PP-OCRv5, with bbox and confidence, provenance `ocr`), csamHashHit (BOOLEAN, set before any vision model runs, 5.7), mime, w, h, firstSeen, faceCount, tattooCount |
| `Face` | `media_<p>` | `fc:<imageSha>:<idx>:<algo>` | bbox, quality, yaw, ageBand and ageConfidence (ROC age module; never a scalar age, round 4), minorFlag, gallerySegment, gallerySlot (envelope-encrypted, 5.6), templateSha, clusterKey, representative (BOOLEAN), faceCode (S-FACE PQ-64 posting code and int8 AuraFace-v1 512-d rerank vector, envelope-encrypted, representatives only, 4c), state (staged, searchable, tombstoned) |
| `Tattoo` | `media_<p>` | same pattern | ROC tattoo template location, tattooCode (S-TATTOO, 4c) |
| `AuditEntry`, `ChainHead`, `OutboxEvent`, `OutboxCounter` | every database | per-database `seq` | section 5 |

The upstream sub-nodes are inlined where they are 1:1 with a Record: Description, RedLight metadata and Shanenet flags. `Review` stays a vertex because it is rare.

### 2.2 Edges

Every edge type is a registered LinkType with two-sided names (2.7): `HAS_PHONE` reads as "uses phone" from the Record side and "appears on record" from the Phone side, `MEMBER_OF` as "resolved into" and "has member", `TARGETS` as "targets" and "targeted by", so the link-analysis UI can label both directions without a lookup table. Registry edges that an analyst can see carry their own markings, independent of their endpoints (Gotham's `portionMarkings` on `create-object-link`; owner question 17).

**Hard edges** come deterministically from canonical keys.
- **Partition-local:** `HAS_PHONE`, `HAS_EMAIL`, `HAS_HANDLE`, `HAS_NAME`, `HAS_BUSINESS`. These are lightweight edges (`LightEdgeRID`, no edge record) from Record to local identifier vertices. Image hashes use the BY ITEM index on `Record.imageHashes`, so no local vertex is needed. Place uses the `placeId` property and the (placeId, tsPosted) composite.
- **Registry:** `CO_OCCURS` (Identifier to Identifier, aggregated). Properties: weight (record count), firstSeen, lastSeen (recency order for typed expansion, 4b). Created when two shared identifiers appear on the same record; reposts only raise the weight.
- **Registry:** `MEMBER_OF` (shared Identifier or FaceCluster to Identity). Properties: decisionId, confidence, and the engine-owned `_txFrom` stamp. **Versioned type** (round 7, `ALTER TYPE MEMBER_OF VERSIONED true`; a regular edge type, since lightweight edges have no record to version): a membership that ends is deleted, the engine copies its image with `out`, `in` and `txTo` into `_VersionHistory`, and `history.edges(identity, t)` returns the memberships as of `t`. No `validFrom` or `validTo` property and no ended edges in the live edge list (2.6).
- **Registry:** `TARGETS` (Target to a shared Identifier or Identity) and `SUPERSEDED_BY` (Identity to Identity). Many target identifiers, often the anchor itself, are singletons that exist only in `rec_<p>`, and an edge cannot cross databases, so deconfliction uses `Target.identifierKeys[]`, not `TARGETS`.

**Soft edges** (registry) are regular edges with properties.
- **Types:** `SIMILAR_STYLE`, `SIMILAR_FACE`, `NAME_LIKE`, `CO_LOCATED`, `ANALYST_LINK`. Once accepted, a soft link is also written as its own `ACCEPTED_LINK` edge type, because `GraphAnalyticalViewBuilder` filters only by vertex and edge type, not by property (2.6).
- **Properties:** confidence (calibrated), rawScore, method (`style24@v2`, `roc-021@3.17`, `jw+dm@v1`), threshold, evidence (record or face keys), createdAt, createdBy, opContext, state (proposed, accepted, rejected), decisionId.
- **Degree cap:** top-k = 10 per vertex.
- **Never automatic.** Soft edges never feed the automatic union-find. They become identity links only through a ResolutionDecision, made by a thresholded rule or an analyst's acceptance. Rejected edges are kept so recomputation never re-creates them.
- **Face thresholds** come from ROC's calibrated FMR table, never a raw cosine.

### 2.3 Canonical keys (`spork-keys`)

Recon's normalization rules move into one shared Java library, `spork-keys`. Ingest and the API both use it, and a TypeScript port ships with it. Recon has no single normalization today: at least six phone normalizations disagree (`engine.ts` canonPhone and normPhone, `ops/board.ts` normalizeAnchor, `ops/dossier.ts` identKey, `ops/feed.ts` phoneKey, `compare.ts` normPhone), and the board normalizes business names differently from cluster and dossier. So:
- Golden vectors are written from each Recon use site (`engine.ts`, `ops/board.ts`, `ops/dossier.ts`, `ops/feed.ts`, `ops/audit.ts`, `compare.ts`), with the expected SPORK key and Recon's current output side by side.
- Target ids are never recomputed with `spork-keys` (2.1).
- Where the canonical key changes a Recon result, the change is an explicit decision accepted through shadow-diff (WS4). The first one: whether a pivot on a 7-digit number matches inferred `ph:<10>` keys. Recon's `Engine.pivot` and `search` do not infer an area code today, so the default is to match the 7-digit raw value plus inferred keys from the same city only, flagged in the response.

The rules:
- **Phones:** last 10 digits. A 7-digit number inherits the posting city's area code and gets `inferredAreaCode=true`.
- **Emails and handles:** lowercased, leading `@` stripped.
- **Business names:** lowercase alphanumeric.
- **Image hashes:** the upstream string, exact match, never truncated.
- **Names:** NFKD, lowercased, letters only.

Keys are the only way databases refer to each other.

### 2.4 The registry rule

An identifier becomes a registry vertex only once it links **2 or more records**. Identifiers seen once stay local to their period database.

**Promotion.** When the second record arrives, the ingest saga does the following idempotently:
1. Creates the registry vertex.
2. Reads the first record's other identifiers from its partition by key.
3. Adds CO_OCCURS edges, dayCells, periodMask bits and the union-find update.

**Why the rule matters.** Singleton image hashes (most of the 1.5 distinct images per record) stay out of the only database that cannot be partitioned. That keeps `identity` at 263 B per record instead of a RecordRef per record (462 GB at 3x in the base proposal).

**Hub suppression and stop flags.**
- An identifier with recordCount over 2,000 across more than 25 cities gets `hub=true`.
- Identifiers on a curated stop list (agency phones, stock images) get `stop=true`.
- Both are excluded only from automatic expansion: component unions, RID sibling expansion and CO_OCCURS growth. Recon never suppresses hubs.
- **Per-token fetch cap (Recon parity).** Recon caps every source token at `RECON_FETCH_LIMIT`: 300 by default, at most 2,000, newest first (`server.ts` `/api/signal`, `api-source.ts` materializeMany, `ops/dossier.ts`, `resolve-targets.ts`, `missions/routes.ts`). `/intake`, `/dossier:footprint`, watch scans and mission targeting take the same per-token `limit` (default 300, max 2,000, newest first) and return `truncated` and `totalAtLeast` per token. Only an explicit `/pivot` pages a full footprint, with a `hub` flag and a caveat. The cap is part of Recon parity in the shadow-diff harness, and it keeps Recon's quadratic set analyses (geoAnalyze convergence, authorship pairs) bounded on hub identifiers.
- Recon's dossier caps (200 records, 120 nodes, 360 edges) are display caps. Dossier analysis runs over the capped re-resolved footprint (up to 25 tokens x the per-token limit), as Recon does.

### 2.5 Upstream OrientDB RID compatibility

- Every migrated Record stores `upstreamRid = clusterId << 40 | clusterPos` as a LONG with a UNIQUE index per partition, plus `upstreamClass` and `ridEpoch` (incremented if upstream is ever re-exported and its RIDs move). The literal `"168:8"` is rebuilt on output.
- `upstreamRid` does not encode the period, so the `recdir` database maps it to its period (70 B per record, 1.2). Intake recognizes `^\d+:\d+$` as a legacy RID and does one `recdir` lookup plus one partition lookup (4 cold page reads, 56 KB: two 4 KB HASH bucket pages, a 16 KB `recdir` page and a 32 KB Record page, about 0.4 ms), instead of probing 12 partitions at 3x.
- `record_id` is assigned by SPORK with the period id in its high bits, so it needs no directory. Analyst-imported records (Recon's `/api/ingest`, arbitrary non-RID ids) get `source='analyst-import'`, a `record_id`, the analyst's id in `importedId` with a (tenant, importedId) index, provenance and an audit entry, and no `upstreamRid`. Round 4: `/ingest` returns the importedId to `record_id` map, hydrate and intake accept `imp:<id>`, and intake checks `importedId` before `recdir` for a RID-shaped token (`123:45` may be an analyst's own id), so Recon's sets, board sources and `get_record` keep resolving the ids the analyst typed.
- `POST /records:hydrate` (7.2) reads per id, cold: `recdir` HASH bucket page + `recdir` document (only for an upstream RID; a `record_id` carries its period), then the partition's `upstreamRid` or `record_id` HASH bucket page + the Record page. That is 4 reads per upstream RID and 2 per `record_id`, because city, country and the display identifier arrays are on Record (2.1): no `HAS_*` edge chunk, local vertex or Place read. Place is read only when lat/lon are missing, through an in-heap Place cache. ArcadeDB loads pages synchronously in the calling thread, so the plugin sorts probes by page and runs both phases on a dedicated bounded `spork-hydrate` pool of 32 threads (caller-runs on saturation, Micrometer metrics, per the engine-concurrency rules). A call takes at most 5,000 ids (round 4; Recon pages larger sets), so one call moves at most 5,000 x 56 KB = 280 MB, 1.2% of the page cache.
- **Bytes, not just reads.** The engine reads whole pages, so the page sizes of 0.4 apply: 4 KB HASH bucket pages on the fixed-width keys, a 16 KB `recdir` page, a 32 KB Record page. A 2,000-id hydrate by upstream RID is about 8,000 reads and 112 MB: about 31 ms at 32-way on read latency and 17 to 22 ms on bandwidth (5 to 6.5 GB/s), so about 35 ms of I/O plus 6 ms of CPU (`02-retrieval-speed.md` P9b: 45 / 120 ms cold p50 / p99), 72 MB and about 26 ms by `record_id`, 80 and 40 MB after M20; against 0.8 s serial. On the stock 256 KB index and 64 KB bucket pages the same hydrate would move 640 KB per upstream RID, 1.3 GB in all: 0.2 to 0.26 s of transfer, and 5.7% of a 23 GB page cache per call.
- **No series walk (round 6).** The round-4 version of this bullet budgeted a resident walk of every compacted series (519 for `recdir` today, 1,609 at 3x at 16 KB) and 1.2 to 3.8 GB of bloom pages per probe; with HASH point-lookup indexes a probe is page 0, one directory page and one bucket page (3 cached touches, 1 cold read, 2 with an overflow page), the resident directories total under 0.25 GB, and there is no per-series CPU (0.4). P0a measures reads, bytes, overflow-chain length and page-cache churn per probe under a concurrent cold 2,000-id load; the full compaction at seal and after admission now covers the sealed partition's LSM composites (0.4), not its point lookups.
- SPORK RIDs are internal and never reach Recon. Recon sees `upstreamRid`, `record_id` and canonical keys only.
- Incremental sync from upstream uses idempotent upserts keyed on `upstreamRid` and `record_id`, with `ts_date_collected` as the change cursor and a nightly reconciliation by `upstreamRid`.

### 2.6 Reversible entity resolution

**Nothing is destroyed.**
- **Merge:** creates a new Identity with a fresh key, the **winner key** (Gotham's `winnerObjectPrimaryKey`, round 4): every write made after the merge (notes, assertions, memberships, soft-edge acceptances) attaches to the winner, never to the merged inputs, so a later split returns each input with its own pre-merge history intact and the post-merge writes stay with the winner for an analyst to reassign. The decision records `canonicalKey` (the input the UI shows as primary), `winnerKey` and the flattened `otherKeys[]` of recursive merges. It **deletes** the old `MEMBER_OF` edges, creates new ones onto the winner, sets the inputs' `status=superseded`, and links a ResolutionDecision listing them all, in one transaction (round 7; round 6 set `validTo` on the old edges and kept them).
- **Split:** deletes the chosen memberships and creates new ones the same way; the winner is superseded if nothing remains in it.
- **Undo:** a decision with `kind=unlink, supersedes=X` that recreates the earlier memberships (read from `history.edges(identity, t_X)`) and reactivates the earlier Identities.
- Every decision is audited in the same transaction.

**Nothing is destroyed because the engine versions it (round 7, `08-native-versioning-design.md`).** `Identity` and `MEMBER_OF` are system-versioned types (`ALTER TYPE Identity VERSIONED true`, `ALTER TYPE MEMBER_OF VERSIONED true`; `DocumentType.setVersioned` in Java, persisted in `schema.json` and inherited by subtypes). Every record of a versioned type carries one engine-stamped `_txFrom` (`LONG`, epoch microseconds, one stamp per transaction, strictly increasing per database; an update that changes it is refused). On the first update or delete of such a record in a transaction the engine copies its committed image byte for byte into the internal `_VersionHistory` document type `{rid, txFrom, txTo, out, in, data}` in the same transaction (indexed `NOTUNIQUE` on `rid`, `out` and `in`, nulls skipped), so rollback discards the history entry with the rest and HA replicates it with the transaction's pages. A deleted edge keeps its history even when its endpoint vertex is deleted. The store refuses `UPDATE` and `DELETE` from clients; only `history.purge(rid)` removes entries, which the deletion saga calls for a deleted subject's Identities and memberships (5.5 step 4). Transaction time only: application (valid) time stays a property where a type needs one.

**Views.**
- **The current view is the plain edge list**: `MEMBER_OF` out of a shared identifier, or into an Identity, with no predicate. Round 6 kept ended edges and made every traversal filter `validTo IS NULL`, so a 500-record identifier merged ten times walked 11 edges to find 1 and every k-hop count, typed expand, common-neighbour and induced-subgraph procedure (4b.2) paid the dead edges and the filter; now they read live edges only, which is a retrieval-speed win on every registry traversal and removes the as-of filter from every query author's hands.
- **The as-of view is `history.edges(identity, t [, direction [, edgeType]])`**: the Identity's current edges that already existed at `t` plus the superseded or deleted edges valid at `t`, from one edge-list read plus one `in` index lookup. `history.asOf(identity, t)` gives the Identity's own state at `t` (kind, status, confidence) and `history.of(identity)` every version oldest first with `@txFrom`, `@txTo` and `@current`. They are SQL functions, callable from Cypher (`RETURN history.edges(n, $t)`) and from Java (`RecordVersioning.of(db)`), and the plugin's `graph.history` wraps them under the OC and markings predicate (4b.2 R13).
- **`ResolutionDecision` is the why record, not a version store**: rule and version, evidence, actor, opContext, rationale, `supersedes`, `revertedBy`, the winner key. The what and when of every membership and Identity state come from the engine's history; the decision explains them and is what an analyst reads before an undo.
- Versioning is enabled at the Q2 engine merge (E25, 3.6): Q1 runs the same delete-and-recreate rule on the stock engine with the ResolutionDecision chain (inputs[], outputs[]) as the only as-of source, and records written before `VERSIONED true` are treated as valid since the beginning. History entries are not yet checked against the reader's markings (E13 covers the history store when it lands); until then only the plugin's `graph.history` route exposes them (5.3).
- Targets attach to identifier keys (`anchorKey`, `identifierKeys[]`), so they never move on merge or split.

**`componentId` is derived data, labeled the "network" view.**
- It is updated incrementally at ingest with a streaming union-find over non-hub, non-stop shared identifiers.
- It is rebuilt every night on n4 by a streaming union-find in the plugin: one scan of the shared identifier and Identity vertices in bucket order (skipping hub and stop vertices) fills a sorted RID `long[]`, then one scan of `CO_OCCURS` and `ACCEPTED_LINK` edges unions the endpoints by binary search into a parent `int[]`. That is 10.2 GB at 3x, inside n4's 16 GB OLAP slice (1.4). A `GraphAnalyticalView` is not used: its CSR is about 26.6 GB steady at 3x and over 36 GB at build peak (two offset arrays per edge type and direction, BFS reorder permutations). Splits and deletions cannot be undone incrementally, which is why the nightly rebuild is needed.
- **No named GAV anywhere.** A named view is saved in the replicated schema (`GraphAnalyticalViewPersistence`, extension `graphAnalyticalViews`) and rebuilt by every node when it opens the database (`LocalDatabase` calls `restoreAll`), so it would also build on n1 to n3. Any GAV the plugin builds (set-scoped graph endpoints) is unnamed, built only on the node serving the call, and released after it.
- Only componentIds that changed are written back, in throttled batches. A full rewrite of about 549M vertices at 3x would flood the Raft log.
- It is **not** what Recon's link clusters, graph metrics or dossier use. Those are computed over the record-level identifier lists of a hydrated set, including singletons, with the caller's `linkOn` profile and `fuzzy` style unions (4, Graph algorithms). The global component is a separate, labeled network view.

**Records with only singleton identifiers** are their own component. Their component is defined, and needs no registry entry.

### 2.7 Intel-platform ontology contract

The link-analysis platform the owner will build on SPORK (Kaseware "PATH", Palantir Gotham/Foundry, Maltego class) needs the data layer to be an ontology, not a schema: typed objects, typed properties, typed links with a name for each direction, mandatory markings, provenance on asserted values, immutable set algebra, and reversible decisions (`research/intel-platform-data-layer.md` section 4). SPORK's 2.1 to 2.6 already fit; this section states the contract that the plugin exposes and that every new type must satisfy. It is schema data in `identity`, replicated, and read by the plugin's query builders, so no engine change is needed.

**ObjectType** `{apiName, title, primaryKeyProperty, titleProperty, interfaces[], prominent, defaultMarkings[], retentionClass}`. Every 2.1 vertex type is one. The abstract `Identifier` is an **interface** (Foundry's polymorphic shape): `Phone`, `Email`, `Handle`, `Name`, `Business`, `ImageHash` and `Url` implement it, so a search-around over "any identifier" is one call. `primaryKeyProperty` is the canonical key (2.3) or the SPORK-assigned id, never a RID.

**PropertyType** `{objectType, apiName, baseType (string, int, long, double, bool, date, timestamp, geopoint, geoshape, vector, binaryRef, array, struct), searchable (exact, text, prefix, range, geo, knn), markings[], pii, displayMask}`. A property can carry a marking its object does not (Foundry property-level markings): `Face.gallerySlot` and `Record.styleVec` are examples of properties with tighter markings than their objects. `searchable=knn` names the 4c space.

**Provenance** is a parallel struct `_prov {sourceId, collectedAt, assertedBy, confidence}` on the few properties analysts can assert, and on every Record (13 B, 0.4). Ingested values carry `sourceId` = upstream source and `assertedBy` = `system:ingest`. **Value history is the engine's (round 7):** an analyst assertion on a versioned object (`Identity`) updates the property and its `_prov` in place, the engine copies the superseded image into `_VersionHistory`, and `history.of(id)` replays every value with who asserted it (`_prov.assertedBy`), from where (`_prov.sourceId`) and when (`@txFrom`, `@txTo`); no overlay vertex is read on the hot path and no `supersedes` chain is walked. `_prov` still records who and from where, the engine records when. Only subjects whose types are not versioned (`Record` fields, shared identifier display values, rewritten by ingest and sync) keep a narrowed `Assertion` document beside the ingested value (2.1), itself versioned so corrections replay the same way. Soft edges already carry `method`, `evidence[]`, `createdBy`, `opContext` (2.2), which is the same struct under older names.

**LinkType** `{apiName, sideA {objectType, apiName, display}, sideB {objectType, apiName, display}, cardinality (1:1, 1:N, N:M), directed, propertyTypes[] (confidence, weight, firstSeen, lastSeen, method, evidence[], state proposed|accepted|rejected, decisionId, markings[]), transformId}`. Two-sided naming is copied from Foundry so the UI labels both directions. SPORK keeps real edge properties, a strict superset of Foundry (which models link attributes as intermediary objects). `transformId` names the Maltego-style server procedure (4b) that materializes the link type on demand.

**Markings** are mandatory controls (a reader must hold every marking on an object, property value and link; roles are discretionary and come second), inherit from case and source, can hide existence, and removing one is an audited "expand access" write. The engine has none, so the plugin's query builders apply them as predicates and projection masks now (5.3), and an engine row and property filter in `SelectExecutor` and `FetchFromTypeExecutionStep` is E13 (Q3, train 3). The marking model itself (flat labels with intersection semantics, or classification plus compartments) is owner question 16.

**ObjectSet** is the immutable algebra `base(type) | static(ids[]) | ref(savedId) | filter(set, where) | union | intersect | subtract | searchAround(set, linkType, side, limit) | knn(set, space, probe, k)`, cacheable by its definition hash, nested at most 3 search-arounds deep (Foundry's chained-hop limit). Its service and its mapping to Recon compare are in 4b.

**Decision** is 2.6: merge, split, link and unlink with `supersedes` and `revertedBy` on the decision, memberships deleted and recreated under engine versioning (no `validFrom` or `validTo`; `history.edges` is the as-of view), and the winner key that owns post-merge writes.

**Writes** are typed actions: assert link (idempotent on (a, b, type, source)), retract link (soft, `state=rejected`), merge and split through a Decision, annotate (note, bookmark colour, tag), mark and unmark (audited), save set and watch, and a multi-object action with submission criteria whose side effect is an Outbox event. Every write appends an audit entry in the same transaction (5.2). Each has a route (round 5): `POST /links` and `DELETE /links/{id}`, `POST /resolution/decide`, `POST /assertions`, `POST /annotations`, `POST` and `DELETE /markings`, `POST /sets/save` and `/watches`, `POST /actions` (7.2), built in WS11b (Q2). Annotations are per object, tenant and case scoped, and the `bookmark` colour is what Maltego's `bookmarked` machine filter reads.

**Caps**, pending owner question 18: typed expand 100 per link type, page 1,000, facet topN 1,000 with an `approximate` flag past 1,000 distinct values, 10,000 groups with `excludedItems`, 3 chained hops, 2,000 nodes for an induced subgraph, 10,000 reached nodes for k-hop counts. Every read returns `truncated` and `totalAtLeast` instead of timing out (the Palantir pattern: budgets are enforced by caps, not by timeouts).

**Export** is a markings-filtered subgraph (nodes, edges, provenance) to JSON, GraphML and GraphSON through the existing `integration` exporter, plus CSV (`nodes.csv`, `edges.csv`; new, the exporter has no CSV today), with the redaction rules of 5.4 applied (no ad text, no images).

---

## 3. Replication strategy

### 3.1 Quorum, failure behaviour and consistency

| Event | Effect |
|---|---|
| n4 down or resyncing | No effect on writes (after E4; in Q1 the 4-voter quorum still holds with 3). Read capacity drops by 25%. |
| One voter down | Writes continue (2 of 3). Promote n4 if the outage will be long. |
| Two voters down | Writes stop, and so do audit appends. Followers serve only unaudited `eventual` reads (search rows, board GET; audit pages need a linearizable read and wait for a leader). Audited endpoints (intake, dossier, feed, face, text access, package) return 503 and fail closed, as Recon does when its audit log is not writable. Recon shows a read-only banner. |
| Leader change | Ratis re-elects within the `HA_ELECTION_TIMEOUT_*` window, 5 to 10 s by default. P0c measures whether lower values stay free of false elections under ingest before E0 lowers them. Ingest and job workers retry with idempotency keys (`HA_IDEMPOTENCY_CACHE_*`). Queue state is replicated data, so nothing is lost. |

**Routing.**
- Interactive reads go to n2 and n3 (weight 3) and n1 (weight 1). Analytics and dossier go to n4.
- Governed plugin writes are pinned to the leader by the gateway. An embedded commit on a follower is not forwarded: it is submitted to Raft from the follower, and on hot pages (`ChainHead`, the Outbox counter) it would fail with `ReplicatedPageConflictException`. So the plugin refuses follower-originated commits for chain-bearing types. `LeaderForwardContext` is only a loop-prevention marker for redirected `/api/v1/command` calls.
- Audited reads served on a follower append their audit entry on the leader through an internal `spork-recon` route (`POST /internal/audit/append`, mTLS, the OC in the body) before results are released. This works in Q1 without E6.
- A node stops reporting ready when it lags more than `SERVER_READINESS_HA_MAX_LAG` entries, which requires `SERVER_READINESS_REQUIRES_HA=true` (default false, set in E0). After E5, readiness is per index, so a node rebuilding vectors still serves everything else.
- With one Raft group, the `X-ArcadeDB-Commit-Index` bookmark is global, so read-your-writes works across databases. The consistency headers are parsed only by `DatabaseAbstractHandler`, so `spork-recon` routes call `HAReplicatedDatabase.setReadConsistencyContext` and emit the bookmark header themselves (WS3).

| Recon call | Node | Consistency |
|---|---|---|
| Search, hydrate, intake, pivot, partial, group-by, timeline, live feed, style and S1 similarity, set-scoped geo and graph analysis (their hydrate phase needs the resident bloom set, 1.4), ObjectSet algebra | n2/n3 (n1) | `eventual`; responses carry `asOfCommitIndex`. **Audit is by data class, not by endpoint (round 4):** every response that carries identifier footprints or record projections appends one batched audit entry per request on the leader first (action by route, one Merkle root over the returned record keys) and passes the same purpose gate as `/intake` (5.1, 5.2). |
| Any read right after this analyst's own write (add target, confirm link, intake after submit) | n2/n3 | `read_your_writes` with `X-ArcadeDB-Read-After: <commit index>` |
| Board get-for-edit and PATCH (OCC, 409 on stale revision) | leader | linearizable, in a transaction |
| Evidence writes, resolution decisions, deletion orders | leader | linearizable |
| Audit pages and verify, legal-hold checks before delete | any | `linearizable` |
| Dossier footprint (Recon assembles the dossier, 7.1), the nightly components, communities, corpus-wide k-hop counts over the registry GAV, exports | n4 | `eventual`; dossier footprint and exports audited on the leader first |
| Face and tattoo search | roc-agents on all nodes, results hydrated on n2/n3 | `eventual`, with a `partial` flag if a gallery quarter is reloading |
| Change feed | any | ordered by per-database `seq` with a merged cursor |

### 3.2 Bounding resync headroom

**What the code does today (verified in source).**
- `SnapshotInstaller` downloads into `<db>/.snapshot-new` while the live database stays open, then swaps the live files to `.snapshot-backup`. The peak extra space is one copy of that database.
- `DatabaseReconciler` and the single-worker `ArcadeStateMachine.snapshotInstallExecutor` install one database at a time. So peak headroom is the largest database.
- `SnapshotInstaller.checkUsableSpace` (issue #7037) already refuses an install whose inflated size, plus the allocation reserve, does not fit. What is missing is an alert before that refusal happens (E14).
- A Raft-level InstallSnapshot (a follower behind the purged log) refreshes **every** local database, changed or not: `DatabaseReconciler.classifyReconcile` puts every database the leader holds into `toRefresh`, and each refresh is a full `SnapshotInstaller.install`. So the 120 GB cap bounds peak headroom, not resync volume or time.
- **The reconcile listing is a full hash of the leader.** `reconcileDatabasesFromLeader` first fetches the leader's database listing (`fetchBootstrapState`, timeout `HA_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 120 s). The leader answers by computing `BootstrapFingerprint.computeSettled` for every database on each request, a SHA-256 over every data file in 64 KB reads with no cache (`PostBootstrapStateHandler`, `ArcadeStateMachine.localBootstrapState`). At 1,005 GB that takes 8 to 17 minutes, so on stock settings the fetch always times out. An empty follower then fails the install (`failInstallWhenNoLocalDatabases`, issue #4799) and Ratis retries, each retry starting another full hash on the leader: a replacement node never acquires anything. A follower with local databases falls back to the legacy refresh-everything path. E0 raises the timeout (1.1) and E2 removes the fingerprints from the listing (3.6).
- The reconcile plan is sorted alphabetically and run serially, and the Raft applied index is recorded only after the whole reconcile returns, so a node is not caught up for any database until all are installed.
- Per-database failure isolation and give-up after 3 attempts already exist. A leader-driven per-database resync also exists (`forceResyncStalledReplica`, `POST /api/v1/cluster/resync/{db}`).

**Design rules.**
1. **Cap every period database at 120 GB.** Boundaries are chosen by projected size, not by calendar.
2. **`identity` is the only database that grows without bound** (89 GB mid today, 322 GB at 3x including 43 GB of version history, round 7; it crosses 120 GB about month 3.5, 2027-02, at mid, so E1 is the first Q2 item). Up to 120 GB it installs normally. Above that, it uses **cold install (E1)**, built on the existing per-database resync path: the node retires its local copy first and then downloads. This is allowed only when at least 2 other replicas of that database report healthy, so a node never discards what might be the last good copy. While the install runs, that node's readiness excludes the query types that need `identity` (E5).
   - If E1 slips: split shared ImageHash identifiers into `identity_img`. Shared image hashes are 0.375 of the 0.61 shared identifiers per record, so `identity` drops to about 900M x (0.235 x 270 + 42.5 + 20 + 36) / 1e9 + 42.6 = 188 GB at 3x, 231 GB with the version history (whose MEMBER_OF share stays with the Identities, round 7). That is still above 120 GB, so the split only delays the need for E1 from month 3.5 to about month 9 at mid. Components then run over two in-process views.
3. **One single filesystem for the whole SPORK data root.** A new-database acquire stages into `databases/.acquire-<name>/` and publishes with `Files.move(staging, dbPath, ATOMIC_MOVE)` (`SnapshotInstaller`, around lines 188 to 191 and 836 to 887). That rename cannot cross filesystems.
   - So a database directory on a separate mount breaks node replacement. This was the fatal flaw in the single-replicated-cluster proposal's media mount.
   - Disks therefore grow by LVM extension of the one volume (0.6), never by new mounts.
4. **Acquisition order on a replacement node** (after E5): `ops`, `recdir`, `identity`, hot `rec_*` and `txt_*`, recent `media_*`, then warm, with per-database readiness while the rest install. Until E5 the order is alphabetical and the node serves nothing until every database is installed, so replacement downtime is the full transfer.
5. **E2 skips databases that are already current.** During reconcile, a database whose persisted per-database applied index (`<databaseDirectory>/.raft/applied-index`) and lastTxId (plus a database uuid or epoch, so a re-created database never matches) equal the leader's report is not refreshed. Full-content fingerprints are not used: `BootstrapFingerprint` is documented as valid only at the cold-start boundary, not as a runtime consistency check, and it would read 3.0 TB end to end on both sides at 3x. `LeaderDatabaseQuery.DatabaseInfo` carries only name and lastTxId today, so E2 also changes the listing to (name, lastTxId, appliedIndex, uuid) with no hashing. Only databases that changed in the purged range are downloaded. Until E2, every log-gap resync costs a full-node transfer (3,063 GB at mid 3x) plus the leader hash pass.
6. **Raft log retention.** `HA_LOG_PURGE_GAP` is only the minimum distance between purges: with `HA_LOG_PURGE_UPTO_SNAPSHOT=true` and a snapshot every 5 minutes, Ratis purges to the latest snapshot and retention falls to about 5 minutes. E0.5 adds `HA_LOG_PURGE_PRESERVATION_ENTRIES`, wired to Ratis `purgePreservationLogNum`, sized to about 24 h of entries in P0, and then the setting returns to true. Before E0.5, `HA_LOG_PURGE_UPTO_SNAPSHOT=false` is set permanently from day 1 (it is read only at Ratis startup, so it cannot be toggled per window without rolling restarts). With it false, Ratis purges only up to min(snapshot index, every peer's commit index), so a follower down for maintenance catches up from the log. The same rule means a dead peer that is still a member pins the leader's log indefinitely, and the free-space guard does not help: it only forces a snapshot, which cannot purge past that minimum. Round 4: the log carries ingest deltas (15 to 25 GB/day) plus incremental compactions (about 8 GB/day) plus any scheduled full compaction (up to 27 GB in a day), so the 120 GB line lasts 2.0 days with a full compaction in the window and 3.6 to 4.8 days at 25 to 33 GB/day without one. **Operational rules:** the plugin suspends scheduled full compactions while any peer is down (the compaction schedule in `ops` checks cluster health), so the window is 3.6 to 4.8 days; remove a dead peer (`DeletePeerHandler`) before the Raft log reaches 80 GB or within 48 h, whichever is first, with an alert on Raft log bytes at 50 GB and a page at 80 GB. After that a full log volume makes Ratis mark the log failed and the leader rejects every append.

### 3.3 Failover and node replacement

1. Remove the dead peer (`DeletePeerHandler`), provision the host with LUKS and the single LVM volume, and add the peer (`PostAddPeerHandler`).
2. **Databases.** Auto-acquire first fetches the leader's listing, which hashes every database on the leader (3.2): 8 to 17 minutes today, 26 to 52 minutes at mid 3x, and only possible with `HA_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT_MS` raised in E0 (on the default 120 s, an empty replacement never acquires anything). It then pulls every database (alphabetically until E5, in priority order after). At mid 3x, 3,149 GB at 0.6 to 1.0 GB/s takes 52 to 87 minutes of transfer; with the hash pass, verification and reopen, about 4 to 6.5 hours. The S1 postings travel inside the partition databases, so they need no separate step. P0c drills replacement of an empty node at full size. A new peer starts with an empty Raft log, so it always takes this path today: `HA_BOOTSTRAP_FROM_LOCAL_DATABASE` adopts local files only at first cluster formation. Faster path after W3e (Q3, ha-raft): a restored backup records its Raft index, and E2's reconcile skip accepts it when the leader still holds log entries past that index.
3. **ROC segments.** The `roc-agent` pulls all segments listed in the manifest from peers or the object store: 316 GB including tattoos, 5 to 9 minutes. It then loads its quarter into RAM.
4. **Vector graphs.** These are rebuilt locally because `SnapshotHttpHandler` deliberately does not ship `.vecgraph` files (issue #9758). Until S-STYLE moves onto the IVF index in Q2 (round 9; after which only the case galleries have graphs), at 3x that would be 100M style vectors / 6.25M per bucket = 16 buckets x 10 to 20 minutes = 2.7 to 5.3 hours on one permit. The node reports `vector:not-ready` until the `LSMVectorIndexGraphManifest` fingerprint is current. E3 ships graph files with the snapshot and removes this step.

### 3.4 Vector graphs on every node

- Vector indexes exist only in hot `txt_*` partitions, and only until S-STYLE moves onto the `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` index in Q2 (1.4, round 9); after that the hot `txt_<p>` carry no jvector graph and this section applies to the case galleries alone.
- Vectors go into **month buckets**, with hash sub-buckets so that no bucket holds more than 8M vectors. At 3x pace, 835K x 30 x 0.5 = 12.5M unique texts per month means 2 sub-buckets of 6.25M each. Sealed months are never rebuilt, and only the current month churns.
- `VECTOR_INDEX_MAX_CONCURRENT_REBUILDS=1` per JVM (the compiled-in default, stated for explicitness). Every index is created with `storeVectorsInGraph=true` and an explicit `searchCacheSize` (1.4).
- Rebuilds are triggered inside the engine on each node (mutation thresholds, an inactivity timeout, a lazy build on the first search after a snapshot ships the graph empty), and the engine has no hook for an external gate. E5 adds a rebuild-admission callback that consults a lease in `ops`, so at most 2 voters rebuild the same bucket at once. Until then the plugin only routes queries away from a node whose bucket is rebuilding.
- When a partition ages from hot to warm, the offline rewrite (0.4) leaves the vector index out. Exact rescoring uses the float32 `styleVec` on Record.

### 3.5 Bulk backfills

- **Backfills above 10M records** are built **offline on n4** with `GraphBatch` as a new period group (`rec_<p>`, `txt_<p>`, `media_<p>`), always with `withWAL(true)` (round 5: WAL off retains about 8 KB of heap per record and fills the 12 GB build slice at 1.5M records, 1.4; the measured WAL-on rate is 265K elements/s, 6.1). Each database is packaged as a full backup archive and admitted with the `ServerControlPlane` restore-database path on the leader (`BackupCoordinator` is only the per-database operation lock). In HA every replica pulls the restored files. The archive is served from an allowed URL: a presigned object-store URL, or a local file with `SERVER_RESTORE_IMPORT_ALLOW_LOCAL_URLS`.
- **Space on the leader:** the archive (up to about 60 GB compressed) plus the extracted database (up to 120 GB). Admissions take the `transient-slot` lease in `ops` (0.4) and never run while any node is resyncing or a backup is staging, so the leader's peak is the steady-state footprint + 180 GB, which at the ceiling is 1,440 + 180 = 1,620 GB, inside the 10% install reserve. The other followers pay one database of headroom at a time.
- **Space on n4 (the builder).** A mid period group is rec 120 + txt warm 60 (hot 72) + media 36 = 216 GB (hot 228), so building it whole on n4 at the ceiling would reach 1,656 GB (92%) before any archive and about 1,760 GB with archives. n4 therefore works one database at a time: build it, package it, upload the archive to the object store, delete the local build and archive, and only then let the leader admit it (n4 pulls its own replica copy like any follower). Each build is gated on n4 having at least 180 GB free above the steady state, and holds the `transient-slot` lease for the build and upload. n4's peak is then steady + max(build 120 + archive 60, admitted pull 120) = +180 GB, the same as the leader. **The build's source is a restore, not the live replica (round 4):** a second process cannot open a database the server holds, even READ_ONLY (`LocalDatabase.prepareRecovery` throws "needs recovery" when `database.lck` exists, and a READ_WRITE open takes an exclusive `tryLock`), so an aging rewrite restores the partition's latest backup archive into n4's scratch under the same lease and reads that. The slot therefore holds source + output + archive: for a `txt_<p>` at the period boundary, 72 + 60 + 30 = 162 GB, which fits; a `txt_<p>` at the 120 GB cap would need 120 + 101 + 50 = 271 GB and does not, which is why `txt_<p>` is capped at the `rec` period boundary (1.2).
- **Admission stalls apply on every follower.** A `ServerControlPlane` restore commits an `INSTALL_DATABASE_ENTRY` with forceSnapshot, so every follower pulls the database at once. The forced install runs synchronously on Ratis's single `StateMachineUpdater` thread (`ArcadeStateMachine.installLeaderCopy`), so each follower stops applying entries for every database until its copy lands. With `HA_SNAPSHOT_MAX_CONCURRENT=3` (1.1) the three followers pull at once and share the leader's link, so all finish at 3 x T: a 120 GB admission stalls every follower for 6 minutes at 1.0 GB/s aggregate and 10 minutes at 0.6 GB/s. With one slot the stall would be set by the retry schedule, not the link: refused followers retry together at 30, 90, 210 and 450 s (base 30 s, no jitter), so the second follower starts at 210 s and the last at 450 s, a stall of 9.5 to 10.8 minutes (4.2 to 4.6 minutes at 40 GB), which fails the gate at 0.6 GB/s by construction. With `SERVER_READINESS_REQUIRES_HA=true` (max lag 100 entries) n2, n3 and n4 all report not ready during the stall and interactive reads fall to n1 alone. Rules: admissions run in a declared off-peak window, Recon shows a degraded banner, and P0c measures the **last follower's** stall (not the transfer time) and the leader's aggregate rate with three concurrent pulls as a gate. If the stall exceeds 10 minutes, backfill and aging databases are admitted at 40 GB (more, smaller period databases, a stall of 2 to 3.3 minutes). A year-2 ha-raft item moves forced installs off the apply thread (3.6).
- **Hot-to-warm aging uses the same path, under an aging protocol.** Each sealed `txt_<p>` leaving the hot tier is rewritten on n4 without the vector index (and, if valve 1 is on, with the pruned FTS; after E17, without text) and admitted the same way. A sealed `txt_<p>` takes no inserts (new texts go to the current period), but the deletion saga keeps writing to it, and the build, upload and off-peak admission can take hours. `restore database` refuses a name already taken (`ServerControlPlane.restoreDatabase`), so the copy is admitted under a new name. The protocol:
  1. The plugin marks the partition `aging` in `ops` with the copy's lastTxId (the copy is the partition's latest backup archive, restored into n4's scratch under the `transient-slot` lease; the archive records the lastTxId it was taken at) and audits `partition.aging.start`. **The restored archive must carry the partition's S1 codes (round 5):** the plugin asserts that the archive's lastTxId postdates the partition's S1 backfill completion and codebook version recorded in `ops` (4c.4); if it does not (a `txt_<p>` sealed and archived before its S1 backfill whose re-archive has not run), the rewrite re-encodes S-TEXT from the stored codes of the live copy, read through the console-role plugin route, into the new database before step 5, or waits for the re-archive, and never switches to a copy that lacks the codes.
  2. While it is `aging`, the deletion saga still applies every order to the live `txt_<p>` (reads stay correct) and also records each order that touches it (TextBody deletes, holdCount and retentionClass changes) in an `AgingReplay` list in `ops`. Its `txt` acknowledgement and completion certificate for those orders wait.
  3. n4 builds, uploads and the leader admits the copy as `txt_<p>_w<n>` (n increments per rewrite).
  4. The plugin replays the recorded orders against `txt_<p>_w<n>` (idempotent, keyed by order id). From the start of the replay until step 5, new orders are applied to both databases.
  5. In one `ops` transaction the plugin switches its partition map (period p to `txt_<p>_w<n>`), audited as `partition.switch`; every node's plugin reloads the map from the Outbox event.
  6. After a 10-minute drain for in-flight queries, the old `txt_<p>` is dropped, audited as `partition.drop`.
  7. Only then does the saga write the `txt` acknowledgements and the completion certificates for the recorded orders.
- **The registry delta goes through Raft.** It is about 50M x 263 B = 13 GB per 50M records, applied in throttled batches. This keeps the bulk data itself out of the Raft log, which would otherwise grow by about 250 GB a day during a 50M-record backfill.
