- **No backups during admission.** Stock `AutoBackupSchedulerPlugin` and `BackupTask` have no gate other than `shouldRunOnThisServer()` (round 4), so before W3d the plugin disables the affected databases' backup schedules by configuration reload for the admission window and re-enables them after, and admissions are scheduled outside the daily backup window; disk safety is unaffected either way because archives go off node, only I/O contention is at stake. W3d's own scheduler checks the `transient-slot` lease.
- **No stale-follower recovery during the stall.** E0 sets `HA_STALE_FOLLOWER_RECOVERY_DURATION_MS=1800000` and workers batch MediaJob writes (1.1), because the stock 60 s window with a 10,000-entry threshold would turn every admission into a full-node resync of all three followers (3,149 GB each at mid 3x) with n1 serving reads alone. P0c asserts the absence of the "Persistent follower lag" log line during an admission.
- **P0 gate:** prove this admission path, including the follower apply stall. Fallback: throttled online ingest.

### 3.6 Engine work for replication (real classes, ordered by Recon impact)

| ID | Change | Classes | Size | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E0 | Configuration only: `HA_SNAPSHOT_MAX_CONCURRENT=3`, `HA_SNAPSHOT_INSTALL_RETRIES=6`, `HA_SNAPSHOT_INSTALL_RETRY_BASE_MS=30000`, `HA_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT_MS=3600000` (5,400,000 above 2 TB of databases), `HA_SNAPSHOT_COMPRESSION_LEVEL=0`, `HA_SERVER_ROLE=replica` on n4, `VECTOR_INDEX_MAX_CONCURRENT_REBUILDS=1` (already the default), `SERVER_READINESS_REQUIRES_HA=true`, `HA_LOG_PURGE_UPTO_SNAPSHOT=false` permanently until E0.5, `HA_STALE_FOLLOWER_RECOVERY_DURATION_MS=1800000` (round 4, 3.5); per database: `arcadedb.indexCompactionFullSeriesThreshold=0`, `arcadedb.indexCompactionFullDeadEntriesRatio=0`, `arcadedb.indexCompactionMinPagesSchedule=64` on `identity` and the current period (round 4 blocker, 0.4; round 6: `recdir` has only a HASH index, and `02-retrieval-speed.md` C5's schedule 4 and `fullSeriesThreshold=3` were rejected), `arcadedb.vectorIndex.searchCacheSize=500000` on every `txt_*`, `arcadedb.externalPropertyBucketDefaultPageSize=16384` on every `txt_<p>` before the EXTERNAL property exists; auto-backup directory on the off-node encrypted mount (KMS-held keys, 5.6), daily for `identity`, `ops`, `audit`, `recdir` and the current period, 7 archives, sealed databases off the schedule (0.4); monitoring without `?presence=true`; per index at creation: `storeVectorsInGraph=true`; schema v1 page sizes through SQL `CREATE TYPE ... PAGESIZE` and the HASH defaults, Java builders only for FULL_TEXT and GEOSPATIAL at 64 KB (0.4, round 6) | `GlobalConfiguration`, `AutoBackupConfig`, database settings, index metadata | 0 | Q1 |
| E0.5 | Raft log preservation: new key `HA_LOG_PURGE_PRESERVATION_ENTRIES` wired to `RaftServerConfigKeys.Log.setPurgePreservationLogNum`, sized to about 24 h of entries; `HA_LOG_PURGE_UPTO_SNAPSHOT` then returns to true (one rolling restart) | `ha-raft/.../RaftPropertiesBuilder`, `GlobalConfiguration` | 0.5 ew | Q2 |
| E1 | Cold-install mode for databases above a size threshold (new key, for example `HA_SNAPSHOT_COLD_INSTALL_MIN_BYTES`), built on the existing per-database resync path (`RaftHAServer.forceResyncStalledReplica`, `PostResyncDatabaseHandler`). Retire the live copy to the reserved `.dropped-` prefix, delete it, then install. Precondition: 2 or more other healthy replicas. | `ha-raft/.../SnapshotInstaller`, `DatabaseReconciler`, `RaftClusterStatusExporter` (replica health), `GlobalConfiguration` | 2 ew | Q2, first (`identity` passes 120 GB about 2027-02 at mid) |
| E2 | Reconcile listing and skip: the reconcile listing returns (name, lastTxId, persisted applied index, database uuid) with no fingerprints, so the leader never hashes for it; a database whose applied index, lastTxId and uuid match is not refreshed. Fingerprints stay only in the cold-start bootstrap election. Per-pass failure isolation already exists. Touches the install-boundary bookkeeping (`installedBoundaryIndex`, #6760, #8577), so it ships with chaos tests. | `DatabaseReconciler`, `ArcadeStateMachine` (reconcile path, `localBootstrapState`), `LeaderDatabaseQuery.DatabaseInfo`, `PostBootstrapStateHandler` | 4 ew | Q2 |
| E14 | Free-space alert: project the next install or admission against usable space and raise a metric before `checkUsableSpace` refuses | `SnapshotInstaller`, `HealthMonitor` | 0.5 ew | Q2 |

Cost: reads that do not ask for history are unchanged; writes to non-versioned types pay one `isVersioned()` check; a versioned create pays 9 B; the first update or delete per transaction pays one image copy plus the index entries (0.4). ---

## 4. Serving each modality at scale

Latencies are targets on NVMe with a warm hot set, at 3x unless noted. Cold-row figures count both reads (about 100 us each, 32-way) and bytes (whole pages over 5 to 6.5 GB/s), with the schema v1 page sizes of 0.4 (HASH point lookups at 4 or 16 KB, Record and TextBody buckets at 32 KB, `recdir` at 16 KB); on stock page sizes the hydrate and search figures would be about 10x and 6x higher. Today's upstream baseline is p50 1.08 s and p95 1.55 s. Fan-out across databases runs in process inside the SPORK plugin. Four engine items this table used to list as gaps are implemented in worktrees and merge in Q2 (rounds 4 and 5): geo kNN, metre radii and Cypher geo seeks (`workstreams/geo.md`, was E9), n-gram, edge n-gram, reversed, phone and phonetic analyzers with automaton-driven fuzzy seek (`workstreams/partial-text.md`, was E11), BM25 top-K scoring on primitive arrays (`workstreams/fulltext-topk.md`, the non-format half of what E8 used to be), and parallel per-bucket vector search with heap-budgeted rebuild permits (`workstreams/vector-scale.md`, was E7). Set-oriented retrieval is 4b, the System 1 index 4c, the decision layer 4d.

| Modality | Index and placement | Query path | p50 / p95 | Engine gaps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Exact identifier lookup** | UNIQUE HASH on `key` in `identity` and in each `rec_<p>` (16 KB pages); UNIQUE HASH `upstreamRid` and `record_id` per partition and `recdir` for upstreamRid (4 KB pages); round 6, `02-retrieval-speed.md` C8 | Normalize the token, then a registry lookup (counts, periodMask, componentId). If shared, only the periods in periodMask; if not, all partitions by key (up to 12 point lookups at 3x). Then `HAS_*` in-edges to Records. `/intake` and footprint calls apply Recon's per-token cap (default 300, max 2,000, newest first) and return `truncated` and `totalAtLeast`; only `/pivot` pages a full footprint, with a `hub` flag (2.4). Pages of 500 with keyset cursors. | key 2 / 10 ms; 500-record footprint, cold rows, 60 / 200 ms. Reads per row: edge chunk (amortized, about 0.1) + Record page (1), since identifiers and place are on Record; 500 rows is about 550 reads and 17 MB (32 KB Record pages, 16 KB edge-chunk pages), 55 ms serial, about 3.5 ms of I/O on the 32-way pool (2.5; `02-retrieval-speed.md` P2) | None |
| **Structured search** | LSM_TREE composites (stateCode, tsPosted), (areaCodes BY ITEM, tsPosted), (services BY ITEM, tsPosted), (countryCode, city, tsPosted), (age, tsPosted), (views, tsPosted), (tsCollected) per partition, on the denormalized Record fields (2.1). A city-only filter resolves through `Place.cityNorm` to placeIds, then (placeId, tsPosted). Business and email filters are substring matches through grams3 on local Business and Email. Keyset on (tsPosted, record_id). | Newest partition first, with early stop once limit and window are satisfied. `sort=views_desc` uses (views, tsPosted). **Totals:** an exact count by COUNT pushdown, capped at 10,000, above which the response says "at least 10,000"; `POST /search:count` is the count-only path for Recon's bulk tool (up to 50 terms). **q:** Recon's q is a substring over title, source, text, phones, emails, businessNames and services. SPORK serves it as full-text over `TextBody.text` (console role) unioned with intake classification of q against identifier keys and grams; the difference is accepted through shadow-diff. | First page of 50 rows 20 / 100 ms; 500 cold rows 60 / 200 ms. Reads per row: Record page (index leaves are sequential and amortized) + title from `txt_<p>` (textHash HASH bucket page + TextBody page) = about 3, since phones, emails, socials, businessNames, city and country are on Record. A row moves a 32 KB Record page, a 16 KB HASH bucket page and a 32 KB TextBody page = 80 KB, and the title needs no external page (it is inline on TextBody). 500 rows = 1,500 reads and 40 MB: 150 ms serial, about 9 ms of I/O on the 32-way `spork-hydrate` pool (6 ms on latency, 6 to 8 ms on bandwidth; `02-retrieval-speed.md` P4b), inside the target. On stock pages (64 + 256 + 64 KB per row) it would be 197 MB and 30 to 39 ms; after M20 (16 KB Record and TextBody pages) 24 MB. | Check the planner's choice among composites; the plugin forces the index when needed |
| **Full-text** | FULL_TEXT (BM25) on the single derived property `TextBody.text` per `txt_<p>`; warm unpruned unless valve 1 is on. The plugin keeps its own term document-frequency table in `identity`, counted at ingest with the same analyzer. Evidence FULL_TEXT in `identity` on one derived plaintext property (title + excerpt + origin, Recon's FTS5 columns), protected by role grants and LUKS, not envelope encryption (5.6). | Newest-first parallel top-K per partition on the BM25 top-K path (`FullTextTopK`, done, round 5: one pass over each term's postings into primitive arrays, about 80 ns per posting against 350 ns and four boxed structures on the general path, 4x on 1M documents, no format change, switch `arcadedb.fullText.topK`, a key that exists only in the partial-text worktree until the Q2 merge). Partitions are merged by RRF on rank, because each partition scores with local IDF and the engine accepts no external statistics until E8a (the top-K path pools statistics only within one database). The gate is the **sum** of df over the query's terms: 12% of a partition's unique texts on the top-K path (16 B per posting), 2% on the general path (100 B per entry), which still serves phrases, wildcard, prefix, fuzzy and regexp expansions, `NOT`, minimum-should-match, unlimited queries and any RID allow-list pushdown; parallel across partitions only under 0.3 GB of summed heap, else sequential; over the bound, refused on n1 to n3 or sent to n4 (1.4). Hits resolve to Records by `textHash`. | Selective terms 40 / 150 ms. Common terms over all history: about a quarter of the round-4 figure on the top-K path (one pass at 80 ns per posting: a 10% term over a 40M-text partition is about 0.3 s per partition, sequential across 12), seconds until E8. | **E8 (re-scoped, round 5):** a block-max posting layout (RID-sorted segments with per-block max tf and min dl, built by compaction and replicated like the sparse-vector segments) so Block-Max MaxScore can skip postings; the worktree calls it "a project of its own" (format, compaction, recovery, HA replication of the new files), so it is an index-format change, estimated at 8 ew, in Q2 (train 2, round 9), landing by `REBUILD INDEX` and off Recon's critical path until the shadow-diff harness accepts it. E8a (1.5 ew, Q2) for global statistics. A RID-filter-aware top-K (set-scoped full-text off the general path) is a 1 ew follow-up listed with E8. Until then, common-term queries require a selective term or a posted window. Phrases are approximated by term intersection (no positions). Per-field analyzers now work (partial-text workstream: each property indexed and queried with its own `<field>_analyzer`; the BM25 length signature includes them, so an upgraded index needs `REBUILD INDEX`). Analyzers are built with a no-arg constructor by `Class.forName` in the engine classloader and never see the index metadata, and `FullTextIndexMetadata` refuses unknown keys (round 4), so the warm-pruning stopword list is resolved **by convention**: the offline rewrite generates one analyzer subclass per partition (`SporkStopAnalyzer_<p>`, in a jar in the server `lib/`) that reads `${spork.analyzers.dir}/<class>.stop`, and names it as the index analyzer. |
| **Partial: phone digits** | Local `Phone`: `rev` (suffix becomes a prefix scan), `digits` (area code and exchange range scans, sequential blocks), `digitSuffixes[] BY ITEM` (all suffixes of the 10-digit key, so any substring is a prefix of some suffix) | Range scan per partition, deduplicated by key | 5 / 30 ms | None. Lists are built at ingest; `BucketIndexBuilder` supports BY ITEM. The FULL_TEXT `phone` analyzer (every suffix of at least 4 digits, 0.9 ms for a last-4 lookup over 1M rows in the workstream benchmark against 6.2 s for the scan) is the P0a A/B alternative to the BY ITEM suffix list; both are term lookups, the lists need no rebuild to change. |
| **Partial: handle and email fragments** | `grams3[] BY ITEM` on local Handle, Email and Business; a non-unique `handle` index on local and registry Handle | Intersect, then verify `contains` on candidates only. Intake of a bare social token (`jane_doe`, `@jane`) runs the `handle` index unioned with the grams3 substring match, verified by contains, capped by the per-token `limit` (default 300, max 2,000; round 4, the same cap as every other token) and matching Recon's substring rule across platforms; `/dossier:footprint` and the watch scan's `/intake` calls use the same rule. | 20 / 100 ms | None. E11 is done: `ngram(3,8)`, `edge_ngram`, `reverse` and `phone` analyzers exist in `index/fulltext/analysis` (substring 51 ms against 1,743 ms, suffix 4.3 against 1,470 on 1M rows). The n-gram index loads 35x slower than a plain index, so it is used for short identifier fields only and P0a decides per field between the BY ITEM lists and the analyzer. |
| **Fuzzy and phonetic names** | `phonetic[] BY ITEM` + `grams3[]` + `tokens[]`; or a FULL_TEXT `phonetic` analyzer (Double Metaphone or Beider-Morse, workstream) with `term~2` fuzzy | Union of candidates, then Jaro-Winkler rerank (new SQL function in `function/sql/text`). Fuzzy terms now seek through a Levenshtein automaton (`AutomatonTermSeeker`) over distinct keys instead of testing every posting: 75 ms against 562 ms for one edit, 109 against 463 for two, on 1M rows. | 50 / 200 ms | JW function (plugin or engine, 1 ew) |
| **Board search** | The same gram and phonetic lists on Target and Board entries in `identity` | | under 20 ms | None |
| **Geo radius + time** | GEOSPATIAL on 8M `Place` vertices (8M x 300 B = 2.4 GB instead of about 60 GB for record points), plus (placeId, tsPosted) composite per partition; lat/lon on Record for exact distances; `geoCell` (geohash-7) on Record for heatmaps | `geo.dWithinMeters(Place.point, p, r)` (index-backed) or `geo.nearest('Place[point]', p, k, {maxDistanceMeters})` resolves the places, then range scans on (placeId, tsPosted) per overlapping partition, then exact haversine on Record lat/lon. For wide discs the geo workstream's `(cell, day)` pattern is the P0a option: a `cell3` (geohash-3) property and a (cell3, tsPosted) composite per partition make a 200 km x 14 day sweep 9 to 25 index ranges instead of thousands of placeId scans, at +48 B per record (43 GB at 3x), adopted only if the Place path misses the target. | 200 km x 14 days: 30 / 150 ms; kNN k=10 on 5M points 5 to 9 ms (workstream benchmark, 300 to 800x the scan) | None. E9 is done: `geo.nearest`, `geo.dWithinMeters`, `geo.geohashCover`, `NodeGeoIndexSeek` for Cypher `point.distance` and `point.withinBBox`, in-transaction visibility of pending entries. Radius candidates are not distance-ordered ("nearest n within r" uses `geo.nearest` with `maxDistanceMeters`); the index path is single-threaded (parallel per-bucket cell walks are a follow-up). |
| **Convergence, tracks, hotspots, novelty, impossible travel, Markov next-locale** | Set-scoped: a hydrated set with lat/lon on Record. Golden-set parity tests against `engine.ts` and `analysis/spatial.ts`. | 2,000-record set: hydrate 80 / 250 ms plus compute 20 / 100 ms; global detectors every 15 min. Hydrate basis: 4 cold reads (56 KB) per upstream RID, 2 (36 KB) per `record_id` (2.5), about 8,000 reads and 112 MB (72 MB by `record_id`), about 35 ms of I/O on the 32-way pool (1.3 GB and 0.2 to 0.26 s on stock pages); about 0.8 s serial. | None |

Network view: components maintained incrementally (O(1) read), nightly over `CO_OCCURS` + `ACCEPTED_LINK` on n4. Available: `AlgoWCC`, `AlgoLabelPropagation`, `AlgoArticulationPoints`, `AlgoLocalClusteringCoefficient`; degree; frequent pairs; sequential number blocks by digit range scans. The link-chart operations (typed expand, degrees, common neighbours, k-hop counts, typed shortest paths, induced subgraph, history) are 4b. | `/graph/*` take `{ids[]}`, `linkOn` (default phone, email, imageHash, businessName, social) and `fuzzy` (style unions over the exact float32 `styleVec` on Record, the same values Recon thresholds at cosine 0.95 in `analysis/style.ts`). Recon's dossier, which picks the footprint-local component holding the anchor (`ops/dossier.ts` selectTarget), stays in Recon over hydrate (7.1). Golden-set parity tests against `analysis/graph.ts` and `algos.ts`. | Bounded 50 / 500 ms | None |
| **Group-by and time series** | Identifier counters (recordCount, firstSeen, lastSeen), BY ITEM indexes, per-partition aggregates; (identifierKey, dayBucket) counters in `ops` for 120 days (`TimeSeriesEngine` evaluated for rollups); day/week buckets, DOW and hour histograms, 30d vs 90d spikes | Query form: parallel per partition, merged in the plugin. Set form: `{ids[]}` over a hydrated set, as `Engine.cluster`, `commonalities` and `timeline` do today. | 5 / 300 ms | None |
| **Live feed** | (stateCode, tsPosted) and (tsCollected) composites per partition | A record enters a window if its posted **or** collected time falls inside it, so the two scans are unioned. Scans are bounded at now + 2 h to skip misdated records. stateCode is normalized at ingest. `feed.view` is audited on every request, as Recon does. | 6 h window 30 / 120 ms | None |

**Throughput.** Each node should handle roughly 300 to 800 mixed interactive queries per second (CPU-bound, to be measured in P0). Read capacity scales about 3x across n1 to n3, with n4 as overflow.

### 4.1 ROC tier details

**Segments and manifest.**
- Templates live in immutable segments of 1M templates (448 MB), encrypted with the month's biometric data key (5.6).
- The **gallery manifest** in `ops` (replicated by Raft) lists every segment, its quarter assignment, its representative or full role, and its tombstones.
- Each `roc-agent` keeps its local disk in line with the manifest. Sealed segment copies also go to the object store for DR.

**Checkpoint and replay.** Each roc-agent persists the manifest version and the last applied `outboxSeq` with its RAM gallery. On restart it replays ENROLL and DELETE events from that point.

**Node loss.**
- The 3 survivors each take a third of the lost quarter: 51.3M / 3 = 17.1M templates = 8.8 GB, plus a third of its tattoo quarter, read from their own disks.
- The load takes about 30 to 60 s, and results are flagged `partial` meanwhile.
- RAM after failover is 35 GB of face templates + 5 tattoo + 1 case gallery + 4 overhead = 45 GB, within the cap.

**Scores.** Scores are ROC calibrated similarities. Thresholds come from ROC's FMR table. Nothing ROC-derived goes into an ANN index.

**Version upgrades.** A new algorithm builds a parallel gallery from crops in the object store, under a new `algo@ver` namespace, and the manifest then switches atomically. At mid today that is 218M crops x 0.1 core-s = 21.8M core-s = 252 core-days, so GPU is strongly preferred (Q-ROC2).

## 4b. Set-oriented retrieval and the intel-platform read contract

Owner directives 2 and 3 (`00-research-findings.md`) make two demands of the read path: every modality answers "over this set" as well as "over the corpus", with Recon compare (2 to 4 selections in records, entities, sets and faces modes) as the model; and the link-analysis platform built on top issues typed, bounded, set-valued operations (a set, one link type, a page), never "the graph" (`research/intel-platform-data-layer.md` 1.4: Palantir caps search-around at 3 chained hops and pages at 100 to 1,000, Maltego transforms return in well under a second with soft and hard limits). Both reduce to one primitive, the ObjectSet, and fifteen read operations with budgets.

### 4b.1 ObjectSet service

A plugin service on every node materializes any selection as a **Roaring64 bitmap of member keys**, keyed by the SHA-256 of its canonical definition tree (2.7 algebra) plus the `asOfCommitIndex` bucket it was built at.

- **Member keys.** Record sets use `record_id` (the period sits in its high bits, so the bitmap's high-32-bit containers are per partition and a paged hydrate walks it in page order). Identifier sets use the 64-bit hash of the canonical key, image sets the first 64 bits of `sha`, face sets the hash of the Face key, place sets `placeId`. A 2,000-member set is about 8 KB, a 100K-member set about 400 KB (about 4 B per sparse member).
- **Materialization cost is the cost of the leaf.** `static(ids[])` is free; `filter(base(Record), where)` is a `/search` with a 10,000-row cap (R8); `searchAround(set, HAS_PHONE, sideA, 100)` is a typed expand (R2); `ref(savedId)` loads an `ObjectSetDef` and re-evaluates it or, for a watch, reads its `ObjectSetSnapshot` (2.1). Union, intersect and subtract of 2 to 4 materialized sets of up to 100K members run in well under 1 ms; `count` is O(1) on the bitmap.
- **Projection.** `project(set, dimension)` turns a record set into identifier keys (phones, emails, socials, businessNames, imageHashes, all from the denormalized arrays on Record, 2.1), places (`placeId`), days (`tsPosted`), style labels, or faces (Face keys of the set's images in `media_<p>`). Its cost is one hydrate of the set (2,000 records: 112 MB, about 35 ms of I/O by upstream RID, 72 MB and 26 ms by `record_id`, 2.5), after which the projected sets are cached under the derived definition hash.
- **Cache.** An LRU of recent bitmaps and projections, 1 GB on n2 and n3 inside the 8 GB query slice (1.4), with the manifest of what is cached in `ops` so a node restart rebuilds the hot sets lazily. Saved sets (`ObjectSetDef`) and watch snapshots (`ObjectSetSnapshot`, envelope-encrypted) persist in `identity` with tenant, case, markings and `createdOc`.
- **Markings and purpose.** A bitmap is built under the caller's OC; its definition hash includes the marking set the caller holds, so two analysts with different clearances never share a cached set. Returning any member list is a footprint read and is audited by data class (5.2).

**Recon compare, as Recon computes it (round 5).** `compare.ts` and `server-compare.ts` define four modes over 2 to 4 groups G1 to Gn (`slice(0, 4)`), and SPORK reproduces each exactly, with the golden-set parity tests of WS4 extended to the compare field set:
- **records mode** (`compareRecords`): one record per column (the first id of each group). For each of the 12 fields in order (locale as `city, state`, posted as the first 10 characters of `postedAt`, source, age, phones, emails, socials as `platform:handle`, business, services, image hash, style label, views), `project(single record, field)` with Recon's keyer (phones through `normPhone`, the last 10 digits; everything else lowercased), then the **all-way intersection** across every column (`intersect` folded left), a per-column `sharedMask` marking which values fall in the intersection, and the row-level `shared` flag only when there are at least 2 records, **every column carries a value** and the intersection is non-empty. The response is `{columns: [{label, id, place, posted, imageHashes[0..1]}], fields: [{key, values, shared, sharedMask}]}`.
- **entities and sets modes** (`compareAggregates`, the same code for both): per group, `project(G_i, dim)` for dim in {phones (`normPhone`), images (image hashes verbatim), emails (lowercased), biz (lowercased business names), places (the `city, state` string, not `placeId`, whose key carries a geohash7 suffix)} plus count, spanDays, first and last from `postedAt`; then **pairwise** intersections per dimension for every (i, j), i < j, returned as `overlap[{a, b, aLabel, bLabel, phones[], images[], emails[], biz[], places[]}]` display lists; the columns ship `phonesDisp` (raw display values) capped at 12 and counts for images, emails and biz. **Sets mode is this aggregates path**, not records mode: Recon's sets are per-session minted refs from `/api/sets`, so the groups arrive as id lists (a `static(ids[])` leaf), never as saved `ObjectSetDef`s.
- **faces mode** (`server-compare.ts`): accepts **one** group or more; `POST /face/cluster {ids: union(G) capped at 120, cap: 80}` (4, ROC face clustering within a set); each cluster's `groups` is the **union** of the group labels over its `recordIds`, `crossGroup = |groups| > 1`, sorted crossGroup first then by size descending; the face set per group is `project(G_i, faces)`.
- **per-group timeline, places and tempo** are R10 and R11 over each `G_i`, with `segmentBy` = group.

On the ObjectSet service these are projections (one hydrate per group, cached under the derived hash) and bitmap intersections over the field keyer's 64-bit hashes, with the display values hydrated for the lists Recon ships. The n-way `shared = G1 AND ... AND Gn` and `distinct_i = G_i MINUS (OR of the others)` algebra over record and identifier bitmaps is a **SPORK extension** for the link-analysis platform, exposed through `/sets/*` and not labelled "Recon compare". `POST /compare` (7.2) composes the four modes on one code path with one audit entry per request.

### 4b.2 The fifteen read operations

Budgets are p95 targets at 290M records from `research/intel-platform-data-layer.md` 4.2, derived from Palantir's caps and Maltego's sub-second transform expectation. "Exists" means WS3 (Q1) already serves it; "plugin" is new plugin code; "procedure" is a new Cypher procedure registered under `graph.*` or `sets.*` in `query/opencypher/procedures` for the platform's admin-only Bolt path, with the same code behind a `spork-recon` route; "engine" is an engine change.

| # | Operation | Shape and cap | Budget | SPORK mapping | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Hydrate | ids[] ≤ 5,000 per call, projection mask | ≤ 50 ms per 100 ids | `POST /records:hydrate` (2.5): 4 cold 16 KB reads per upstream RID plus the series walk; 2,000 ids in 25 to 30 ms of I/O and 12 to 38 ms of walk CPU on the 32-way pool | exists |
| R2 | Expand 1 hop, typed, capped | object(s), linkTypes[], side, perTypeLimit (default 100), order by recency or weight, `truncated` + `totalAtLeast` | ≤ 100 ms, independent of degree | `graph.expandTyped(ids, {linkType: cap}, side, order)`. Record to identifier reads the denormalized arrays (no edge). Identifier to records walks `HAS_*` in-edges partition by partition, newest partition first (periodMask), and stops at the cap; a hub (recordCount > 2,000) returns only the newest partition with `truncated=true` and `hub=true`. `CO_OCCURS` is ordered by `lastSeen` after reading the (hub-bounded) edge list. The OLTP walk is O(total degree) on a supernode because per-edge-type adjacency chains are designed but not implemented (`docs/2026-10-07-8868-per-edge-type-adjacency-design.md`); **E23** ships them in Q3 (train 3, round 9) so the walk is O(edges of the type). | procedure; E23 later |
| R3 | Degrees per link type | object(s) -> {linkType: count} | ≤ 10 ms | `graph.degrees(ids)`: `recordCount` and the precomputed `degree_<type>` properties maintained at ingest on registry identifiers (CO_OCCURS, MEMBER_OF, ACCEPTED_LINK counts), `Vertex.countEdges` as the fallback | procedure |
| R4 | k-hop neighbourhood counts, k ≤ 3 | seed set, linkTypes, k, nodeCap 10,000 | ≤ 300 ms for ≤ 10K reached nodes | `graph.khopCounts(set, types, k, nodeCap)`: BFS over OLTP edge lists in `identity` with hub and stop exclusion, reading neighbour ids only (no vertex load), per-depth and per-type counts, `truncated` at the cap. 10K reached nodes is about 10K edge-list reads at 32-way, 30 to 60 ms. A persistent registry-only GAV (`CO_OCCURS` + `ACCEPTED_LINK` + `MEMBER_OF`: 549M x (8 + 16 + 4) B + 450M edges x 8 B = 19 GB at 3x, 11 GB if restricted to identifiers with a CO_OCCURS degree of 2 or more) is worth measuring on n4 for uncapped analytics, but it does not fit next to the 10.2 GB union-find in n4's 16 GB OLAP slice and the capped path needs no GAV | procedure |
| R5 | Shortest paths | a, b, linkTypes, maxHops ≤ 4, k ≤ 5 | ≤ 200 ms | `graph.pathsTyped(a, b, types, maxHops, k)`: hop-bounded bidirectional BFS over registry edges with hub exclusion. The existing `shortestPath()`, `algo.kShortestPaths` and `algo.cchShortestPath` have no type filter and no hard hop cap | procedure |
| R6 | Common neighbours of 2 to 4 objects | ids[2..4], linkTypes | ≤ 50 ms | `graph.commonNeighbors(ids, types)`: read each object's typed adjacency (hub-bounded), sort, merge-join. For Records it intersects the denormalized identifier arrays with no edge read. GAV `countCommonNeighbors(a, b)` and `algo.commonNeighbors` (1 vs all) do not take 2 to 4 inputs | procedure |
| R7 | Object-set algebra | union, intersect, subtract of 2 to 4 sets up to 100K members | ≤ 100 ms counts, ≤ 200 ms first page | ObjectSet service (4b.1); the page is a hydrate of the first 1,000 members in `record_id` order | plugin |
| R8 | Faceted search with counts | filter tree, facets[] topN ≤ 1,000, total | ≤ 300 ms | `/search` with the composites of 4 and `COUNT` pushdown (`CountFromIndexStep`) for index-resolvable facets (stateCode, areaCode, service, age band, posted bucket, website); other facets are counted over the capped 10,000-row result and flagged `approximate`; total capped at 10,000 as today | exists, facets plugin |
| R9 | Aggregation over a set | groupBy exact, ranges, fixedWidth, duration; count, min, max, sum, avg, approximateDistinct; maxGroups 10,000 with `excludedItems` | ≤ 300 ms | `POST /groupby {ids[]}` over the hydrated set (Recon's `cluster` and `commonalities`), `StreamingAggregationStep` for the query form | exists |
| R10 | Timeline buckets over a set | set, timeProperty, bucket, segmentBy | ≤ 200 ms | `POST /timeseries {ids[]}` with `time_bucket()` over `tsPosted` and `tsCollected`; `segmentBy` group for compare; `dayCells` for the corpus-wide form | exists |
| R11 | Geo over a set | heatmap cells, bbox or polygon, within-distance | ≤ 300 ms | `/geo/*` over the set's `lat`, `lon` and `geoCell`; heatmap = `GROUP BY geoCell` at the requested precision; bbox and polygon through `geo.geohashCover` and `point.withinBBox`; radius through `geo.dWithinMeters` and `geo.nearest` (E9, done) | exists |
| R12 | Subgraph induced by a set | set ≤ 2,000 nodes, linkTypes, edge cap 360 by default (Recon's dossier cap), hard 10,000 | ≤ 300 ms | `graph.induced(set, types, edgeCap)`: for Record sets, build the identifier to members map from the denormalized arrays in memory (exactly Recon's `linkClusters`), emit one edge per shared identifier up to the cap; for registry sets, merge-join each member's typed adjacency against the set bitmap. `path.subgraphAll` has no set input | procedure |
| R13 | Provenance and history | object or link -> sources, assertions, decisions, as-of view | ≤ 50 ms | `graph.history(id, asOf)` (round 7): the engine's `history.of(id)` for every version of a versioned object or edge with `@txFrom`, `@txTo` and its `_prov` per version, `history.asOf(id, t)` for the state at `t`, `history.edges(identity, t)` for the memberships as of `t` (one edge-list read plus one `in` index lookup, versions decoded only for the ones returned), the `ResolutionDecision` chain (`supersedes`, `revertedBy`, winner key) for why, the narrowed `Assertion` documents for non-versioned subjects, and soft-edge `evidence[]`. Cost: one `rid` index lookup plus one read per stored version, under the 50 ms budget at any plausible version count; nothing is read from the history store by any other operation. The procedure applies the OC and markings predicate to every version it returns (the engine does not, until E13) | procedure over E25 |
| R14 | Saved set or watch re-evaluate and diff | setDef -> added and removed since cursor | ≤ 1 s, background | re-materialize the `ObjectSetDef`, symmetric difference against the last `ObjectSetSnapshot`, store the new snapshot; triggered by the Outbox when an event touches the set's sources (7.3) | plugin |
| R15 | Similarity over a set or the corpus | probe (vector, image, text, face template), k ≤ 100, allow-list set | ≤ 200 ms corpus, ≤ 100 ms set | 4c: the set is the allow-list bitmap; sets under about 20K items skip the index and score stored codes directly; faces go to the ROC tier for verification | 4c |

**Maltego transforms** map onto R2 with `weight` as the ordering score: `f(entityType, value, fields, softLimit, hardLimit) -> [{type, value, fields, weight, linkLabel, linkDirection}]` is `graph.expandTyped` with `perTypeLimit = softLimit`, the LinkType's two-sided display names as `linkLabel`, `ignoreSeenEntities` as `subtract(result, seenSet)` in the ObjectSet service, and Maltego's collection threshold (25 same-type entities collapse) served by a `collapsed: [{type, count, sample[]}]` block in the response so a hub never ships 100K nodes. Perpetual machines are R14.

**Memory and routing.** R1 to R12 run on n2 and n3 (they hydrate, and the resident bloom set lives in the 23 GB page cache, 1.4); the ObjectSet LRU is 1 GB per node; R4's BFS frontier of 10K ids is under 1 MB. Only the nightly components job, uncapped corpus analytics and exports go to n4 (3.1).

### 4b.3 Export

Subgraph export (nodes, edges, provenance, markings filtered to the requester) uses the `integration` exporter's `jsonl`, `graphml` and `graphson` formats plus a new CSV pair (`nodes.csv`, `edges.csv`), through an ObjectSet and R12, audited as `export.subgraph` with the Merkle root of the exported keys (5.2). Object-set export to CSV or JSONL uses the same path. No ad text or image leaves in any export (5.4).

## 4c. System 1 multimodal index

A System 1 index answers "what is near this?" in a few milliseconds with 90 to 95% recall at 100, precomputable and self-hostable; a System 2 verifier spends 100 to 500 ms on at most 500 candidates and decides. Recall lost in System 1 is unrecoverable, so the recall budget (nprobe, oversample) is the knob the analysts' "exhaustive" mode turns up (`research/system-one-multimodal-index.md` 2). SPORK keeps one posting-list ANN index per **embedding space** in each period database (one global codebook per space, shared by every partition's index, 4c.2), not per modality, and never embeds a structured constraint: time, geo, set membership and case ACL are allow-lists. Set-scoped **full-text** is the exception that still runs on the engine's general path: a RID allow-list pushed into `SEARCH_INDEX` is not eligible for the BM25 top-K path until a RID-filter-aware top-K exists (4, Full-text), so the plugin applies the 2% df gate to it.

### 4c.1 Spaces, models, codes, sizes (mid; today to 3x)

| Space | Members | Model (license) | Stored | Scan code | Rerank code | Items today to 3x | Disk at 3x |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **S-IMG** scene, object, watermark; text queries through the text tower | PDQ-cluster representative images (30% of distinct images, A15) | SigLIP 2 so400m 1152-d (Apache-2.0; multilingual text tower) PCA to 256-d, or PE-Core B/16 1024-d (Apache-2.0) when cost matters more than text-to-image quality; P0a picks on the 1M-image sample | none (regenerable) | 1-bit 256 = 32 B + 8 B ordinal and RID + overhead = 57 B | 4-bit 128 B | 131M to 405M | 23 + 52 = 75 GB |
| **S-PDQ** near-duplicate | every distinct image | PDQ 256-bit (Meta, BSD), Hamming threshold 31 | 32 B on `Image.pdq`; plus `pdqBands` (8 x 32-bit Hamming band keys, LSM `BY ITEM`, 144 B per distinct image) on the hot-window `media_<p>` and case databases only, 24 GB today and 43 GB at 3x, for radius-15 re-encode lookups and ingest dedup (`06-stylometry-perceptual-hash.md` 3.1); the radius-31 corpus-wide near-dup is this IVF space, and a corpus-wide band index (194 GB at 3x) or pHash (49 GB) is not stored (round 6) | the hash (Hamming) | exact Hamming + SHA | 435M to 1.35B | 54 GB (+43 GB of hot-window band keys, counted in `media`) |
| **S-FACE** recall shortlist | representative faces (1 per 2.5, A9) | YuNet (MIT) detection and 5-point alignment, AuraFace-v1 ResNet100 512-d L2-normalized (Apache-2.0, LFW 0.9965, CFP-FP 0.952, AgeDB 0.961; a few points under buffalo_l, enough for a shortlist) | int8 512 B, envelope-encrypted | PQ-64 B (1-bit untested on faces, A15) = 70 B | int8 512 B (4-bit 256 B is valve 5) | 87M to 270M | 19 + 138 = 157 GB |
| **S-TEXT** ad text and OCR text | unique `TextBody` | gte-multilingual-base 768-d (Apache-2.0, 305M params, 8,192 tokens, 70+ languages, Matryoshka) truncated to 256-d; the paired learned-sparse leg is the OpenSearch neural-sparse multilingual v1 doc encoder (Apache-2.0) in `LSM_SPARSE_VECTOR`, hot tier only | none | 1-bit 32 B + 8 B + overhead = 56 B | 4-bit 128 B | 145M to 450M | 25 + 58 = 83 GB (sparse leg +72 GB on the hot tier if enabled) |
| **S-STYLE** authorship | hot unique texts | the existing 24-d Recon vector (exact float32 copy on Record, cosine 0.95, 5.4); optional LUAR-MUD 512-d (Apache-2.0) validated on labelled operator clusters first | as today | INT8 jvector (4, Dense style) | exact float32 | 200M cap | counted in `txt` hot |
| **S-TATTOO** | detected tattoo crops | detector: an Apache or MIT YOLO-family model (YOLOX or RT-DETR, not the AGPL Ultralytics weights) trained on CC-BY tattoo data plus internal crops; embedding: PE-Core or DINOv2-B (Apache-2.0) 768-d to 256-d on the crop | none | 1-bit 32 B + overhead = 74 B with ordinal and RID | 4-bit | 8.7M to 27M | 2 GB |
| **S-DOC** case documents | screenshots and posters in open cases | ColQwen2 multi-vector through MUVERA fixed-dimensional encodings or Light-ColPali token merging | PQ | MaxSim | ≤ 1M | 2 GB fixed |

Totals (0.4): 453 B per record at mid, 410 GB at 3x including 37 GB of SPANN boundary replication and the 2 GB S-DOC leg; 133 GB today; heap 7.1M centroids = 2.57 GB at mid 3x (0.83 GB today, 5.34 GB at high 3x) at the per-space centroid cost of A15 (int8 codes: 356 B for the 256-d spaces, 612 B for S-FACE, 132 B for S-PDQ), inside the 8 GB vector residency slice only once S-STYLE has moved onto the IVF index in Q2 (1.4, round 9). The owner's plan caps disk at 4,520 GB after H1: with the index the mid footprint is 93% at 3x and the second-drive order trigger (3,616 GB) falls at month 18.8 (0.6; round 7 added the version history line). Valve 5 (4-bit face rerank) saves 69 GB. OCR: PP-OCRv5 mobile (Apache-2.0; detection 0.77, recognition 0.80; docTR, Apache-2.0, for Latin-only ads) runs on text-bearing images (about 35%), and its output is not an embedding: the deterministic normalizers (libphonenumber, Recon's last-10-digits and lowercase-handle rules through `spork-keys`) turn it into Phone and Handle identifiers with `_prov.sourceId = ocr`, confidence and bbox, so OCR text enters the exact identifier indexes and the link graph, and S-TEXT as a second text field.

**Retired.** InsightFace buffalo_l (SCRFD det_10g + ArcFace w600k_r50), which Recon ships today, is licensed for non-commercial research only and is retired outright: no ArcFace embedding is kept, migrated or used as a seed, and `face-onnx.ts` and `face-fetch.ts` are removed with `SporkSource` (7.2). Also not used: EdgeFace (CC BY-NC-SA), AdaFace official weights (research-only training data), facenet-pytorch (VGGFace2 terms), ImageBind (CC BY-NC), jina-embeddings-v3 and SPLADE-v3 (CC BY-NC), Wegmann style embeddings (no license), MiVOLO weights (unstated license) and any open age model as a signal (2 to 3x worse than ROC on minors and unvalidated). DINOv3 (custom Meta license with an espionage clause) and Hotels-50K (no license file; TraffickCam partnership) are owner questions 20 and 21; DINOv2 (Apache-2.0) is the substitute if DINOv3 is rejected.

### 4c.2 The `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` index type (E18)

`Schema.INDEX_TYPE` is a closed enum, so this is engine work: a SPANN-style posting-list index in which **centroids live on heap and postings live in LSM pages**, which is what makes corpus-wide recall fit a 126 GB node and replicate for free.

- **Centroids.** A per-space, globally trained, versioned codebook (`algo@ver`, so scores compare across partitions), about 200 items per posting corpus-wide (1,000 for PDQ), held once per node on heap at **int8 precision** for every dense space (round 5: nprobe selection needs more than the 1-bit scan precision; 256 B for the 256-d spaces, 512 B for S-FACE, the 32 B hash for S-PDQ) plus the engine's 36 B of location and ordinal per resident vector (`workstreams/vector-scale.md`) and a 64 B share of the degree-16 jvector graph over the centroids (`OnDiskGraphIndex` and `GraphSearcherPool` reused) for the nprobe selection: 356, 612 and 132 B per centroid, 7.1M centroids and 2.57 GB at mid 3x (0.4). Per-partition k-means is rejected: it would lose score comparability across partitions and multiply the resident codebooks.
- **One index per space per partition database (round 5).** The postings live inside `media_<p>` and `txt_<p>` (1.2), and the engine never queries across databases, so each partition holds its own `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` per space, built against the shared codebook. Each index keeps a centroid-to-posting **directory** in its own pages (8 B per centroid, about 57 MB per partition at 3x, read through the page cache, never on heap). A corpus-wide probe selects nprobe centroids once on the global codebook, then reads those centroids' postings from every partition in the time window: `partitions x nprobe` posting reads (4c.3). A partition's posting holds about 200 / partitions items (50 today, 17 at 3x, about 1 KB), so posting pages **pack many postings** (about 17 per 16 KB page at 3x, in centroid order within the page) and a posting is a (page, offset, length) run, not a page of its own.
- **Postings.** Each posting is a run inside `PaginatedComponent` pages holding (ordinal, RID, scan code) entries, borrowing `SegmentFormat`, `SkipEntry` and `RidScoreMinHeap` from `index/sparsevector`; rerank codes sit beside the scan codes or inline on the vertex for small sets. Boundary items are replicated into their 2 nearest postings (SPANN), the 30% in A15. Because postings are LSM pages they ride the Raft log and the snapshot path like any index, which closes the "`.vecgraph` is not replicated" gap of jvector for these spaces (E3 still matters for S-STYLE until it moves onto the IVF index in Q2, round 9, and for the case galleries).
- **Codes.** `LSMVectorIndexPQFile` and `BinaryCodeCache` supply the PQ and 1-bit codebooks; 1-bit scan plus 4-bit or int8 rerank is the Elastic BBQ and RaBitQ pattern (recall@1 0.90 to 0.99 at 4 to 7 bits with a 3x oversample rerank).
- **Updates.** SPFresh-style in-place split, merge and reassign of postings at about 1% daily churn, so a space is never rebuilt whole; the build of a new period's codes is a streaming k-means on a sample plus one assignment pass, O(centroids) of heap, which removes jvector's 1,104 B per node of build heap and its 10M-per-bucket comfort zone (`workstreams/vector-scale.md`).
- **Allow-lists.** `preFilterPlan` and the IVF scan accept a Roaring bitmap of ordinals (the ObjectSet of 4b, mapped to ordinals through the location index); `VECTOR_INDEX_PREFILTER_MAX_SELECTIVITY` stays the exact-scoring crossover. Posting-list indexes avoid the graph stall that ACORN shows at 1% filters (67.7% recall), which is why the corpus tier is IVF and not a graph; per-case galleries stay small jvector graphs.
- **Liveness and deletion.** `LiveVectorBitsFilter` hides tombstoned ordinals; the deletion saga removes codes through index maintenance in the same transaction as the vertex (5.5), and a posting is rewritten on its next split or merge.
- **`HAMMING` metric (E19)** for BINARY-encoded properties, so S-PDQ uses the same path.

### 4c.3 Query path and fusion

1. **Classify the probe** (image, face crop, tattoo crop, text, token, RID, saved set); 4d's decider routes ambiguous probes and picks the legs.
2. **Encode** in the sidecar under `algo@ver` namespaces: an image enters S-IMG, S-PDQ, and, after YuNet and the tattoo detector, S-FACE and S-TATTOO; text enters S-TEXT, the S-IMG text tower, FULL_TEXT (BM25) and the sparse leg; a RID probe reuses stored codes at zero encode cost. Probe embeddings are cached.
3. **Constrain.** Time window to partitions; geo radius to a `placeId` set to a `(placeId, tsPosted)` scan to a RID allow-list; case ACL and Recon set to allow-lists. All bitmaps.
4. **Scan.** nprobe 64 centroids by default, adaptive to 512 when the allow-list is selective; the selected centroids' postings read from each partition's index in the time window (12 partitions at 3x unless 4d.2 prunes them); Hamming or asymmetric distance on scan codes; top 10k; rerank on 4-bit or int8; top k per leg.
5. **Fuse.** RRF (k = 60) across legs first; DBSF per leg once calibration tables exist (the modality gap means text-to-image and image-to-image need separate calibrations); learned linear weights from `RankerModel` later (E22).
6. **Verify (System 2).** Faces: ROC 1:1 on the shortlist, and ROC's age band for a minor-likelihood flag (stored as band plus confidence, never a scalar, human review only). Tattoos: ROC tattoo 1:N on the shortlist. Near-duplicates: exact Hamming and SHA. Text: a cross-encoder rerank on at most 50 candidates (100 to 200 ms on CPU, 30 to 50 ms on a T4). Images that need their text: OCR on the candidates only, then the deterministic identifier normalizers. Rooms: dense patch matching (DINOv2 or DINOv3 patch tokens with mutual nearest neighbours) and analyst review, with people masked before embedding (the TraffickCam protocol). Then the analyst.

**Set-oriented behaviour (R15).** A set of at most about 20K items skips the index: codes are stored inline on `Image`, `Face` and `TextBody` (40 to 72 B each), so the set-scoped endpoints that already hydrate the set score it directly (6,000 x 6,000 32-byte Hamming comparisons is about 10 ms on one core), and "shared faces, images or style across groups" is an in-memory kNN join tagged by group. Between 20K and about 20% of a partition the IVF scan intersects the bitmap, with cost about 1 / selectivity (nprobe up to 512, 20 to 40 ms at 1%). Above that it is a plain nprobe 64 corpus scan.

**Latency (per node, 32-way pool, NVMe about 100 us).**

| Stage | 300M records | 900M records |
|---|---|---|
| Probe encode: text 0.3B on CPU int8 / GPU; SigLIP 2 image on CPU / GPU; RID probe | 30 to 60 ms / 5 ms; 150 to 300 ms / 10 ms; 0 | same |
| IVF scan (round 5): nprobe 64 postings in each of `partitions` indexes, 12.8K codes in all | 4 partitions x 64 = 256 posting-page reads: 0.8 ms I/O on the 32-way pool + 4 x 0.2 ms compute = 1.6 ms | 12 x 64 = 768 reads: 2.4 ms I/O + 2.4 ms compute = 4.8 ms; 1.6 ms when the decider prunes to the newest 4 partitions |
| Rerank 200 candidates | 0.7 to 1 ms | same |
| Per leg | 3 to 5 ms p50, 10 to 15 ms p95 | 5 to 8 ms p50, 15 to 25 ms p95; 3 to 5 ms pruned |
| 4 legs in parallel + RRF | 5 to 8 ms p50 | 8 to 12 ms p50; 5 to 8 ms pruned |
| Set-scoped over a hydrated 2,000-record set | +1 to 2 ms over the hydrate | same |
| System 2: ROC rescore 500 faces, probe templatize included | about 100 ms | same |
| System 2: cross-encoder on 50 texts | 100 to 200 ms CPU, 30 to 50 ms GPU | same |

**Partition pruning is the stated mitigation at 3x, with its own budget.** The decider's partition-pruning question (4d.2) runs once per query against the partition summaries in `ops` and returns the partitions to probe first; the default policy probes the newest 4 partitions (the 300M cost) and widens to all 12 when the capped result is short or the Noul confidence is under threshold, so an exhaustive probe is never more than one round trip behind. Its budget is the decider call itself (under 150 ms, in parallel with the cheap legs) and nothing on the scan.

**Anchors do not transfer (round 5).** SPANN reaches about 1 ms at 90% recall on 1B items with 32 GB resident and DiskANN under 3 ms at 1B with 64 GB: both keep 32 to 64 B per item resident, while SPORK keeps about 1 B per item (2.57 GB over 2.5B items at mid 3x) by holding one centroid per 200 items and nothing else. Neither anchor, nor anything in the research note, derives 90 to 95% recall at 100 from nprobe 64 at that ratio, so the recall claim is a measurement, not a citation: **P0a gate**, on the 1M-image and matching text samples, measure recall at 100 against exact brute force as a function of nprobe (16 to 512) at 200 items per posting and report the centroid count (items per posting) needed for 90% recall at nprobe 64; the RAM line in 0.4 and 1.4 is re-run with that count before E18 starts, since the heap figure is the justification for this section. The engine's first signal is then 5 to 12 ms and the **probe encoder dominates**: a GPU per node, or the probe cache and RID-probe reuse, is the largest lever on perceived System 1 latency (owner question 22).

### 4c.4 Encoding: sidecar, ingest, backfill

- **Sidecar.** ONNX Runtime (Java `onnxruntime` for the CPU path; `OrtSession.run` is thread-safe; models exported with a dynamic batch axis, micro-batches of 16 to 64 on a dedicated pool per the engine-concurrency rules) runs as a 3 GB process per node in an `encode` cgroup of 4 cores (2 on n1), with a model registry, `algo@ver` on every code, a probe-embedding cache, and image decode off the DB heap (libvips). If a GPU exists it is a gRPC embedder (Triton or a small ORT server) in the sidecar; CUDA never loads into the DB JVM (1.4).
- **Daily stream (A16).** At today's 300K distinct images/day: tier A (YuNet, AuraFace, scene model, text presence) 300K / 32 img/s = 2.6 h and OCR 300K x 0.35 x 1.75 s / 16 cores = 3.2 h on 16 CPU cores cluster-wide, so CPU is enough; at 3x pace (1.25M/day) that is 10.9 + 13.3 h, which does not fit an off-peak window, while one H100-class card does tier A in 1.2 h and OCR in 1.2 h. A GPU is therefore required by 3x pace; on CPU, degraded mode encodes faces and PDQ for every image and defers S-IMG and OCR to a backlog.
- **Round 10 (fixed hardware, no GPU):** the GPU figures below are kept for the record; the plan is 0.8's CPU-only one: S-PDQ and S-TEXT in 9.4 days on the 16 encode cores, S-FACE in 17.3 and S-IMG on hot-window representatives in 17.9, OCR on the daily stream only.
- **Backfill (mid today, 435M distinct images).** Tier A 435M x 3.5 ms = 423 GPU-hours; OCR on 152M text-bearing images x 10 ms = 423 GPU-hours; text 145M unique x 1.2 ms = 48 GPU-hours; about 900 GPU-hours, 1,700 to 3,300 with a larger scene model, which is 3 to 6 weeks on one L40S-class box or $3.5K to $13K of spot cloud. It runs on the same burst host as the ROC face backfill (6.1), after the CSAM hash gate (5.7), and commits through the MediaJob path. CPU-only backfill (40 to 50 days for scene and face, OCR infeasible) is the fallback only.
- **Order (round 8).** S-PDQ and S-TEXT first, with Recon's Q1 move (S-PDQ also drives the S-IMG membership and dedup; 145M unique texts x 1.2 ms = 48 GPU-hours and 435M distinct images x 10 ms of CPU PDQ, 0.8 days on 64 cores, `budget.py`), then S-FACE, S-IMG, S-TATTOO and S-DOC in Q2 from one tier-A image pass with OCR (846 GPU-hours: 35 days on one card, 9 on four; round 9), case- and target-linked images first within each. **Sealed partitions are re-archived as the backfill completes on them (round 5):** the backfill writes about 26 GB of codes into every sealed `media_<p>` and 7.5 GB into every sealed `txt_<p>`, and sealed databases are otherwise archived only at seal and after deletion batches (0.4), so the backfill records its completion and codebook version per partition in `ops` and the plugin archives the partition again under the `transient-slot` lease; the aging rewrite checks that record before it trusts an archive (3.5 step 1).

### 4c.5 Risks specific to the index

Representative ratios (30% PDQ-cluster, 50% exact-text) are P0a measurements: at 100% S-IMG grows to 76 GB of postings and 173 GB of rerank (disk only; RAM is unchanged, which is the point of the design). 1-bit codes on faces are untested, so PQ-64 is the default. ROC's written position on a shortlist plus ROC rescore (Q-ROC5) gates S-FACE. The index relies on the planned drives (0.6) and on the 2.6 GB of centroid heap at mid 3x (5.3 GB at high), which fits the 8 GB slice only after S-STYLE moves onto the IVF index in Q2 (1.4); recall at nprobe 64 over 200-item postings is unmeasured until the P0a gate (4c.3), which round 8 makes a pass/fail gate by the end of week 4 with a fallback for the first spaces (8), and at 3x a corpus-wide probe reads 12 partitions' postings unless the decider prunes them.

## 4d. System 1 decision layer

A System 1 **decision** model is the other half: it evaluates a state and returns typed answers with probabilities (`noul` P(true), `choice` over 1 to 255 options, `score` over 1 to 10 levels), in one non-autoregressive pass, 70 to 500 ms, priced on input tokens, calibrated at population level. The interface (state plus typed questions in, probabilities out) is becoming a commodity across about 20 to 30 "decision models" from 13 makers (`research/system-one-decision-models.md` 2.3), so SPORK designs to the interface, not to a vendor. Embeddings decide *what is near*; deciders decide *where to look and what to trust*. One call per decision, so it runs once per record at ingest, once per query at planning, and on shortlists of tens to hundreds, never per index entry.

### 4d.1 Pluggable decider interface

```
decide(state: JSON | text, questions: {name: Noul | Choice | Score}, assets?: [image])
  -> {name: {probabilities, confidence, answer}}, latency, cost, modelVersion
```

Providers behind the interface, selected by `ThresholdPolicy.provider` per question:
- **Jev** (TypeSafe AI, `jev-1.14`): hosted API only, no weights, 64K tokens per request, all questions in a request evaluated in parallel against one state (the fan-out pattern: adding questions barely changes latency), $0.042 per million input tokens, 30 to 60 day version deprecations, no SLA found, enterprise zero data retention on request. Optional provider only (4d.4).
- **Perplexity pplx-decider-v1 27B**: Apache-2.0 open weights (a Qwen3.8-27B fine-tune), 262K context, about 49 GiB of GPU memory, self-hosted on the GPU burst host or a dedicated inference box; 85.7% against Jev's 84.5% on Perplexity's 7,210-sample panel. The **required** provider for any question whose state carries CHT data.
- **Rules**: a deterministic fallback per question (hand-written thresholds and regexes) that always answers, so no path depends on a model being up.
- **In-house classifier**: a logistic regression or small model over the decider's own signals (the phishing study in the research reached 95% from five Jev signals against 62.6% direct), trained from `OutcomeFeedback`.

Question templates are versioned like code, options are shuffled per call (Choice leans to the first option), dates, numbers and buckets are precomputed server-side (the models read dates as text), and the state is a compact decision-relevant JSON view (a record, a candidate pair, a partition summary, a query context), never raw ad text beyond what the question needs.

### 4d.2 Where it is used

| Use | Primitive | State | Action on the answer | Budget |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Query intent routing** | Choice over {face, tattoo, phone lookup, geo-temporal sweep, graph expansion, full-text, image similarity, set compare} plus Nouls per leg | the analyst's query text or probe type, recent session context | choose the 4c legs and their order; below confidence 0.6 run every leg | once per query, ≤ 150 ms, in parallel with the cheap legs so it never adds latency to them |
| **Partition pruning** | Noul fan-out "could the answer be in this partition?" | query plus each partition's summary (time range, sources, regions, modalities, size, hit rate, kept in `ops`) | probe partitions above threshold first, exhaustive fallback when the capped result is short | once per query |
| **Ingest priority scoring** | Score 1 to 10 on rubrics (trafficking indicators, urgency, identifier richness, quality) | the record's structured fields and text | stored as `Record.priorityScore`; hot items get S1 encoding and System 2 (OCR, face) first and sort first in feeds | once per record, batched 50 states per request |
| **Entity-resolution fast pass** | Score {different, possibly related, same} plus Nouls per attribute (same phone family, same style, same image set) | a candidate pair from blocking or S1 recall, as structured features (never pixels) | `same` above the high threshold proposes a `ResolutionDecision` under rule `decider@ver` that an analyst accepts; the middle band goes to `AnalystQueueItem`; `different` is recorded and never re-asked for the same pair and version | per candidate pair, ≤ 300 ms, batched |
| **Shortlist rerank** | Noul "same person, same ad, same room?" per candidate | the probe and each 4c candidate's structured features and verifier outputs (ROC score, OCR text, PDQ distance) | reorder the shortlist before the analyst sees it; never replaces the ROC verdict | ≤ 50 candidates per query |
| **Prefetch** | Score "will this entity, case or partition be touched again soon?" | session and case activity | warm the ObjectSet cache and the probe cache (4b.1); too slow for the page cache, right for entity-level prefetch | background |
| **Guardrails on System 2** | Noul relevance or extraction check | an LLM extraction or an analyst-authored spec | filter before indexing or alerting | per item |

Safety-critical actions (a deletion, a merge that is not reversible, an alert that pages a human) are never gated on the decider alone; adversarial ad text is a documented weak point and the models do not treat hostile content as hostile by default.

### 4d.3 Tables (all in `identity`, 2.1)

- **`DecisionLog`**: modelVersion, provider, questionHash, stateHash (never the state), probabilities, confidence, threshold applied, action taken, latency, cost, opContext, at. Written in the same transaction as the action it drove when there is one; otherwise appended by the caller. It is what audit, calibration monitoring and version deprecation read.
- **`OutcomeFeedback`**: decisionLogId, outcome (analyst confirmed or rejected the merge, verifier agreed, lead was real, alert was actionable), at, by. Joined to the log to fit thresholds per question and to detect drift when a provider ships a new version.
- **`ThresholdPolicy`**: (question, riskLevel) -> provider, model pin, accept and reject thresholds, middle-band width, batch size, rate limit, fallback. **Policy is data**, changed through an audited action, never a code deploy.
- **`AnalystQueueItem`**: the middle band as a first-class table: decisionLogId, subject keys, suggested answer, assignee, state, resolution. Recon's board and missions UI consume it.

At 3x pace the ingest scorer is 835K states/day in batches of 50 = 16.7K requests/day; at about 1,500 input tokens per state that is 1.25B tokens/day, $53/day on Jev's list price. **Self-hosted throughput and hardware (round 5).** The decider is one non-autoregressive pass, so it is prefill-bound. No throughput figure exists in the research note; the planning figure is derived, and WS14 measures it before any rate is promised: 2 x 27e9 FLOP per token against about 990 TFLOPS of dense FP16 on one 80 GB H100-class card is 18K tokens/s at full utilization, and at 45% (batch 32 states, 1,500 tokens each) about 8K tokens/s. At that rate 1.25B tokens/day is 43 GPU-hours/day, 1.8 cards running continuously (at 2K tokens/s it would be 174 GPU-hours, 7.2 cards; the round-4 figure of 7 GPU-hours/day would have needed 50K tokens/s per card and is withdrawn). The FP16 checkpoint is about 49 GiB, so it fits neither the L4 (24 GB) nor the L40S (48 GB) of owner question 22: the inference box is **one 80 GB card** (H100 or A100 80 GB class) for the FP16 weights, or an L40S with an int8 quantized checkpoint (about 25 GiB) or an L4 with int4 (about 14 GiB), each at an accuracy cost measured against the 85.7% panel before it is trusted; it is a separate box next to the encoder GPU, not in the 126 GB node budget (9.2 Q22, Q23). **Until measured, ingest priority scoring is scoped:** every record gets the rules fallback score, and the decider scores only the newest 300K records per day (today's whole stream at 65% of one card's duty; 36% of the 3x stream), chosen newest first, with the remainder scored by rules; the full 3x stream needs a second card. Query routing, partition pruning, the entity-resolution fast pass and shortlist rerank share the same card and are small by comparison (tens of thousands of states a day).

### 4d.4 Privacy posture

Hosted inference on CHT data is a real constraint: a decision call ships the state off the enclave, and the only legal footing found is a Master Customer Agreement plus DPA with a no-training commitment and zero retention on request; no SLA, acceptable-use list or SOC 2 statement was found and the MCA was not reviewable. So the **self-hosted path is required** before the layer ships: every question runs against the Apache-2.0 decider or the rules fallback by default, and a hosted provider may be enabled per question only by `ThresholdPolicy` for states that carry no personal data (partition summaries, query routing without identifiers), with the owner's sign-off (9.2 Q23). Calibration is population-level and says nothing about an individual answer, so every decider-driven resolution stays reversible with provenance (2.6), and image inputs stay out: faces and tattoos go to ROC, and the decider sees verifier outputs as text.

---

## 5. Governance in the database layer

### 5.1 Operating Context (OC)

- **Token.** The Recon gateway signs a compact JWT `{analyst, role, caseRef, purpose, tenant, markings[], iat, exp}` and sends it as `X-Spork-Context`. Later this moves to OIDC. **`role` is `console` or `ops`** (round 4): it is asserted by the gateway from which Recon module made the call (the classic console and its tools use `console`; dossier, package, watch scan, missions, overview and the board use `ops`), and it is the only thing that selects the engine user a request runs as (5.4). `markings[]` are the markings the analyst holds (2.7).
- **Analyst identity.** Recon's OC analyst is a self-declared header (`x-recon-analyst`). The gateway sets `analyst` to the authenticated `Principal.id` (`gateway/auth.ts`) and rejects a request whose `x-recon-analyst` differs, so signing never vouches for a self-asserted name.
- **Classic console.** Recon's classic console (`/api/run`, `/api/exec`, `/api/signal`, `/api/faces`, `/api/faces/stream`, `/api/geo`, `/api/recent`, `/api/focus`, `/api/expand`, `/api/compare`, and the monitor and media routes round 4 adds: `POST /api/watch`, `POST /api/watch/scan`, `GET` and `DELETE /api/alerts`, `POST /api/feedback`, `GET /api/image`) sends no OC today, and `intakeAudit` records nothing without one. Before `SporkSource` is enabled, the console declares an OC, or the gateway injects a default per-session OC (WS4). `/api/watch`, `/api/watch/scan` and `DELETE /api/alerts` are retired in favour of the ops routes (`/watches`, `/alerts`), which require a declared OC; `/api/feedback` requires an OC and records a `feedback.record` audit action; `/api/image` becomes the grant-checked `GET /image` (5.2). Recon's console and faces module handle 428 and the new 403 `purposeDenied`.
- **Missions and watches.** Recon's missions API takes no OC today (`missions/routes.ts`), yet mission targeting re-resolves seeds and runs detectors every 15 minutes per mission, and interval and Outbox-triggered watch scans do the same: intake-equivalent sensitive reads. So a Mission and a Watch store the creator's OC (`createdOc`: analyst, caseRef, purpose) at creation, and `/missions` and `/watches` writes require an OC (428 without one). **A gateway-injected default OC is never stored as a `createdOc`** (round 4): automation needs a purpose an analyst declared, so a watch or mission created under the session default is refused with 428 `declaredOcRequired` and the console prompts for one. Each automated run executes under that stored OC with actor `system:mission-targeting` or `system:watch-scan` (the creator in `onBehalfOf`), appends a `mission.targeting` or `watch.scan` audit entry per run before footprints are released, and is checked against purpose gates at run time (a `training` OC gets only minimized views; a revoked purpose stops the automation and raises an Alert). WS4 adds an OC to `/api/missions` and the missions UI.
- **Verification.** The `spork-recon` ServerPlugin verifies the token. Sensitive routes return **428** when it is missing or invalid.
- **Binding.** The engine has no transaction attribute API, so the plugin keeps the context in its own ThreadLocal scoped to the request and its transaction (transactions are thread-bound). Audit, scope checks, redaction and the Outbox all read it there.
- **Engine user binding.** Engine grants are enforced only when a security user is bound in `DatabaseContext` for the calling thread: `LocalDatabase.checkPermissionsOnFile` and `checkPermissionsOnDatabase` return immediately when there is no context or `getCurrentUser()` is null. The plugin runs in process and opens databases through `server.getDatabase(...)`, so every `spork-recon` route binds the engine user named by the **OC's `role` claim** (round 4: not by the route, because `/records:hydrate`, `/search`, `/intake` and `GET /records/{id}` serve both roles), the console or ops `ServerSecurityUser` resolved to its `SecurityDatabaseUser` per database, with `DatabaseContext.INSTANCE.init(db).setCurrentUser(...)` for each database it touches, before any read, and clears it in a `finally`. Under `ops` the plugin also refuses, before touching the engine, a title or text projection, the `q` full-text filter and any text-access call (403 `roleDenied`). Work handed to the `spork-hydrate` pool binds the same user on the worker thread. WS3 tests assert that an ops-role route reading `txt_*` fails with a security exception and that a shared route (`/records:hydrate` with a title projection under an `ops` OC) returns 403.
- **Grants are by exact database name.** `ServerSecurityUser` matches a database grant by exact name or `*` (round 4), so "the ops role has no grant on `txt_*`" cannot be a pattern: the ops user is granted explicitly on `identity`, `recdir`, `ops`, `audit` and every `rec_<p>` and `media_<p>`, and **granting the ops user on the new database is an audited step of period creation and of every admission** (3.5); a database it is not granted on lands on the deny-all sentinel, which is the safe failure. The WS3 test creates a period and asserts the grant.
- **Leader work.** Governed writes are pinned to the leader by the gateway, and audited reads on followers append through the plugin's internal leader route with the OC in the body (3.1). No header forwarding (E6) is needed.
- **Purpose gates** are a new policy, not a port: Recon enforces none today, and an analyst under `standing_monitoring` or `training` can run intake, feed, dossier and package. They are an owner decision (9.2 Q10), enforced only after Recon's `ApiError` and UI handle 403 `purposeDenied`. Proposed:

  | Purpose | Allows |
  |---|---|
  | `victim_identification`, `investigative_lead` | Face and tattoo search, and only with a caseRef. ARC (NCMEC) image search only under `victim_identification` with a caseRef. |
  | `network_disruption` | Everything except biometrics (phone lookup allowed) |
  | `standing_monitoring` | Watches and detectors only |
  | `training` | Synthetic or minimized views only. No biometrics. |

  Results flagged `minorFlag` require `victim_identification` and are audited at an elevated level. Which purposes may run set-scoped face clustering (`/face/cluster`, which Recon runs after every intake today) is part of the owner decision. When it is denied, the Recon client skips the auto-scan.

  **Gates and audit attach to data classes, not endpoints (round 4).** Recon's `resolveSource` for a phone or email token is `Engine.pivot`, so `/intake` and `/pivot` are the same read, and `/search` with an identifier filter, `/partial`, `/groupby`, `/records:hydrate`, `/graph/*`, `/geo/*`, `/sets/*` and `/compare` all return identifier footprints or record projections. Every route whose response carries that data class passes the same purpose gate as `/intake` and appends one batched audit entry per request (5.2); routes that return only counts or aggregates pass the gate but are audited per session. An analyst under `standing_monitoring` or `training` therefore cannot reach through `/pivot` what `/intake` would refuse.

### 5.2 Transactional, keyed, hash-chained audit (compatible with Recon)

**One chain per database**, because a transaction cannot span databases. Each database has `AuditEntry` documents and a `ChainHead` document updated in the same transaction.

**Hashed body and sidecar.**
- **v1 (imported genesis only).** Recon's existing file chain is imported byte for byte as the genesis segment of the `identity` chain. Its body is Recon's entry field set (every field except `hash`) and `hash = sha256(prevHash + canonicalJSON(body))`, Recon's `ops/audit.ts` `entryHash`. Because that body contains plaintext `subject` and `detail`, v1 entries cannot be redacted: destroying their subject or detail would break `entryHash(entry) === entry.hash`. Redaction of a v1 entry needs a signed redaction record (Ed25519, HSM) naming the entry's seq and original hash, which the v2 verifier accepts in place of recomputation; until the owner wants that, the imported segment is kept whole under its retention class.
- **v2 (every entry SPORK writes).** The hashed body is Recon's field set with commitments in place of plaintext: `subject` becomes `subjectKey = HMAC(K_subject, exact subject string)` and `detail` becomes `detailKey = HMAC(K_subject, canonicalJSON(detail))`, plus `v: 2`. `hash = sha256(prevHash + canonicalJSON(body))` as before. The display copy of subject and detail is envelope-encrypted (5.6) and stored outside the hash, so deletion propagation can destroy it and the chain still verifies.
- `db`, `tenant`, `epoch` and `mac` live in a sidecar outside the hashed body, so the MAC never feeds its own hash. A versioned verifier checks v1 (Recon's rules) on the genesis segment and v2 (commitment body, MAC, anchors) after it. Recon's `verifyLines` gains the v2 rule in WS4; without it, Recon's verifier and the overview "Audit Chain" KPI would report every SPORK entry as broken.
- The first v2 entry's `prevHash` is the last hash of the imported v1 segment.
- `mac = HMAC-SHA256(K_epoch, hash)`. `K_epoch` comes from KMS/HSM, rotates monthly, and is never written to disk. A local admin who rewrites a chain then fails the MAC check and the anchor check.

**Behaviour.**
- **Same transaction, fails closed.** The append is explicit plugin code inside the transaction on the leader, not a trigger: triggers and record listeners fire only where the transaction executes. If the append fails, the business write rolls back.
- **Ingest** is audited per batch, with the Merkle root of the batch's record keys.
- **Sensitive reads** append an entry on the leader before results are released, through the internal leader route when served on a follower. That adds about 5 to 15 ms, and `RaftGroupCommitter` batches these commits. `feed.view` is audited on every request, including cache hits, as Recon does. With no leader these endpoints return 503. Round 4 defines the set **by data class**: any response carrying identifier footprints or record projections (intake, pivot, search with identifier filters, partial, group-by, hydrate, the set-scoped graph and geo calls, set algebra, compare, dossier footprint, feed, face and tattoo search, text access, package, exports) appends **one entry per request** with the route's action and a Merkle root of the returned record keys, so a 2,000-id hydrate is one commit, not 2,000.
- **The returned ids are recoverable, not only provable (round 5).** A Merkle root alone lets a party who already holds the exact key list prove inclusion; it cannot answer "which analysts viewed record X" or "which records did analyst Y receive", which the intel-platform contract (per-access audit of who, what ids, purpose and case; 28 CFR Part 23 and CJIS dissemination logging, `research/intel-platform-data-layer.md` 3 and 4.4) and this document's own 5.5 step 4b and 9.2 Q19 rely on, and Recon's detail allowlist carries counts, never ids. So each data-class entry also persists the returned key list **outside the hashed body**, in the same transaction: a per-database `AuditAccess {db, seq, recordKeyHmac}` side table, one row per returned key (up to 5,000 per entry, the hydrate cap), with `recordKeyHmac = HMAC(K_subject, record key)` so the index on it is keyed exactly as `subjectKey` is. The Merkle root in the hashed body covers the same keys, so the rows are chain-bound (a tampered row fails the root) without being in the hash; they carry the entry's audit retention class and are destroyed by deletion propagation step 4b with the display copies. `GET /audit?record=<id>` (7.2) joins the table to the entries (who touched this record, under which OC and purpose); `GET /audit?analyst=&since=` lists an analyst's disseminated keys. Cost: 2,000 keys x about 48 B = 96 KB per hydrate entry, under 1 GB/day at the 1.7 entries/s of 20 analysts, inside the `audit` growth line (0.4). WS3 sizes it at 0.5 ew.
- **Images are audited per view, not per fetch (round 4).** Recon renders grids: a face scan shows one thumbnail per matched face instance (up to 120 records x 8 images = 960), and the client runs a scan after every intake, so a per-fetch `image.view` would be 960 chain commits per intake and 320 commits/s for 20 analysts at one intake a minute, against a chain head that serializes 200 to 500/s. Instead the audited call that produced the view (`face.cluster`, `records.hydrate`, `compare`, `intake.run`) writes one `image.view` entry with the Merkle root of its (record, hash) pairs and returns a short-lived signed **image grant** (HMAC over the pair set, 10 minutes, bound to the OC); `GET /image` checks the grant and appends nothing. A fetch without a grant is 403.
- **Completion entries from Recon.** Some audit detail exists only after Recon computes: `dossier.view` carries the dossier contentHash prefix, records, live and overlap counts (`ops/dossier-routes.ts`), and `intake.run` carries status (ok, empty, failed), ref, records, signals and targets (`server.ts` `/api/signal`). SPORK's pre-release entry (`dossier.footprint`, `intake.run` with status `released`) stays the access record and carries a `requestId`. Recon then appends a completion entry through `POST /audit/append` with the OC, the same `requestId` and only allowlisted detail keys (`dossier.view`: contentHashPrefix, records, live, overlaps; `intake.run`: status, ref, records, signals, targets; `package.export` as before; round 4 adds `export.csv` and `export.brief` for the console's `export csv` and `export brief` verbs and the `tools.ts` dossier verb (detail: rows, columns, a Merkle root of the exported record keys), `knowledge.export` for the mission knowledge snapshot (`GET /api/missions/knowledge`, which returns every mission and every evidence excerpt; served from SPORK's `/knowledge/export` with its own entry), and `feedback.record`). The plugin checks the action, the key allowlist and value types, and refuses a completion whose `requestId` has no pre-release entry from the same analyst within 15 minutes. So a later `package.export` can be tied to the viewed evidence by contentHash, as Recon's audit does today.
- **Subjects.** Recon filters audit by the exact subject string (for intake, a composite of up to 12 normalized tokens plus "(+n more)") and shows subject and detail in the activity feed. A v2 entry's `subjectKey` serves the filter, and the API returns the decrypted display copy for the feed. Deletion propagation destroys the display copy; the hashed commitments and the chain survive and still verify.

**Anchors.** Every 60 s a leased job does three things:
1. Writes all chain heads `(db, seq, hash)` into `audit`.
2. Signs the set with Ed25519 (HSM).
3. Exports it to WORM object storage (S3 Object Lock).

`GET /audit/verify` checks chains, MACs, anchor signatures and the WORM copies.

**One audit page for Recon.** The API merges per-database chains newest first by `(at, db, seq)`. Each entry carries its own per-database `seq` and a `db` field, so Recon's verifier checks seq = position per database, not over the interleaved page. Recon's numeric `AuditQuery.before` becomes a versioned cursor contract (v2: the opaque per-database seq vector). Filters: action, case, subject (by `subjectKey`). `total` is Recon's count of entries matching the filters (round 4: `ops/audit.ts` `query()` increments `total` only for entries that pass the action, caseRef and subject filters), so it comes from `COUNT` pushdown on (action), (caseRef) and (subjectKey) indexes on `AuditEntry`, capped at 10,000 like search totals, and from the per-database counters only when no filter is given. Verification is served from an incremental "verified up to (db, seq)" checkpoint in `audit`, maintained by the anchor job, never a full walk per request; Recon's overview audit KPI reads the same checkpoint. Analyst actions are almost all on the `identity` chain, so the merge is cheap.

**Throughput.** One chain head per database serializes audited transactions at roughly 200 to 500 per second. With audit by data class and per-view image grants, 20 concurrent analysts each running one intake a minute (intake, the face scan's `image.view`, a dossier footprint and two searches = 5 entries) produce 20 x 5 / 60 = 1.7 entries/s; the ingest batch entries and the automation runs add under 5/s. Per-fetch image audit would have added 320/s and saturated the head (round 4). If the chain ever binds, move to per-partition chains merged by anchor.

### 5.3 Case and tenant scoping (no RLS in the engine)

- The open-source ad corpus is shared across tenants. The case layer (Target, Case, Evidence, Board, Watch, Mission, case decisions, notes, audit) carries `tenantId` and `caseId`.
- Every plugin query builder injects the `tenantId` predicate and the **markings predicate** (2.7: the object's, the property's and the link's markings must all be in the OC's `markings[]`; unheld objects are absent, not redacted, so existence stays hidden). Case scoping is a per-endpoint choice, because Recon deliberately reads across cases: the board list, overview heat and pipeline cover the whole board, and the evidence library and wiki are global across missions.
