- **Deconfliction** is a safety function and crosses cases within the tenant: `POST /deconflict {identifierKeys}` matches `Target.identifierKeys[]` and returns Recon's `TargetOverlap` (`ops/contracts.ts`): id, label, anchor, priority, assessment, disposition, caseRef, `shared` (the matched request keys as {type, value}, echoed from the request, up to 12) and `sharedCount`, sorted by sharedCount descending and capped at 20 as `overlapsFor` does, audited. Round 4: a 7-digit phone is sent and stored as both its raw-digit key and the city-inferred 10-digit key (2.1), because board snapshots carry no city and Recon's `identKey` keeps 7 digits, so SPORK's overlap set is a superset of Recon's. The dossier uses it for its overlap caveat and shows `shared` as the reason to deconflict. Returning `anchor` across cases matches Recon today; withholding it across cases would be an owner decision (9.2 Q14).
- Recon's service user gets **no** raw SQL, Cypher or MCP access. `/api/v1/command` and the MCP plugin's execute-command are disabled for that user, and the MCP plugin is off in production.
- Per-database users and per-type grants (`security/SecurityManager`, `server/security/ServerSecurity`) are the backstop. They bind only if the plugin sets the role user in `DatabaseContext` on every route (5.1, Engine user binding); otherwise no permission check runs at all.
- Year 2 (E13): engine row filters in `query/select/SelectExecutor` and `query/sql/executor/FetchFromTypeExecutionStep`, 8 ew.

### 5.4 Redaction

- **Two service roles, selected by the OC's `role` claim.** The **console** role reads `txt_*` (search rows with titles, `get_record` text, the q filter), purpose-gated and audited by data class (5.1, 5.2; round 4 makes 3.1 and this section agree: console search rows are audited, one entry per request). The **ops** role (ops APIs, dossier, packages, watch scans, missions, overview, board) has **no grant** on `txt_*` and is refused title and text projections on the shared routes before the engine is touched. The boundary is enforced only because each route binds the claim's user in `DatabaseContext` (5.1); the WS3 tests (an ops route reading `txt_*` throws, a shared route with a title projection under `ops` returns 403, a new period carries the ops grant) gate it. Ad titles and text live only in `txt_*` (both tiers until E17; after E17 warm text moves to an object-store bucket with its own policy), and in encrypted backups (5.6).
- **Stylometry without text.** An exact float32 copy of the style vector (`style[24]`, 96 B) sits on Record, computed per record from the exact `article_body` at ingest (2.1), so the dossier's fuzzy link clusters and the authorship detector run under the ops role with the same values Recon uses: Recon clusters greedily at cosine 0.95 (`analysis/style.ts`), so an INT8 copy would move pairs near the threshold and break dossier contentHash parity.
- **Missions.** Lead `sourceRefs` hold `{provider, recordId, origin, textHash}` and never the title (2.1): WS4 changes `targeting.ts` to emit the `textHash` from the ops hydrate projection, the missions UI resolves titles at view time through the console role, and WS10's import of legacy missions (whole Mission JSON documents, leads included, in Recon's SQLite) replaces every `sourceRefs[].title` with its `textHash` before anything lands in `identity`. Round 4: without this, the import would have put plaintext ad titles where the ops role reads, and new leads built over an ops hydrate would have silently fallen back to the record id.
- **Markings.** Beyond the two roles, every object, property value and link carries markings (2.7) that the query builders enforce as predicates and projection masks; `Face.gallerySlot`, `Record.styleVec`, `Image.faceCode` and the audit display copies carry tighter markings than their objects.
- **By construction.** Ops APIs, packages and dossier exports are built from `identity`, `rec_*` and `media_*` with per-endpoint projection allowlists, so they cannot carry ad text.
- **Images and crops** appear only as hash references that need a separate purpose-gated, audited fetch.
- **Text access** is its own purpose-gated, audited procedure.

### 5.5 Retention, legal hold and deletion propagation

`RetentionPolicy` and `LegalHold` live in `identity`. A hold reaches records through case, then subjects, then identifier `holdCount > 0`.

A deletion request, or the daily expiry job, writes a `DeletionOrder` into the retention ledger in `ops`. A leased propagation worker then runs ordered, idempotent steps. Each step writes a per-projection acknowledgement:

1. **Hold check** with a linearizable read. Held subjects are skipped and reported.
2. **Tombstone.** `Record.retentionClass=pending_delete` is committed with an audit entry and hidden from all reads at once.
3. **`rec_*` / `txt_*`.** Delete the Record, and its TextBody when no other record references it. FULL_TEXT, jvector and `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` entries (S-TEXT codes) go through index maintenance in the same transaction, which works because `text` is stored on the TextBody (0.4). If the partition is `aging`, the order is also recorded for replay into `txt_<p>_w<n>`, and the `txt` acknowledgement waits for the switch (3.5). `.vecgraph` nodes are purged on the next bucket rebuild, which is forced within 7 days for biometric-related deletes; IVF postings drop the ordinal through `LiveVectorBitsFilter` at once and rewrite it on the posting's next split or merge.
4. **`identity` derived aggregates.** Decrement `recordCount`, `CO_OCCURS.weight`, `dayCells`, `topCells` and `periodMask`, and the Presence rows and (identifierKey, dayBucket) counters in `ops`. Delete identifiers that reach 0. Mark affected components for the nightly rebuild, and recompute Identity membership through a ResolutionDecision. Without this step, deleted records would still count in detectors and group-by.
4b. **Case layer and Recon-derived state (round 4).** The subject's keys are also copied into `Target.identifierKeys[]`, `Target.snapshot` (identifiers, places, sources), `Watch.sources[]` and the per-anchor baseline, `Alert.anchor`, `Mission.seeds[]` and lead `sourceRefs[]` (record keys, textHash), `Feedback` keyed by anchor, `ObjectSetSnapshot` members and `AnalystQueueItem` subjects. The default is **redaction**: remove the subject's keys and record ids from each of these in one audited transaction per document, mark affected targets, watches and missions `subjectDeleted=true` with the order id (the UI shows the gap, the analyst's own notes and assessment stay), and drop alerts and queue items that have no other subject; the `AuditAccess` rows keyed by the subject's record keys are deleted with the audit display copies (5.2), the hashed roots stay. The alternative, declaring case-layer work product exempt under a named `RetentionPolicy`, is an owner decision (9.2 Q5); if the owner signs it, the exemption and the documents it covered are recorded in the completion certificate instead. v1 audit subjects stay as acknowledged in 5.2.
5. **`media_*`.** Delete the Face rows (with their S-FACE codes), the Tattoo rows, the Image when it has no references left (with its PDQ hash and S-IMG codes), and the OCR-derived identifiers whose only provenance was that image. Recompute FaceCluster and PDQ-cluster representatives.
6. **ROC.** Add a manifest tombstone, which every roc-agent filters at once. The segment is rewritten without the template within 7 days. roc-agents acknowledge, and replay by `outboxSeq` guarantees that a restarted agent applies the delete.
7. **Object store.** Delete the original (if SPORK-owned) and crops (and, after E17, the warm text object, once its postings are removed), and purge old object versions.
8. **Backups.** Biometric segment files and template-bearing backups are encrypted with **monthly biometric data keys**. Destroying a month's key (crypto-shredding) removes those biometrics from older backups. Other personal data in backups expires with backup retention, which is the documented deletion lag: 7 days on the encrypted mount before W3d (7 daily archives, no weekly copies), 35 days at most after W3d (weekly copies in the object store).
8b. **Shadow-diff replay set (round 4).** The record-replay store the WS4 harness builds (frozen upstream responses with identifiers and ad text) is a store of real personal data: it lives in the object store under SSE-KMS with its own retention class (deleted at cutover plus 30 days), every read of it is audited, and a subject deletion order reaches it through this step (the replay files are keyed by the record ids they contain). It is never copied to a laptop.
9. **Completion certificate.** A signed certificate (a hash of the executed steps) and a hash-only deletion manifest are written to audit.

When a whole period expires, its `rec_<p>`, `txt_<p>` and `media_<p>` databases are dropped and the partition's data key is destroyed. 28 CFR Part 23, if the owner finds it applies to case-scoped intel (9.2 Q19), adds per-record criminal-predicate and reasonable-suspicion fields, a 5-year review-or-purge date that the expiry job enforces, and dissemination logging, all of which fit `RetentionPolicy`, the Record `_prov` struct and the audit chain as designed; the shared ad corpus would stay outside it as open-source material.

### 5.6 Encryption at rest

- **From day 1:**
  - LUKS (dm-crypt) under LVM: each NVMe drive is a LUKS container that is a physical volume of the one SPORK volume group (0.6), so the Raft and WAL directories, the databases, the ROC segments and the staging areas are all encrypted, and a drive added by H1 is formatted, opened and `pvcreate`d before `vgextend`. Keys are KMS-held and released to the initramfs (clevis/tang or a TPM-sealed keyfile), never on the node's own disk.
  - TLS on HTTP, Postgres, Bolt and gRPC, plus Raft mTLS.
  - Object store SSE-KMS with per-partition keys.
  - **Backup mount (Q1 rule).** Stock auto-backup writes plaintext archives (`BackupTask` passes only database, file name, directory, verbosity and compression; the integration `Backup` supports `encryptionKey` but nothing in `server/backup` sets it), and they hold `txt_*` ad text, the plaintext evidence FULL_TEXT and the case layer in `identity`, and Face metadata: about 491 GB per generation at mid today. So the off-node mount must be an encrypted volume with KMS-held keys (LUKS or ZFS native encryption on the export), reached over NFS with krb5p or a TLS gateway, with its own admin separation. WS1 does not enable backups until it is. W3d replaces it with archives encrypted by the integration `Backup` (`encryptionKey` from KMS) in an SSE-KMS bucket.
- **Gallery segments:** AES-GCM with the monthly biometric key, decrypted by roc-agent into ROC RAM.
- **Sensitive properties: envelope encryption in the plugin.** Engine data encryption (`Database.setDataEncryption`) is serializer-wide: once set, every property of every record in that database is encrypted, while index keys are written in plaintext. So it cannot select properties and it leaks indexed values. Instead the plugin encrypts analyst notes, audit display copies and Face slot pointers with AES-GCM under KMS-held envelope keys (about 28 B per value, counted in 0.4 for Face), and never indexes them. 2 ew in WS7.
  - **Evidence is not envelope-encrypted.** Recon's evidence library searches title, excerpt and origin with FTS5 (AND of quoted words, BM25, LIMIT 100), and the excerpt (up to 20,000 characters) is the evidence content. Serving that search needs plaintext tokens, so evidence is indexed in a plaintext FULL_TEXT in `identity` (4) and protected by role grants, tenant scoping, audit and LUKS. A keyed blind index (HMAC of tokens, AND only, no BM25) was rejected because it changes Recon's ranking.
  - A per-property engine opt-in (formerly E12) is a later engine item, sized above 3 ew, only if the plugin approach proves insufficient.
  - Data is compressed before it is encrypted.

### 5.7 Biometric minimization

- **CSAM gate before any vision model (round 4).** Images of possible minors in commercial-sex ads may be CSAM. Every image is hashed (PhotoDNA through the qualified reporting interface where available, PDQ otherwise) and checked against the known-hash lists before YuNet, AuraFace, the scene model or OCR ever see it; a hit sets `Image.csamHashHit`, triggers the 18 U.S.C. 2258A report path, and the image is never embedded, templated or stored beyond the hash and the report record.
- The quality gate drops about 40% of detections, and only templatized faces keep crops.
- Templates live only in ROC gallery segments, never twice: the database holds metadata and `templateSha` only. The S-FACE recall codes (4c) are the one other face-derived artefact: AuraFace-v1 int8 vectors on representatives, envelope-encrypted, deleted with the Face row, never used for a verdict.
- `minorFlag` comes from ROC's age module as a band plus confidence (never a scalar age), thresholded conservatively, and triggers the stricter purpose rule and human review. No open age model ships as a signal (4c.1).
- Face search returns cluster and record references, not other people's crops, unless purpose and role allow it.
- **Default rule:** faces not linked to a case, target or watch for 36 months lose their template and crop. The owner confirms the value (Q5).
- The representative gallery is itself a minimization control for the interactive path.

---

## 6. Ingest

### 6.1 Initial load (600 GB, 290M records)

1. **Export** the upstream OrientDB as NDJSON per class, preserving RIDs, or read it directly with an adapted `integration/importer/OrientDBImporter`.
2. **Transform** in Java, sharing `spork-keys` with the plugin. Steps:
   - Normalize keys, compute `style` and `styleVec` per record from the exact `article_body` (the `spork-keys` port of Recon's `identity.ts`, golden vectors), then deduplicate texts by the exact-byte `textHash` (2.1), geocode places from a cache, hash images (SHA-256, pHash, PDQ) after the CSAM gate (5.7).
   - Count identifier occurrences (pass 1, about 200M keys x 40 B = 8 GB off-heap), so that only shared identifiers go to `identity`.
   - Assign periods by `ts_date_posted` (falling back to `ts_date_collected`) and by size, and write the `recdir` entries.
   - Records older than the hot window are built directly in warm form (no vector index), so the initial load needs no aging rewrite.
   - Every schema object is created through the plugin's schema installer, so the 16 KB page sizes of 0.4 apply to the offline build too.
3. **Build offline on n4** with embedded `GraphBatch` (no Raft), **always `withWAL(true)`** (round 5): the GraphBatch Javadoc recommends `withWAL(false)` for imports, but the read-bench worktree measured that the WAL-off path retains about 8 KB of heap per record until the JVM dies (a 24 GB heap failed at `close()` on 5M records, 12 GB filled at 2M; the suspect is `TransactionContext.commit2ndPhase` to `PageManager.publishPages` when the commit has no WAL ticket), so a build following the engine's own advice cannot finish in the 12 GB slice. `identity` (vertices, then light edges and CO_OCCURS, dayCells), `recdir`, each `rec_<p>`, `txt_<p>` and `media_<p>`. Indexes are built after the load. **Measured rates** (`workstreams/retrieval-bench.md`, WAL on): 30.5M elements (5M records, 6M identifiers, 19.5M edges) loaded in 115 s, about 265K elements/s; index builds 622 s per 5M records, of which the style vector graph 413 s. For 290M records that is about 1.8B elements in 1.9 hours of load, 3.4 hours of non-vector index builds (209 s per 5M, linear) and 1.3 to 2.5 hours of style graphs over the 55M hot unique texts only (warm partitions are built without a vector index, step 2, so the hot partitions are the long pole and the graph build scales a little worse than linearly): about 6 to 8 hours on n4, replacing the round-4 plan of 10 to 35 hours at 50K to 150K elements/s. The 10% rehearsal in P0 confirms it, and the **P0 gate** is a 10M-record GraphBatch load with WAL on inside the 12 GB slice that finishes with a flat live heap after GC; if WAL off is ever wanted for its write-amplification saving on n4, the heap retention is first a small engine item (find and release the publication path's reference), never a build setting.
4. **Verify** per-type counts, `upstreamRid` round trips and checksums.
5. **Seed only n1.** Copy the build (about 905 GB at mid before faces) from n4 to n1 with rsync, about 12 to 16 minutes at 10 GbE, then delete it on n4. n2, n3 and n4 start empty. Pre-staging identical files on every node does not avoid transfer on the stock engine: each bootstrap-state probe has a hard-coded 5 s timeout (`BootstrapElection.probeAttemptTimeoutMs`), each probe makes the peer hash its whole 905 GB (minutes), so a seeded peer never answers in time, is assumed empty and catches up by snapshot anyway, while every 5.5 s re-probe starts another concurrent full hash on it.
6. **Form the cluster from n1 by leader-shipped snapshots.** Start all 4 with `HA_BOOTSTRAP_FROM_LOCAL_DATABASE=true` (only n1 has files, and empty peers answer the probe at once) and the E0 settings: `HA_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT_MS` at 1 h, so the followers' reconcile listing survives n1's full hash pass (8 to 15 minutes), and `HA_SNAPSHOT_MAX_CONCURRENT=3`, so the three followers pull at once and none waits on a retry. The followers then pull about 3 x 905 = 2.7 TB: 45 to 75 minutes of transfer at 0.6 to 1.0 GB/s aggregate, plus the hash pass. With one slot this would fail: the first follower's serial pull (905 GB, 15 to 25 minutes plus open and verify gaps) passes the 930 s retry, both waiters next try at 1,890 s, their last attempt, and one exhausts all 7 attempts, sending an empty follower through `failInstallWhenNoLocalDatabases`, a Ratis retry and another full leader hash pass. P0c rehearses formation at full size. (The alternative, a configurable probe timeout plus a fingerprint cached once at open, is an engine change and would break the Q1 zero-engine-change rule; it is folded into E2 if ever needed.)
7. **Vector graphs.** Each node builds its own, one node at a time (E5's rebuild admission does this automatically once it lands).
8. **Catch up** upstream changes since the export cut through continuous ingest, by `upstreamRid`.

**Face backfill** (the corpus is usable without faces in the meantime).
- **DETECT + REPRESENT:** 435M distinct images x 0.1 core-s = 43.5M core-s = 503 core-days.
- **CLUSTER:** every backfilled face also passes CLUSTER (6.3), whose cost depends on the case/watch gallery size G at backfill time: 218M faces x (2,000 blocking candidates + G) x 0.23 us. Blocking alone is 0.1M core-s (1.2 core-days). The case-gallery term is 12 core-days at G = 20K, 116 at 200K and 1,158 at 2M. So the backfill runs CLUSTER with blocking (a) and (b) only, and the case-gallery check for backfilled faces runs as a separate batched 1:N of the case/watch gallery against each sealed backfill segment, sized by G when it runs (116 core-days at 200K; restricted to victim-identification templates first).
- **Where:** not on n4. ROC is capped at 16 search cores per node, and n4's `roc-work` cgroup has 6, so DETECT alone would take 31.5 days on 16 cores (84 on 6), and DETECT plus a 2M-template case check about 104 days on 16. The backfill runs on a separate burst host: a GPU box for 2 to 4 weeks (Q-ROC2), or a 64-core CPU host for about 8 days of DETECT plus the case-gallery pass. Its results are committed through the normal MediaJob and segment path.
- **Fetching originals:** 435M x 150 KB = 65 TB, which is 14.4 hours at 10 Gb/s or 6 days at 1 Gb/s. That may stress the upstream file store. The S1 encode backfill (4c.4: about 900 GPU-hours of YuNet, AuraFace, scene model, PDQ and OCR) runs on the same burst host over the same fetched bytes, so the originals are pulled once.
- **Order:** case- and target-linked images first, then newest to oldest.
- **Not before H1.** The backfill does not start on any node that has not had its drive installed (0.6).

### 6.2 Continuous ingest

**Volume.**
- Today: 200K/day = 2.3/s average, about 25/s at peak.
- Design: 835K/day on average and 1.5M/day sustained.

**Path.** A `spork-recon` NDJSON route (`POST /ingest`, or a plugin gRPC service) on the leader runs the saga, in micro-batches of at most 500 records or 30 s, which amortizes LSM tail-page deltas in the Raft log (0.4). gRPC `InsertStream` and HTTP `/api/v1/batch` insert rows into one target type and cannot drive the saga, so they are used only for single-database offline loads. Analyst imports (Recon's `/api/ingest`) use the same route with `source='analyst-import'` (2.5).

**Ordered saga.** Each step is idempotent, and a per-batch `IngestCheckpoint` in `ops` records progress:
1. `rec_<p>`: Record by `upstreamRid` or `record_id`, local identifiers, light edges, gram lists; then the `recdir` entry.
2. `txt_<p>`: TextBody upserted by `textHash`.
3. `media_<p>`: Image upserted by sha.
4. `identity`: promotions (2 or more records), CO_OCCURS weights, dayCells, periodMask, counters, streaming union-find, `MEMBER_OF` changes as ResolutionDecisions by rule `hard-key-v1`.
5. Outbox events and the batch audit entry in every database touched.

A reconciler re-drives incomplete batches. P0 chaos tests kill the leader mid-saga.

**Enrichment workers** (stateless) tail the Outbox: geocoding to placeId (and the denormalized city, countryCode and areaCodes on Record), carrier lookup, the ingest priority score (4d, batched 50 records per decider call), image fetch with the CSAM hash gate, sha256, pHash and PDQ (multi-index hashing dedup against the batch and the S-PDQ index; a near-duplicate joins its PDQ cluster and skips S-IMG encoding), then MediaJobs for ROC and for the encoder sidecar (S-FACE, S-IMG, S-TATTOO, OCR, S-TEXT). The style vector and its float32 copy are computed in the ingest saga itself, before dedup (6.1). They run as a 2 GB process inside n4's build slice (1.4), and on n2 and n3 while an n4 build holds that slice.

**Raft log.** At 835K/day, ingest page deltas are expected at 15 to 25 GB/day (0.4), measured in P0a, plus about 8 GB/day of incremental compactions and at most one scheduled full compaction of 27 GB in a day. Snapshots run every 5 minutes (`HA_SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL`), and E0.5 preserves about 24 h of entries behind them, which fits the 120 GB budget.

### 6.3 ROC templatization that survives leader change

**Queue.** `MediaJob(key = imageSha + stage + algo@ver, state, leaseOwner, leaseUntil, attempts, outboxSeq)` lives in `ops`, so the queue is replicated data.

**Stages.**

| Stage | What it does | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| DETECT + REPRESENT | ROC detection, quality gate, template | About 0.1 core-s per image. Today 300K distinct images/day x 0.1 = 30K core-s/day = 0.35 cores. At 3x pace, 1.25M x 0.1 = 1.45 cores. |
| CLUSTER (blocking-based, never a corpus 1:N) | Compare the new face with ROC 1:1 against (a) the representatives of FaceClusters attached to records that share a hard identifier with the new record, capped at 2,000, (b) faces in pHash near-duplicate images, and (c) the case/watch gallery (≤ 2M), which doubles as the watch-alert check. Join the best cluster above the ROC FMR threshold, or start a new cluster with this face as representative. | (G + 2,000) x 0.23 us per face, G = case/watch gallery size: about 0.46 core-s per face at G = 2M. Today 150K faces/day x 0.46 = 69K core-s/day = 0.8 cores. At 3x pace, 626K x 0.46 = 288K core-s/day = 3.3 cores. The face backfill uses a different split (6.1). |
| ENROLL | Append to a local staging segment, then commit Face rows with `state=staged` | |

The CLUSTER stage replaces the pragmatic proposal's per-face 1:N against all exemplars, which costs about 31 cores continuously (65M x 0.23 us = 15 s per face x 180K faces/day). Because blocking cannot merge the same person across unrelated components, the representative ratio is assumed at 1 per 2.5 faces (A9), not 1 per 4. The exhaustive job restores full recall.

**Workers.** Workers on all nodes (n4 weighted) run in the `roc-work` cgroup (6 cores on n4, 2 on n1 to n3), never in the 16-core `roc-search` cgroup, so DETECT (up to 1.45 cores at 3x pace) and CLUSTER (3.3 cores) never slow the interactive fan-out (4). They claim jobs with a conditional update on the leader (5-minute lease) **in batches of 50 claims and 50 completions per transaction** (round 4: unbatched, the 1.25M DETECT and 626K CLUSTER jobs a day at 3x pace are 43 Raft entries/s, which passes the 10,000-entry stale-follower threshold in 230 s of an admission stall; batched they are 0.9/s), fetch the image from the object store, run ROC locally, and write results idempotently (unique Face key). At 3x pace the 4.75 worker cores fit n4's 6 plus 6 more on n1 to n3.

**Segment sealer.** One sealer, elected by a lease in `ops` and not tied to Raft leadership, seals full segments. It uploads them and, in one transaction, commits the manifest entry plus `Face.state=searchable`.

**Leader change.** In-flight commits fail and are retried, and expired leases are reclaimed. A template is never duplicated and never lost.

---

## 7. Recon integration

### 7.1 Protocol

- HTTPS JSON with NDJSON streaming, from the `spork-recon` ServerPlugin under `/api/spork/v1/{tenant}/...`.
- An OpenAPI contract generates the TypeScript client package for Recon (Bun).
- gRPC only for bulk ingest and exports. Bolt and Cypher only for admins.
- **Headers:** `X-Spork-Context` always; `X-ArcadeDB-Read-Consistency` and `X-ArcadeDB-Read-After` where needed (handled by the plugin itself, 3.1).
- **Every response** carries `consistencyBookmark`, `servedBy` and `partial`.
- **In Recon**, a new `SporkSource` implements the `ReconSource` seam (`engine.ts`, next to `LocalSource` and `ApiSource` in `api-source.ts`). The `ReconSource.all()` full-scan path is retired:
  - **`SporkSource` is a materializing per-session cache (round 4)**, as `ApiSource` is: `ReconSource.get` is synchronous and `Engine.search`, `pivot`, `recordsBySocial`, `recentLocated` and `totalRecords` call `source.all()`, so `SporkSource` prefetches through `/intake`, `/search`, `/pivot` and `/records:hydrate` (the `tools.ts` prefetch hooks, extended from search, pivot and get_record to every tool that resolves records) and serves `get(id)` and the set analyses from the cache. The Engine methods rewritten to async SPORK calls in WS4 are `search`, `pivot`, `recordsBySocial`, `recentLocated`, `totalRecords`, `resolveSource` and `resolve`; the set analyses (`cluster`, `timeline`, `geoAnalyze`, `commonalities`, `linkClusters`, `detect`) keep running over the cached records.
  - `resolve(ids)` and `get(id)` call `POST /records:hydrate` (at most 5,000 ids per call, paged above that), which returns Recon's analysis projection plus `textHash`.
  - `arc`, `imageOf` and `phoneLookup` (the `tools.ts` `arc` and `phone_lookup` tools, which today call `ApiSource`) call `POST /arc/search` and `GET /phone/lookup` (7.2), so the victim-ID tool does not silently report that it needs the live RedLight API.
  - Most of Recon's analysis runs over an arbitrary set of record ids: cluster, timeline, geoAnalyze, commonalities, linkClusters, `detect()` (about 25 detectors), rollupTargets, rankRecords, tree, dashboard, simplify, compare and mission lead generation. Each either keeps running in Recon over hydrated records, or calls a set-scoped endpoint with `{ids[]}` that reproduces its rules. The choice is per module, behind `SPORK_*` feature flags, starting with "keep in Recon over hydrate".
  - **Stays in Recon, by decision.** Dossier assembly (`ops/dossier.ts` buildDossier with detect, indicatorsFor, nextActionsFor, caveats, seeded commands and hashDossier), watch-scan scoring (`resolve-targets.ts`: linkClusters, detect, rollupTargets, applyModel) and mission lead generation (`missions/targeting.ts` generateLeads) are about 2,700 lines of TypeScript (`detectors.ts` with 27 detectors and compiled specs, `analysis/*`, `model.ts`, `ops/indicators.ts`, `ops/package.ts`). A Java port would have to keep dossier contentHash byte-identical and track every later detector change. SPORK supplies capped footprints (`POST /dossier:footprint`), hydrate, leases, persisted state and audit; Recon runs the pipelines. A SPORK-side port is not planned; if it is ever required, it is a separate sized workstream (5 to 7 ew) with golden-set parity.
  - Analyst-authored `DetectorSpec` and `ClusterSpec` (including the trusted-only `expr` evaluated with `new Function`) stay in Recon over hydrated sets. The plugin evaluates only declarative specs and refuses `expr`. Specs that outlive a session are stored per analyst or case with audit.
  - **Shadow-diff harness (WS4).** `compare.ts` and `server-compare.ts` are an analyst feature, not a test harness, and Recon has none. WS4 builds one against the right reference. Production Recon serves intake, search, dossier, watch scans and mission targeting through `ApiSource` whenever `TPD_API_KEY` is set (`session.ts`; missions refuse to run without it), and `LocalSource` holds only analyst-imported records, so diffing against `LocalSource` would prove nothing. The harness records real intakes, dossiers and watch scans with a record-replay layer that freezes upstream responses (`rldSearch`, `gss.recent`, record fetches), replays each call through `ApiSource` and `SporkSource`, and diffs rows, signals and dossier contentHash after normalization.
  - **Footprint semantics (decision).** `ApiSource`'s footprint is the upstream token query (`rldSearch({query: token})`, limit 300) unioned with identifier matches over its cache. SPORK reproduces the identifier rule over the whole corpus (identifier keys and grams, newest 300 per token, 2.4), which is a superset of the cache-local identifier matches. Accepted differences, listed per run: records where the token appears only inside ad text and was never parsed as an identifier (upstream full-text hits), the order and choice of the 300 when upstream ranks differently, records not yet synced (ingest lag), and (round 4) the **mission footprint**: Recon's mission collection uses only the upstream per-seed results (`idsForToken`, up to 20 seeds) with no identifier re-match and no RID sibling expansion, so missions moving onto `/intake` gain the identifier rule and siblings; `/intake` takes `expand=false` for callers that want the seed-only footprint, and WS4 picks per mission type which to use and lists the choice in the harness. If the first class is material in P0a, `/intake` adds a full-text arm over `TextBody.text` that returns record ids only, which is an owner decision because it reads `txt_*` on an ops path.
  - The in-memory scan is removed once a recorded sample matches within the accepted differences.

| `POST /records:hydrate {ids[], projection}` (ids ≤ 5,000 per call; upstream RIDs, `record_id`s or `imp:<importedId>`), `GET /records/{id}` | `Engine.resolve`, `Engine.get`, `tools.ts` `get_record`. Projection, all read from the Record document (2.1): phones, emails, socials, businessNames, imageHashes, lat, lon, city, state, country, postedAt, collectedAt, age, services, paymentTypes, clientele, source, viewCount, hasImages, style (fingerprint label, for compare), styleVec, textHash (for mission sourceRefs), priorityScore, markings. Title and text only under the `console` role claim (403 under `ops`). Cold cost: 4 reads (56 KB: two 4 KB HASH bucket pages, a 16 KB `recdir` page, a 32 KB Record page) per upstream RID, 2 (36 KB) per `record_id`, no series walk (HASH, round 6), on the 32-way pool: about 112 MB and 35 ms of I/O for 2,000 upstream RIDs, 72 MB and 26 ms by `record_id` (2.5). ### 7.3 Change feed, subscriptions, detectors

**Outbox.**
- Every governed transaction writes `OutboxEvent(seq, db, type, subjectKeys, ctxHash, auditSeq)` in the same transaction.
- `seq` comes from a per-database counter document updated inside the leader transaction, so there are no gaps.
- The feed is replicated data: it can be replayed from any node and survives leader change. That is unlike `WebSocketEventBus` listeners today, which fire only on the node that ran the transaction.
- `GET /changes` and the WebSocket bridge use a merged cursor (the per-database seq vector).

**Watches keep Recon's contract, and Recon keeps the scan.** A Watch is a set of source tokens plus an actionability threshold. A scan re-resolves the sources (capped per token at 300, as Recon does), runs the full detector battery, rollupTargets and applyModel, and diffs target scores against the stored per-anchor baseline. Alerts are `threshold` on the first scan, `new` when an anchor first appears above threshold, and `escalated` at a +15 score delta. Scans run on demand, on `RECON_SCAN_INTERVAL`, and when an Outbox event (delivered over SSE) touches a watch's sources or footprint; the Outbox is only the trigger. The scan executes in Recon over `/intake` and `/records:hydrate`, under a per-watch lease from SPORK (TTL, one holder across Recon instances) and the watch's stored OC with actor `system:watch-scan` (5.1); Recon writes the result back with `PUT /watches/{id}/baseline`, and SPORK appends `watch.scan` and stores alerts in one transaction. `Alert` lives in `identity`, capped at 500 per tenant.

**Corpus-wide detectors** run as leased jobs that consume the Outbox incrementally:
- convergence on each newly located record (dayCells and Presence)
- spikes from 30d vs 90d counters
- recurring phones
- a new face matching the case/watch gallery (from the CLUSTER stage)

Their results are `Alert` documents plus Outbox events, pushed to Recon over SSE.

**Mission automation.** Recon's `LeadScheduler` keeps running `generateLeads`, but each run first takes the mission's lease from SPORK (`POST /missions/{id}/lease`, keyed by mission id, TTL 10 minutes). The lease replaces Recon's in-process mutex (`MissionService.active`, 409 "already has work in progress") and is shared by analyst-started runs and the scheduler, across Recon instances. SPORK persists `nextRunAt`, `intervalMinutes` and `consecutiveFailures`, so an interrupted run is retried after its lease expires. **Restart recovery is lease-aware (round 4):** `MissionStore` marks every `running` iteration `interrupted` on process start today, which with several Recon instances would interrupt runs another instance holds; WS10 changes it to interrupt only iterations whose SPORK lease has expired. Each run uses the mission's stored OC with actor `system:mission-targeting` and appends `mission.targeting` (5.1).

**Set watches (R14).** A saved ObjectSet with `watch=true` is re-materialized when an Outbox event touches its sources and diffed against its last snapshot (4b.1); added and removed members become an `Alert` of kind `set.changed` with the diff, so the link-analysis platform's watchlists and Maltego-style perpetual machines run on the same Outbox trigger as Recon's watches.

---

## 8. Phased build plan

**Planning basis (round 9).** The owner directed on 2026-11-03 that human engineering time is not a planning constraint: the engine and plugin work is built by Claude sessions in worktrees. So this plan has no headcount, no capacity totals and no staffing dates. Sizes stay in the tables in engineer-weeks (ew) only as a measure of scope and review weight (about 234 ew in all, round 8). Work is built in parallel from week 1, and **when it lands is set by six kinds of gate**:
1. **Measurements:** Phase 0 (weeks 1 to 4) fixes the schema numbers, the IVF posting size and codes, and the face scenario.
2. **External answers (P0d):** the ROC EULA position (Q-ROC1 to 5), the object store (9.2 Q4), purpose gates (Q10), DINOv3 and TraffickCam terms (Q20, Q21), GPU and decision-model hosting (Q22, Q23).
3. **Hardware, fixed (round 10, 0.8):** 4 nodes x 126 GB RAM, the existing CPUs, no GPU; the only change is each 2 TB drive replaced by a 4 TB one (H1 in this plan, a 3,000 GB ceiling). No burst host, no inference box, no H2, no second drive.
4. **Compute and replication windows:** encode and face backfills in CPU core-days on the 16 encode cores (`budget.py`: 9.4 days for S-PDQ and S-TEXT, 17.3 for S-FACE, 17.9 for S-IMG on the hot window; ROC DETECT 42 days on the 12 `roc-work` cores; no corpus OCR backfill), and one admission or re-archive at a time, off peak, under the `transient-slot` lease (3.5).
5. **Data deadlines from the model** (mid, `budget.py`): `identity` passes 120 GB at month 3.5 (2027-02-15, E1); the face-free footprint crosses 1,560 GB at month 3.7 (2027-02-21, H1); the hot tier reaches its cap and the first partition ages at month 4.7 (2027-03-22, WS7); ROC representatives pass 205M at month 15.5 (2028-02-15; with H2 void, Q-ROC1 or restricted representatives); on 4 TB with combination A the footprint reaches the 2,400 GB trigger at month 8.9 (about 2027-07) and the 3,000 GB ceiling at month 14.9 (about 2028-01), so E26 (fewer copies, decided as plan C) must be in production by then (0.8).
6. **Integration risk:** everything lands on the engine Recon runs on, so work lands in **release trains**, each accepted by the shadow-diff harness (WS4) and, for anything that touches replication, by a P0c-style drill before Recon's flags move. Train 1 is Recon's move with the System 1 first spaces (Q1); train 2 is everything gated only by Phase 0, P0d, the 4 TB expansion and its drills (Q2, as a series of releases); train 3 is the deeper replication and format work that should follow train 2 into production (Q3); the rest is triggered by the model or by an owner decision, not scheduled.

**Rules:** engine work never blocks a Recon feature already shipped, and Recon's cutover never waits on engine work it does not use. Within a train, **retrieval speed first** (the owner's directive 1), then Recon impact, then replication robustness decides what goes first when items compete for an admission window, a drill or a release.

**The format rule (round 8, kept).**
- **Additive formats may ride train 1; changes to an existing format may not.** E18 adds a new index type behind the closed `Schema.INDEX_TYPE` enum and its own component file; no existing database, index or bucket changes layout. E8 (block-max FULL_TEXT postings), E23 (per-edge-type adjacency) and M20 (the 256-slot bucket format) change structures Recon reads, so they land by `REBUILD INDEX` or new partitions, behind their own settings, once the shadow-diff harness accepts them.
- **Recon's cutover never waits on E18.** The System 1 routes (`/similar/{space}`, the fused search legs) sit behind `SPORK_S1_*` flags. If the gate fails or E18 is not ready, the owner chooses between holding Recon's move for the index (the directive) and cutting Recon over with the index following behind its flags (R33).
- **A downgrade path exists.** Once a space is created, a binary without E18 cannot open the partitions that hold it, so any rollback below the E18 release first runs `DROP INDEX` on the S1 spaces (they are regenerable: S-PDQ from `Image.pdq`, the others by re-encoding, 4c.1).

**Already done (rounds 4 and 5).** Four engine workstreams are implemented and benchmarked in worktrees and are not in the sizes below except for their merge: geo kNN, metre radii, Cypher geo seeks and in-transaction visibility (`geo-knn`, was E9 at 6 ew); per-field analyzers, the `ngram`, `edge_ngram`, `reverse`, `phone` and `phonetic` analyzers and automaton fuzzy seek (`partial-text-search`, was E11 at 6 ew); **BM25 top-K scoring** (`FullTextTopK` in the same `partial-text-search` worktree, switch `arcadedb.fullText.topK` at database scope, default true: one pass over each term's postings into primitive arrays, about 4x on 1M documents, no format change; the half of the old E8 that needed no new layout, round 5); the `VectorSearchPool` parallel bucket search, `NeighborMerge` and `VectorRebuildPermits` (`vector-parallel-search`, was E7 at 4 ew). Their merge (conflict resolution, one regeneration of the Studio function reference, `REBUILD INDEX` of any FULL_TEXT index with `<field>_analyzer`, the `parallelSearch` and `fullText.topK` database settings) is 1 ew in Q2. The read benchmark worktree (`read-bench`, `workstreams/retrieval-bench.md`) supplies the measured GraphBatch rates of 6.1 and the WAL-off heap finding. **Round 7 adds a fifth done engine item:** native record versioning (`native-versioning` worktree, E25 in 3.6, `08-native-versioning-design.md`; 284 tests green, uncommitted): `ALTER TYPE x VERSIONED true`, the engine-owned `_txFrom` stamp, the `_VersionHistory` store written in the same transaction, the `history.of`, `history.asOf`, `history.edges` and `history.purge` functions and `RecordVersioning.of(db)`. Its merge is 1 ew in Q2; the follow-up slices are E25c (stamp seeding on open, 0.5 ew) and E25b (`history.scan`, 1 ew) in train 2 and the optional E25d (delta images, 2 ew) when triggered (round 9). The merges stay in train 2, not train 1, for integration reasons: the day-1 configuration bundle (`config/`) and the Q1 identity path are built on the stock engine plus E0, and the per-field FULL_TEXT indexes are rebuilt once at the merge.

**The IVF recall gate (rounds 8 and 9: it passes or fails inside Phase 0, by the end of week 4, and the first spaces are built to its answer).** It runs on the `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` spike already built in a worktree (`worktree-agent-a38ee13437f37dbff`, `docs/spork/workstreams/ivf-vector.md` there, uncommitted: the index type, SQL and Cypher wiring, BINARY or INT8 scan with INT8 rerank, bitmap allow-lists, `HAMMING`, WAL replay tested). On synthetic 512-d data the spike measured recall@10 of 0.763 at nprobe 32 with a 10x rerank oversample and 0.932 at 50x, flat from nprobe 32 to 256: the one-bit candidate pool, not nprobe, bounded recall. So the gate sweeps nprobe (16 to 512), rerank oversample (10 to 200) and items per posting (50 to 200) on the P0b image sample (S-PDQ, and S-FACE codes for the Q2 decision) and the matching text sample (S-TEXT), against exact brute force at k = 100.
- **Pass:** 90% recall at 100 with nprobe at most 128 and oversample at most 50, at no fewer than **66 items per posting at mid (139 at high)**, the smallest posting size at which the centroid heap still fits the 8 GB vector slice at 3x once S-STYLE is on the IVF index (`budget.py`: 7.44 GB at 66 against 2.57 GB at 200 at mid 3x; 7.53 against 5.34 at high). nprobe 128 doubles the scan to 3.2 ms over 4 partitions (9.6 ms over 12), still inside the 4c.3 per-leg budget.
- **Any passing posting size fits from the start:** S-STYLE leaves the slice in Q2 (round 9), so even at the 66-item floor the slice is 6.4 GB at the Q1 exit with the style graphs still resident and 4.1 GB at the Q2 exit (`budget.py`); round 8's rule that a result under 200 pulls S-STYLE forward is moot.
- **Fail (fallback for the first spaces, proposed):** E18 is re-scoped (4-bit or PQ scan codes, which the spike lacks) and re-gated in Q2, and the first spaces ship on what exists. **S-PDQ** answers radius-15 near-duplicates on the hot-window `pdqBands` index of WS12 (already in the footprint, no vector index; the radius-31 corpus-wide search waits for E18). **S-TEXT** runs on the existing `LSM_VECTOR` (jvector, BINARY scan, `searchCacheSize` capped so codes are read through the page cache) at 36 B resident per vector (`workstreams/vector-scale.md`): corpus-wide that is 145M x 36 B = 5.2 GB today, more than the slice holds next to the style graphs, so the fallback covers the newest unique texts that fit, 75M at the Q1 exit (the newest 5.9 months) and 47M from month 4.7 (3.7 months; `budget.py`), with older text reached through BM25 only. **S-FACE** stays ROC-only (the interactive representative gallery and the exhaustive job), which loses the license-clean shortlist, not face recall. E3 (`.vecgraph` in snapshots) then matters for the S-TEXT fallback graphs as it does for S-STYLE.

### Train 1, Q1: Recon onto SPORK with the System 1 first spaces (weeks 1 to 13, 2026-11-02 to about 2027-01-29)

Two tracks built side by side. The **Recon track** is round 7's Q1 and still needs zero engine changes. The **System 1 track** shares no file with it and joins it only through the 4 TB expansion, the WS2 schema and the WS5 ingest hook. The date is set by the gates, not by build effort: P0a (end of week 4), the initial load and leader-shipped formation (6.1), the shadow-diff harness accepting the replayed sample, and, for the index, the 4 TB expansion on every node followed by the first-spaces backfill on the 16 encode cores (round 10: 9.4 days of CPU, S-PDQ 3.1 and S-TEXT 6.3, `budget.py`) and the re-archive of the sealed partitions. **The 4 TB expansion gates every backfill:** without it the face-free footprint plus the first spaces would be 1,558 GB at the Q1 exit at mid (100% of 1,560) and crosses 1,560 GB at month 3.0 (high 0.5, low 4.7), so for the index to be live at the Q1 exit the backfill starts by 2027-01-20 and the expansion is on every node by about 2027-01-15; its hard deadline remains the face-free crossing at month 3.7 (2027-02-21).

**Q1 exit criteria.**
- Recon intake, search, pivot, partial, group-by, feed, board and audit run on SPORK, and Recon's dossier runs in Recon over `SporkSource` (`/dossier:footprint` + hydrate) with contentHash parity (stylometric links included, over the exact float32 `styleVec` computed per record from the exact `article_body`). The shadow-diff harness accepts a recorded sample replayed through `ApiSource` and `SporkSource`, within the listed footprint differences, and the first page returns with p50 under 150 ms.
- **System 1 (round 8):** the IVF gate has passed (or the fallback is in place and E18 is re-gated in Q2); S-PDQ and S-TEXT are built over every partition of the corpus, with every sealed partition re-archived after its codes; `/similar/pdq` and `/similar/text` answer with allow-lists inside the 4c.3 per-leg budget from n2 and n3 and are in the generated TS client behind `SPORK_S1_*` flags; the CSAM hash gate runs before any vision model.
- The classic console, the monitor and media routes, and the missions UI declare an OC with a role; every footprint-bearing route is audited by data class; no `GET /image` appends to the chain.
- Audit lives in the database, with Recon's chain imported as the v1 genesis segment and verified, and Recon's verifier accepting v2.
- The 4 TB expansion is installed on all 4 nodes before the S1 first-spaces backfill (by about 2027-01-15 for the index to be live at the Q1 exit), no SPORK database runs automatic full compaction, and the cluster has been drilled (empty-node replacement and formation at full size, an admission without stale-follower recovery, a 27 GB scheduled compaction).

### Train 2, Q2: faces, the remaining spaces, set-oriented retrieval, governance, replication

Lands as a series of releases through Q2, each behind the harness and, for replication items, a drill; an item a drill holds back moves to the next release, not to a later quarter. Ordered by deadline first (E1 before 2027-02-15, WS7 before 2027-03-22), then by retrieval speed. The CPU face and scene pass is the longest calendar item (round 10: about 35 days of the encode cores for S-FACE and hot-window S-IMG, with ROC DETECT's 42 days on the `roc-work` cores in parallel) and is set by the answers it waits for (P0d), not by engineering. Combination A's savings (0.8) land here: valves 3 and 1 with WS7, valve 5 with WS15b, S-IMG and S-TATTOO on the hot window.

| E5 | Per-index readiness (IVF spaces included), acquisition order, rebuild admission | 4 | a P0c-style drill |
| E3 | Ship `.vecgraph` in snapshots (the case galleries, the S-TEXT fallback graphs if the IVF gate fails, and S-STYLE until it moves; IVF postings need nothing) | 5 | a P0c-style drill |
| E25c | Version-stamp seeding on open (round 7, 3.6), before the P0c-style failover drill is repeated with versioned data | 0.5 | E25 merge |
| E25b | `history.scan(type, t)` with a `txTo` index on the history store (type-level as-of reads for exports and set snapshots; round 7) | 1 | E25 merge |
| CSV export | CSV subgraph export (4b.3) | 1 | WS11 |
| GAV | Registry-only GAV on n4, measured against the capped OLTP path for uncapped k-hop analytics | 1 | WS11 |
| W3d | Backups streamed to the object store | 2 | the object store (9.2 Q4) |
| Cutover | Shadow-run cutover of Recon modules behind `SPORK_*` flags, then of the remaining modules, the link-analysis platform's first consumer of 4b; pace set by the shadow-diff harness, not by build effort | 8 | WS4, the harness |
| Recon S1 panels | Near-duplicate, similar-text and (after WS15b) similar-face panels in Recon over the generated client, behind `SPORK_S1_*` (round 8) | 1 | WS15a |

**Train 2 exit criteria (round 9).** Every System 1 space (S-PDQ, S-TEXT, S-FACE, S-IMG, S-TATTOO, S-DOC, and S-STYLE on the IVF index) serves from n2 and n3 within the 4c.3 per-leg budget, unless P0d has not answered for S-FACE (Q-ROC5) or S-IMG (Q20, Q21), in which case those spaces land in the release after the answer; ROC face search with the exhaustive job runs over the backfilled faces; the ObjectSet service and the graph procedures serve Recon compare; the first partition has aged through the WS7 protocol; E1, E0.5, E2, E14, E5 and E3 have each passed a drill.

### Train 3, Q3: fewer copies of warm data, deeper replication, engine filters and the edge layout

These change code paths every Recon read or the Raft apply loop runs through, so they land one train after E1, E2 and E5 have run in production, each through the P0c drills (R15: upstream ha-raft conflicts are smallest one change set at a time).

| ID | Work | Size (ew, scope) | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| E26 | **Warm partitions on 2 of 4 nodes** (round 10, 0.8; **committed: the owner chose plan C on 2026-11-03**, so "replicate, do not shard" holds for hot data only): warm sealed `rec_<p>`, `txt_<p>` and `media_<p>` leave the Raft group as read-only node-local databases placed by the plugin on one of n2/n3 and one of n1/n4, with deletion batches and aging rewrites admitted to the 2 holders; corpus-wide warm queries fan out to n2 and n3; saves 658 GB per node at month 12 and 1,166 GB at mid 3x; must be in production before combination A reaches the 2,400 GB trigger (about 2027-07) | spike in Phase 0 sizes it | the Phase 0 spike (can a `ha-raft` server serve a non-replicated local database?), WS7 |
| E4 | Listener role, promote playbook | 7 | train 2's ha-raft items in production |
| E16 | Forced installs off the apply thread with the stale-follower exemption | 4 | train 2's ha-raft items in production |
| W3e | Restore-then-join in ha-raft | 5 | W3d, E2 |
| E15 | Replicated commit events | 5 | train 2's ha-raft items in production |
| E13 | Engine row and property filters (markings in `SelectExecutor` and `FetchFromTypeExecutionStep`) | 8 | the WS3 denial tests as its regression suite |
| E23 | Per-edge-type adjacency chains (designed in `docs/2026-10-07-8868`), so typed expansion on supernodes is O(edges of the type) | 6 | the format rule (it changes the edge layout Recon reads) |

### Triggered, not scheduled

| Work | Size (ew, scope) | Trigger |
|---|---|---|
| ROC indexed 1:N (Q-ROC1) or representatives restricted to clusters active in the last 3 years (round 10: H2 is void) | 2 | representatives pass 205M at month 15.5 (2028-02-15) at mid |
| Valves 2 and 4 (non-representative gallery and warm text off node; valve 4 needs E17) | 1 + 3 | an object store exists (9.2 Q4): combination E of 0.8 |
| Retention window (combination D of 0.8; not chosen, the owner chose plan C): the oldest records off node, restored on demand to n4 | 3 | P0b measures high, or E26's spike fails |
| E25d delta or compressed version images, only if P0a measures churn above A17 and the history line needs it (round 7) | 2 | P0a measures churn above A17 |
| DR listeners in a second site (if multi-cluster) | 3 | multi-cluster (9.2 Q1, Q7) |
| Per-partition audit chains (if needed) | 3 | the single chain becomes a write bottleneck |

| The optional LUAR authorship leg; the learned-sparse leg | 2 | validated on labelled operator clusters (4c.1); the sparse leg if P0 justifies it |

**Critical path (round 9): gates, not effort.**
- **Recon's move (train 1, Q1 exit, about 2027-01-29):** P0a by the end of week 4 (with the GraphBatch heap gate), the initial load and formation, and the shadow-diff harness's acceptance.
- **The System 1 first spaces with it:** the IVF gate by the end of week 4 (or the fallback), the 4 TB expansion on every node by about 2027-01-15, then the 9.4-day CPU backfill and the re-archive. Building the index earlier puts its bytes on disk earlier (65 GB at the Q1 exit and 201 GB at the Q2 exit at mid, against none in round 7, `budget.py`).
- **Train 2:** E1 before 2027-02-15 and WS7 before 2027-03-22 (model deadlines); P0d's answers, then W8 and the CPU pass (about 35 days of the encode cores, ROC DETECT 42 days in parallel); S-STYLE onto the IVF index in Q2, which removes the month-12 slice deadline of rounds 5 to 8; combination A's valves.
- **Disk on fixed hardware (round 10, plan C decided):** E26 in production before A reaches the 2,400 GB trigger at about 2027-07, since A alone crosses the 3,000 GB ceiling at about 2028-01 at mid; with E26 mid holds to about 2029-01 (95% at 3x).
- **Later:** ROC indexed search or restricted representatives before 2028-02-15 at mid (H2 is void).

### 9.2 Questions for the owner

1. Are the "4 clusters" 4 servers or 4 multi-node clusters? Same site? What network (1, 10 or 25 GbE)?
2. Drive model (1.92 TB or 2 TB NVMe?), cores per node, and is the OS on the same disk? Round 10: the owner confirmed one 4 TB drive per node replacing the 2 TB (3,600 GB usable, 3,000 GB ceiling, 0.8). Which drive model, and can it be on every node by about 2027-01-15, ideally before the initial formation so no node has to be rebuilt onto it?
3. What drives 3x: new sources, a historical backfill, or media?
4. Round 10: does an S3-compatible object store already exist? The plan is shown with and without one (0.8: valves 2 and 4 and combination E need it; a retention window needs it or upstream RedLight). Is there an S3-compatible object store with Object Lock for originals (203 TB at mid 3x), crops, warm text, segments, backups and WORM anchors?
5. Retention per data class (ads, unlinked faces, evidence, audit)? Who holds legal-hold authority? Is 36 months right for unlinked face templates? When a subject is deleted, should the subject's keys be redacted from targets, watches, alerts, missions and feedback (the default, 5.5 step 4b), or is case-layer work product exempt under a named `RetentionPolicy` the owner signs?
6. Tenancy: one organization with case scoping, or partner tenants? Who administers KMS/HSM keys?
7. RPO and RTO targets. Is a second site required?
8. Is upstream RedLight the long-term source of truth?
9. Are existing face crops or embeddings available, and what are image counts per record (to settle low, mid or high)?
10. Which purpose gates should SPORK enforce at all (Recon enforces none today)? Which purposes may run face and tattoo search, ARC (NCMEC) image search and set-scoped face clustering, and what are the rules for minor-flagged faces?
11. Expected analyst concurrency and target p95 per Recon call?
12. **Retired in round 9.** The round-8 staffing question is withdrawn: engineering time is not a planning constraint; round 10 also withdrew the GPU card count (no GPU). If the IVF gate fails or E18 is not ready at the Q1 exit, does Recon's move wait for the index (the directive) or cut over on its own flags first (8, R33)?
13. Round 10: does an off-node mount already exist? Without one, backups have nowhere off node to go on fixed hardware (5.6), which is a governance gap, not a capacity one. Is there an off-node NFS or object-store gateway mount on an encrypted volume with KMS-held keys for backups from Q1? Under the sealed-once policy (0.4) it needs about 1.8 TB today and 4.1 TB at mid 3x before the S1 index, about 1.9 TB once the first spaces' codes land in Q1 (1,823 + 8 x 7 + 19 = 1,896 GB, round 8, `budget.py`), and about 2.0 TB today and 4.5 TB at mid 3x with every space's codes inside the period databases (from Q2, round 9) (8 daily sets of 197 to 354 GB plus one archive of every sealed database, re-archived after its code backfill); 7 daily archives of everything would need 3.9 and 12.2 TB.
14. Footprint semantics: should SPORK's `/intake` also match a token inside ad text, as upstream `rldSearch` does (a full-text arm returning ids only, reading `txt_*` on an ops path), or keep the identifier rule with the listed differences (7.1)? Should deconfliction withhold another case's target anchor (5.3)?
15. **PATH.** Is "Kaseware PATH" a product or module name seen in a demo or contract? Nothing public on kaseware.com or in third-party listings uses it (`research/intel-platform-data-layer.md` 3); the link-analysis requirements in 2.7 and 4b were taken from Kaseware's public link-analysis, intelligence and platform pages, Gotham/Foundry and Maltego instead.
16. **Marking model** (2.7): flat labels with intersection semantics (Foundry and Gotham portion markings: a reader must hold every marking), or classification plus compartments? Should unheld markings hide existence (Foundry) or redact?
17. **Link markings:** should links carry their own markings independently of their endpoints, as Gotham's create-link `security` block does (2.2), or inherit the stricter endpoint?
18. **Hard caps to adopt** (2.7, 4b): typed expand 100 per link type, page 1,000, facet topN 1,000, 10,000 groups, 3 chained hops, induced subgraph 2,000 nodes, k-hop 10,000 reached nodes, hydrate 5,000 ids, intake 25 tokens. Palantir's and Maltego's defaults are the starting point; which does the platform need higher?
19. **28 CFR Part 23:** does it apply to the case-scoped intel SPORK will hold (criminal predicate and reasonable-suspicion fields, 5-year review-or-purge dates, dissemination logging, 5.5), to the shared ad corpus, or to neither? Kaseware claims adherence for its intelligence module.
20. **DINOv3 license review:** Meta's custom license is royalty-free with no explicit commercial prohibition, but bans military, ITAR and "espionage" uses and lets Meta amend; counsel's read is needed before an OSINT product uses it for S-IMG or room matching. DINOv2 (Apache-2.0) is the substitute.
21. **TraffickCam and Hotels-50K terms:** the repository has no license file; a partnership conversation with TraffickCam (Stylianou, GWU) is the path to the hotel-room matching head, not a download. Who opens it?
22. **Retired in round 10:** no GPU will be added, so encoding runs on the 16 encode cores (0.8) and the decider runs rules plus, at most, a hosted provider (Q23).
23. **Decision-model hosting** (4d.4, R26): is any hosted decision provider acceptable for states without personal data (partition summaries, query routing), or must every decision run on the self-hosted Apache-2.0 decider (about 49 GiB of GPU memory in FP16, one 80 GB card, Q22) and the rules fallback? Is scoring only the newest 300K records a day with the model, and the rest by rules, acceptable until the prefill throughput is measured?
24. **Owner decisions for fixed hardware (round 10, 0.8), each with its numbers:** (a) **decided 2026-11-03: plan C**, E26 with warm sealed partitions on 2 of 4 nodes, so "replicate, do not shard" holds for hot data only and mid holds to about 2029-01 (95% at 3x; 96% without (b)); the retention window (627M of 900M records on node) was not chosen; (b) open: S-IMG and S-TATTOO on the hot window only, which bends "multimodal from day one" for scene and tattoo search over warm images (66 GB at 3x); (c) open: OCR on the daily stream only, case- and target-linked images first at 3x pace, no corpus OCR backfill (192.7 days on CPU); and Q4, whether an object store exists (with one, plan C plus valves 2 and 4 holds to about 2029-07).

### 9.3 Questions for ROC

- **Q-ROC1:** indexed or disk-backed 1:N at 100M to 1B templates: method, RAM per template, latency, licensing.
- **Q-ROC2:** GPU throughput for detection and templates, and licensing for a backfill burst.
- **Q-ROC3:** template compatibility across versions, the migration path, and RAM per template in a gallery.
- **Q-ROC4:** tattoo template size and search cost, and age-estimation accuracy for minors.
- **Q-ROC5:** a written EULA interpretation covering:
  - storing templates in SPORK-managed encrypted segment files, with metadata in a third-party database
  - 1:N over templates loaded from those bytes through the SDK
  - blocking-based 1:1 clustering
  - whether a license-clean shortlist plus ROC rescore counts as "merging"
  - storing calibrated scores for audit
  - per-node licensing for 4 search nodes plus workers
  - a Docker image for `roc-serve`

---

## 10. Decisions and rejected alternatives

### 10.1 Decisions

| # | Decision | Source | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Full replication of everything queried interactively, one Raft group, no sharding; round 10 (owner decision, plan C): for hot data only, warm sealed period partitions on 2 of 4 nodes (E26, D56) | owner, all proposals; owner 2026-11-03 | Owner direction; native SPORK HA; fixed 4 TB hardware cannot hold full replicas to 3x |
| D2 | 3 voters + 1 listener as the target; 4 voters with n4 `replica` in Q1 | P1, P2, P3 | Same fault tolerance, faster commits, n4 off the write path |
| D3 | Period databases (`rec`, `media`) capped at 120 GB and `txt` at the `rec` period boundary (about 73 GB hot); a small `identity` registry holding only identifiers that link 2 or more records; a `recdir` directory for upstream RIDs | P2 base + P1 registry rule + review (round 4 for the `txt` cap) | Bounds resync headroom (not resync volume, see D25). `identity` at 3x is 279 GB instead of 462 GB (322 GB with the round-7 version history of `Identity` and `MEMBER_OF`). A hydrate is two index probes (4 cold 16 KB reads plus a resident series walk) per upstream RID. The `txt` aging rewrite needs source + output + archive on n4: 162 GB at the period boundary, 271 at 120 GB. |
| D4 | Canonical keys are the only cross-database links | P1 (fixes P2's RID flaw) | Links survive rebuilds, restores and backfill builds |
| D5 | One XFS filesystem on LVM. Grow disks by extending the volume, never with separate mounts. | new (fixes P1's mount flaw) | `ATOMIC_MOVE` from `databases/.acquire-<name>` cannot cross filesystems |
| D6 | Plan against 1,800 GB usable and a 1,560 GB ceiling (Raft 20% guard, 10% install reserve). Disk expansion is the plan of record and is due now at mid: H1 installed before any S1 or face backfill (for the index at the Q1 exit, by about 2027-01-18; rounds 8 and 9) and before the face-free footprint crosses 1,560 GB (month 3.7 at mid); the second drive at mid ordered at 3,616 GB (about month 18.8, round 7) | P3 + judges + review (round 4 for the two dates and the second trigger; round 7 for the version history) | Honest: 3x does not fit 2 TB in any scenario on the stock engine, high 3x needs two added drives, and the S1 index plus the version history put mid at 93% of one added drive |
| D7 | Hot tier: newest 18 months capped at 200M records | new (fixes P2's hot-window flaw) | Keeps hot costs bounded whether growth comes from backfill or live sources |
| D8 | Warm `txt` partitions produced by an offline rewrite (no vector index) admitted like a backfill under the aging protocol (new name, replay, switch, drop); warm text stays on node until E17 (valve 4, 183 GB at 3x); warm FTS pruning only as a valve | P2 + review (round 3) | The engine never shrinks a bucket in place, and FULL_TEXT index maintenance needs the stored `text` to remove a deleted ad's postings |
| D9 | Geo index on 8M Place vertices plus a (placeId, tsPosted) composite | P2 | 2.4 GB instead of about 60 GB |
| D10 | Partial search through BY ITEM gram, suffix and phonetic lists from day 1 | P2, P3 | No engine work; the index enum stays closed |
| D11 | dayCells/topCells on identifiers; Presence table for global detectors | P1, P2 | Per-target convergence without touching records |
| D12 | ROC is the face engine and the only verifier; ArcFace buffalo_l retired outright; corpus-wide face **recall** through YuNet (MIT) + AuraFace-v1 (Apache-2.0) in the S-FACE `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` space, ROC 1:1 on every shortlist, nothing ROC-derived in any ANN index | P2, P3 + round 4 (research) | InsightFace weights are non-commercial; a jvector ArcFace index would be 346 GB and 29 GB of heap at mid 3x; S-FACE is 157 GB of disk and 0.83 GB of heap because only int8 centroids are resident; EULA (Q-ROC5) |
| D13 | Representative interactive gallery in RAM split 4 ways and sized for N-1 failover; exhaustive all-face async job for recall | P3 graft + new | Fits RAM; fixes P2's failover overrun and P3's recall loss |
| D14 | Blocking-based clustering (component, pHash, case gallery), never per-face corpus 1:N; set-scoped pairwise clustering for Recon's face scans | new (fixes P3's CPU flaw) + review | 0.8 to 3.3 cores instead of about 31, and Recon's question of who appears across a set is still answered |
| D15 | Gallery segments + replicated manifest + tombstones, outboxSeq replay | P2 + P3 | Immediate deletes, crash-safe enrollment, atomic version switch |
| D16 | Recon's chain imported byte for byte as a v1 genesis segment; SPORK entries use a v2 body that hashes HMAC commitments of subject and detail, with the encrypted display copy outside the hash; `mac = HMAC(K_epoch, hash)` and tenant, epoch and db in a sidecar; per-database chains with per-entry seq, versioned cursor, verification checkpoint, anchors to WORM | P3 + P1 + review (round 2) | Keeps Recon's sha256 chain and lets deletion destroy subject and detail without breaking verification; Recon's verifier gains the v2 rule |
| D17 | All case and governance types in `identity` (one board home, one analyst chain) | P2 (fixes P3's two-homes flaw) | One transaction boundary and one chain |
| D18 | Text in `txt_*`, readable only by the console role; exact float32 style copy on Record for the ops role | P3 + review (round 3) | Redaction by construction without breaking stylometry or search rows |

| D20 | OC as a signed JWT with the analyst bound to the authenticated principal, kept in a plugin ThreadLocal; governed writes pinned to the leader; audited reads append through the plugin's leader route | P2, P3 + review | Governed work keeps its context without E6 |
| D21 | Q1 with zero engine changes; small engine items ordered by Recon impact | P3 | Lowest merge and delivery risk |
| D22 | Offline backfill builds and warm rewrites on n4 in a 16 GB build slice, one database at a time (build, package, upload, delete, then admit), admitted by `ServerControlPlane` restore + auto-acquire off-peak | P1 + review (round 2) | Keeps the Raft log bounded and n4 inside the install reserve; admission stalls follower apply, so it is windowed |
| D23 | Month-bucketed jvector style vectors at most 8M, hot only, `storeVectorsInGraph=true` per index, `arcadedb.vectorIndex.searchCacheSize=500000` as a database setting (round 4: not index metadata), `parallelSearch=true`, rebuild admission via E5 and the `VectorRebuildPermits` ledger | P3 + P1 + review | Sealed buckets never rebuild; graphs and caches stay inside the heap budget; 16 buckets fan out 6 to 8x faster with identical rows |
| D24 | Per-index readiness and acquisition order (E5) | P2 + review | A node serves what it can while it heals; today the order is alphabetical and all-or-nothing |
| D25 | Cold install for large databases (E1) on the per-database resync path, plus a reconcile skip for current databases (E2) | P2 + review | Bounds headroom and stops every log-gap resync from moving the whole node |
| D26 | `HA_SNAPSHOT_COMPRESSION_LEVEL=0`, `HA_SNAPSHOT_MAX_CONCURRENT=3` (one slot per follower; round 3) with 6 retries on a 30 s base for transient errors, `HA_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT_MS` 1 h (1.5 h above 2 TB), `HA_STALE_FOLLOWER_RECOVERY_DURATION_MS` 30 minutes with batched MediaJob writes (round 4), Raft log preservation (E0.5; `HA_LOG_PURGE_UPTO_SNAPSHOT=false` permanently before it, with a 48 h or 80 GB dead-peer rule and compactions suspended while a peer is down) | judges + new + review (rounds 2 to 4) | Single-thread DEFLATE caps resync; retries have no jitter, so one slot serializes followers on the backoff schedule; an admission stall would trip stale-follower recovery into a full-node resync of every follower; `HA_LOG_PURGE_GAP` is not a retention window; the setting is read only at startup; the reconcile listing hashes every database |
| D27 | Batch hydrate and set-scoped endpoints that reproduce Recon's rules; Recon keeps set analysis over hydrated records where no endpoint exists, and always keeps dossier assembly, watch-scan scoring and mission lead generation | review (round 2) | Most Recon analysis runs over id sets; a Java port of about 2,700 lines would have to stay contentHash-identical forever |

| D31 | Hub and stop flags limit only automatic expansion; tenant-only default scoping with an explicit cross-case deconfliction read | review | Recon never suppresses explicit pivots and deconflicts across cases |

| D33 | Denormalized city, countryCode, areaCodes[], display identifier arrays and the style label on Record (96 B per record); 32-way hydrate read pool | round 2 | Hydrate and search rows need no edge, local vertex or Place read, so cold latency targets hold |

Buckets through SQL `CREATE TYPE ... PAGESIZE`, HASH through SQL `UNIQUE_HASH` / `NOTUNIQUE_HASH`, only FULL_TEXT and GEOSPATIAL through `IndexBuilder.withPageSize` | round 3 + round 4 + round 6 | The engine reads whole pages; a v0 bucket page spends 8,202 B on header and slots, so 16 KB Record pages hold 13 rows (97 GB per partition) against 40 at 32 KB (63 GB); a HASH lookup is 3 cached touches and 1 cold read with no series, bloom or compaction state, against 1 + 2S + 2 touches for LSM (S = 519 to 1,609 for `recdir` at 16 KB); a 2,000-id hydrate moves 112 MB against 131 MB (round 4) and 1.3 GB (stock) |
