| D42 | **Ontology contract** (2.7): ObjectType, PropertyType and LinkType with two-sided names as registered schema data in `identity`; the abstract `Identifier` as an interface; markings on objects, property values and links enforced by the plugin's query builders (E13 later); a `_prov` struct on asserted values and every Record; asserted values on versioned objects updated in place with the engine keeping the history, `Assertion` overlays only for non-versioned subjects (round 7, D54); typed write actions; caps with `truncated` and `totalAtLeast` instead of timeouts | round 4 (owner directive 3, `research/intel-platform-data-layer.md`) | The platform on top is Gotham, Foundry and Maltego class and asks for an ontology; SPORK's types already fit, so this is data plus plugin code, no engine change |
| D43 | **Winner-key merges:** a merge mints a fresh Identity key that owns every post-merge write; inputs keep their histories (in `_VersionHistory` since round 7, D54); `canonicalKey`, `winnerKey` and flattened `otherKeys[]` on the decision | round 4 (Gotham resolution metadata) | A split returns each input intact and leaves post-merge writes with the winner for reassignment |
| D44 | **ObjectSet service** (4b.1): Roaring64 bitmaps keyed by definition hash and the caller's markings, union, intersect, subtract, project, page and count, a 1 GB LRU per read node, saved sets and snapshots in `identity`; Recon compare's four modes run on it as Recon computes them (records: 12-field all-way intersection with `sharedMask`; entities and sets: per-group aggregates and pairwise overlap lists; faces: `/face/cluster` over the capped union with `crossGroup` from the union of labels), and the n-way shared and distinct algebra is a SPORK extension (round 5); set watches diff snapshots | round 4 (owner directive 2) + round 5 | Set algebra on bitmaps is sub-millisecond; the cost is one hydrate per new set; every modality answers "over this set" through the same allow-list; `compare.ts` never computes n-way shared members, so parity needs Recon's own operations |
| D45 | **Fifteen read operations with budgets** (4b.2) served by new `graph.*` procedures (`expandTyped` with per-type caps and the `collapsed` block, `degrees`, `commonNeighbors` of 2 to 4, `khopCounts`, `pathsTyped`, set-input `induced`, `history` over the engine's `history.*` functions since round 7) over OLTP edge lists that hold live edges only (no `validTo` filter, round 7) with hub exclusion on n2 and n3; no database-wide GAV; a registry-only GAV is a year-2 measurement | round 4 | Palantir and Maltego front ends ask for typed, bounded, set-valued reads; a 10K-node cap makes the OLTP path 30 to 60 ms and needs no 19 GB CSR |
| D46 | **System 1 multimodal index** (4c): one `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` posting-list index per embedding space (S-IMG, S-PDQ, S-FACE, S-TEXT, S-STYLE, S-TATTOO, S-DOC) in each period database, sharing one global int8 codebook per space held once on heap (36 B location and ordinal + code + 64 B graph share per centroid), postings packed many per LSM page and replicated through Raft, a corpus-wide probe costing `partitions x nprobe` posting reads with decider partition pruning as the 3x mitigation, 1-bit or PQ scan with 4-bit or int8 rerank, structured constraints as allow-list bitmaps never embedded, RRF then calibrated fusion, System 2 verifiers behind every shortlist; 453 B per record and 2.6 GB of heap at mid 3x; online from Q1 after H1 (rounds 8 and 9: S-PDQ and S-TEXT with Recon's move, the other spaces in Q2), with S-STYLE onto the index in Q2 and the recall gate a Phase 0 pass/fail with a fallback (rounds 5 and 8) | round 4 (`research/system-one-multimodal-index.md`) + round 5 | Graph ANN keeps 36 to 108 B per vector on heap and stalls at 1% filters; IVF keeps about 1 B per item resident and takes arbitrary RID allow-lists, which is what Recon sets need; the engine never queries across databases, so the index follows the partitions |
| D47 | **Model set** (4c.1): SigLIP 2 or PE-Core (Apache-2.0) for scene and cross-modal, PDQ (BSD) for near-dups, YuNet (MIT) + AuraFace-v1 (Apache-2.0) for face recall, gte-multilingual-base + the OpenSearch sparse encoder (Apache-2.0) for text, LUAR-MUD (Apache-2.0) for style, PP-OCRv5 (Apache-2.0) for OCR with deterministic normalizers; DINOv3 and Hotels-50K pending owner questions 20 and 21; no open age model | round 4 (`research/embedding-models.md`) | Every model is commercially usable without negotiation; InsightFace, EdgeFace, AdaFace, ImageBind, jina-v3 and SPLADE-v3 are not |
| D48 | **System 1 decision layer** (4d): a pluggable decider interface (state plus typed Noul, Choice and Score questions to probabilities) with the rules fallback and the self-hosted Apache-2.0 Decider as the default providers and Jev optional; used for query intent routing, partition pruning, ingest priority, the entity-resolution fast pass with an analyst middle band, shortlist rerank and prefetch; `DecisionLog`, `OutcomeFeedback`, `ThresholdPolicy` and `AnalystQueueItem` as tables; thresholds as data; never the sole gate on a safety-critical action | round 4 (`research/system-one-decision-models.md`) | Deciders decide where to look and what to trust, indexes do the looking; the interface is a commodity, so design to it; hosted inference on CHT data is a constraint |
| D49 | **Audit and purpose gating by data class** with one batched entry per request (Merkle root of the returned keys in the hashed body, plus the returned key list persisted outside the hash in a keyed `AuditAccess {db, seq, recordKeyHmac}` side table written in the same transaction, round 5) on every route that returns identifier footprints or record projections, and **per-view image grants** instead of per-fetch audit; export and knowledge actions in the completion allowlist; filtered totals by count pushdown; `GET /audit?record=` and `?analyst=` | round 4 + round 5 | `/intake` and `/pivot` are the same read in Recon; a face view is up to 960 image fetches, which per fetch would saturate the chain head at 320 commits/s against 1.7/s by data class; a root alone cannot answer who viewed record X or what analyst Y disseminated, which 28 CFR Part 23 and CJIS logging and deletion propagation need |
| D50 | **Role claim in the OC** (`console` or `ops`) asserted by the gateway per Recon module selects the engine user; ops is refused title and text projections before the engine; the ops user is granted by exact database name as an audited step of period creation and admission | round 4 | The shared routes serve both roles; engine grants match exact names or `*` only |
| D51 | **Exact-byte `textHash`** and per-record `style` and `styleVec` from the exact `article_body` at ingest before dedup; A5 becomes the measured exact-match unique fraction (+150 GB at mid 3x per +0.1) | round 4 | Recon's style vector counts case and punctuation; normalized dedup would merge texts whose vectors differ and break contentHash parity |
| D52 | **Set-scoped geo and graph hydrates and dossier footprints on n4** (round 6, as `02-retrieval-speed.md` routes P9c and P11); interactive hydrates on n2 and n3 | round 4, reversed in round 6 | With HASH point lookups the resident index tops are under 0.25 GB of directories plus 0.44 to 1.3 GB of LSM composite tops, which n4's 4 GB cache holds; round 4 had routed them to n2 and n3 because the LSM bloom and series-root set (1.2 to 3.8 GB) would have filled n4's cache |
| D53 | **Retrieval speed is the ordering criterion** for every quarter (then Recon impact, then replication robustness): ObjectSet service and graph procedures ahead of E2 and E14; `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` and the first spaces in Q1 by the owner's directive of 2026-11-03, the remaining spaces, E8 and W3d in Q2 (round 9: engineering time is not a constraint, so work lands when its gates allow); E7, E9 and E11 adopted as done | round 4 (owner directive 1) | Read p50/p99 and read throughput outrank write throughput and storage efficiency |
| D54 | **Native record versioning** (round 7, 2.6, 2.7, 3.6 E25, `08-native-versioning-design.md`): `Identity`, `MEMBER_OF` and the narrowed `Assertion` are system-versioned types (`ALTER TYPE ... VERSIONED true`); the engine stamps `_txFrom` and copies each superseded image into the internal `_VersionHistory` store `{rid, txFrom, txTo, out, in, data}` in the same transaction, indexed on `rid`, `out`, `in`; merge, split and undo delete and recreate `MEMBER_OF` edges, so the current view is the plain edge list and the as-of view is `history.edges(identity, t)`; analyst assertions on versioned objects update the property and `history.of` replays them with `_prov`; `ResolutionDecision` stays as the why record; `graph.history` (R13) is served by `history.of`, `history.asOf`, `history.edges` and the decision chain; the history line is 36 B per record per year at A17 (0 today, 42.9 GB at mid 3x, inside `identity`), paid only by versioned types; the merge is 1 ew in Q2, E25c and E25b in Q2 and E25d when triggered (round 9) | round 7 (owner directive: "a native way to manage versions natively rather than as an exploded mess of nodes"; design note verified against the worktree source) | History leaves the hot read path: no dead edges in any adjacency walk, no `validTo IS NULL` predicate for any query author, no overlay vertex on a value read, and reads that do not ask for history pay nothing; the store is a byte-for-byte image copy on the write path, replicated and rolled back with the transaction; the cost is 3.4x the bytes of ended edges, in a cold store, and the trigger moves 0.3 months (19.1 to 18.8) |
| D55 | **System 1 index from the beginning, scheduled by gates** (rounds 8 and 9, 8): S-PDQ and S-TEXT ship with Recon's Q1 move (E18, E19, WS13, WS12, WS15a), S-FACE, S-IMG, S-TATTOO and S-DOC in Q2 from one tier-A image pass once P0d answers, S-STYLE onto the index in Q2; the IVF recall gate passes or fails by the end of week 4 on the `LSM_IVF_VECTOR` spike, with the existing indexes (`pdqBands`, `LSM_VECTOR` for the newest S-TEXT, ROC for faces) as the proposed fallback; the format rule narrowed to additive formats; H1 before any backfill; the plan as release trains set by measurements, answers, hardware, compute, model deadlines and integration risk, not engineering capacity | owner directives 2026-11-03 | The owner wants the multimodal index there from the beginning and does not want human engineering time to drive the plan; the second-drive date does not move because the model already counted every space from month 0 |
| D56 | **Fixed hardware** (round 10, 0.8): 4 nodes x 126 GB RAM, no GPU, each 2 TB drive replaced by a 4 TB one (3,000 GB ceiling) and nothing else; CPU-only encoding; the decision layer on rules; **plan C, decided by the owner on 2026-11-03**: combination A from Q2 and E26 (warm sealed partitions on 2 of 4 nodes) in production by about 2027-07, so "replicate, do not shard" holds for hot data only; scene and tattoo on the hot window, OCR scope and the object store still open | owner directives 2026-11-03 | "Do as much as we can with the hardware we already have"; with full replicas mid crosses the 4 TB ceiling at about 2027-12; plan C holds to about 2029-01 at 95% at 3x |

### 10.2 Rejected alternatives

| Alternative | Proposed by | Why rejected |
|---|---|---|
| Sharding records across nodes | none | Owner wants replication |
| A RecordRef per record in `identity` | P2 | Makes the unpartitionable database 462 GB at 3x. The registry rule gives 279 GB (322 GB with the round-7 version history). |
| SPORK RIDs as cross-database links | P2 | Break silently on rebuild, restore or re-import |
| A single `spork_graph` database (530 GB today, 1.59 TB at 3x) | P3 | Sets the largest possible resync headroom; 98% of the ceiling today |
| Media databases on a second mount | P1 | New-node acquire uses an `ATOMIC_MOVE` rename that cannot cross filesystems |
| File-granular differential resync built on `PageVersionLedger` | P2 (W3b) | `PageVersionLedger` is a leader-side ledger of in-flight page-version reservations (issue #6965), not a per-page version map a follower can diff. It would need a new protocol and a rewrite of crash-safe swap logic. Cold install gets most of the benefit for 2 ew. |
| zstd dictionary codec, compact FTS postings, quantized-only vector storage as prerequisites for fitting | P1, P2 | On-disk format changes in upstream hot spots. Year-2 options only. The fit plan does not depend on them. |
| Corpus-wide ArcFace (or any open face) ANN index | P1 | Non-commercial InsightFace licence; EULA "merging" risk; 346 GB at mid 3x; does not fit the heap |
| Exemplar-only corpus face search with no full fallback | P3 | Loses recall for victim identification (age gaps). Fixed by the exhaustive job. |
| Per-face CLUSTER 1:N against all exemplars | P3 | About 31 cores continuously |
| Gallery RF2 on disk as a pressure valve | P2 | Partial replication against owner direction. Only with explicit sign-off. |
| HMAC as the chain hash itself | P1, P2 | Breaks Recon's sha256 verifier and existing chain |
| Board split across `spork_graph` and `spork_gov` | P3 | Two sources of truth for OCC |
| Time-based 18-month hot window with no size cap | P2 | Unbounded if growth comes from live sources |
| 256 GB nodes from day 1 | P3 (H2) | Not needed below 205M representatives; scheduled for year 2 by trigger |
| Stretching one Raft group across sites; async log shipping between clusters in year 1 | none / P2 option C | WAN RTT on every commit; the feature does not exist |
| Triggers or record listeners for audit or the change feed | none | They fire only where the transaction executes. Explicit plugin code plus the Outbox replicate correctly. |
| Two-property FULL_TEXT on `title, body` | base design | Posts every token twice (960 B instead of 480 B per hot record) |
| Warm FULL_TEXT without the stored `text` (body only in the object store) | round 2 design | Index maintenance removes keys computed from the stored property, so deleted ads would keep their postings; `ManualIndexBuilder` cannot build FULL_TEXT today (E17) |
| INT8 style copy on Record | round 2 design | Moves pairs across Recon's 0.95 cosine threshold and breaks dossier contentHash parity |
| `HA_SNAPSHOT_MAX_CONCURRENT=1` | round 2 design | Deterministic retry backoff serializes followers: 9.5 to 10.8 minute stalls at 120 GB, and a follower gives up at formation |
| A shorter retry base (5 s) with one slot | round 3 option | Still quantized with no jitter: at 0.6 GB/s the last follower of a 120 GB admission starts at 635 s, a 13.9 minute stall |
| Probing every partition for an upstream RID | base design | 12 probes per id at 3x; 24,000 lookups to hydrate a 2,000-id set |
| Aging `txt_<p>` partitions in place | base design | The engine never shrinks a bucket file, so in-place aging saves nothing |
| Registry `CO_OCCURS` components as Recon's link clusters | base design | A different object: drops singletons, ignores `linkOn`, can form giant components |
| Engine `DefaultDataEncryption` on `identity` or `media` (former E12) | base design | Serializer-wide, plaintext index keys, about 28 B per value on every property |
| Pre-staging identical files with rsync and forming the cluster from local files | round 1 design | Hard-coded 5 s probe timeout against a minutes-long full hash: peers are assumed empty and resync anyway |
| Toggling `HA_LOG_PURGE_UPTO_SNAPSHOT` per maintenance window | round 1 design | Read only at startup; each toggle needs rolling restarts under 5-minute retention |
| E2 matching on full-content fingerprints | round 1 design | Reads every database end to end on both sides; documented as valid only at cold start |
| Porting dossier, watch scoring and lead generation to Java | round 1 design | About 2,700 lines with byte-identical contentHash and every later detector change; unsized |
| Keyed blind index for evidence search | round 2 option | AND only, no BM25; changes Recon's ranking |
| `GraphAnalyticalView` for the nightly components job | round 1 design | 26.6 GB steady CSR at 3x, over 36 GB at build, and named views persist and rebuild on every node |
| Gateway-signed self-declared analyst names | base design | Signing a self-asserted name does not authenticate the analyst |
| Stock automatic LSM full compaction on SPORK databases | round 3 design (by omission) | Rewrites every index over 10,240 pages in full every 10 mutable pages and ships it through Raft: about 320 GB/day today for `recdir` alone, over 4 TB/day at 3x pace (D41) |
| Per-fetch `image.view` audit entries | round 3 design | Up to 960 chain commits per face scan; 320 commits/s for 20 analysts against a 200 to 500/s chain head; per-view grants instead (D49) |
| Role selected by route | round 3 design | Hydrate, search, intake and GET record serve both roles; the OC `role` claim selects the user (D50) |
| 7 daily full archives of every database | round 3 design | 3.4 TB today and 10.7 TB at 3x of mount; sealed databases change only through deletes (D35) |
| `txt_<p>` capped at 120 GB | round 3 design | The aging rewrite needs a restored source copy next to its output on n4: 271 GB, over the install reserve; 162 GB at the period boundary (D3) |
| Valves as a bridge for H1 | round 3 design | Valve 2 needs the gallery (W8, Q2), valves 1 and 3 the aging rewrite (WS7, Q2); the "month 3.7" claim is withdrawn (D6) |
| Normalized text dedup (A5 "after normalization") | round 3 design | Recon's `styleVector` counts case and punctuation; one vector per normalized text would move pairs across the 0.95 threshold (D51) |
| 64 KB pages for the `recdir` index as the plan | round 4 option | 4x fewer series (not 16x) but 229 MB per 2,000-id hydrate instead of 131; superseded in round 6: the `recdir` index is a HASH index with no series (D39) |
| LSM_TREE point-lookup indexes at 16 KB (rounds 3 and 4) | round 6 | 1 + 2S + 2 cached touches per probe with S = 519 to 1,609 for `recdir`, a 3.8 GB resident bloom set and a weekly 27 GB full compaction through Raft, against 3 touches, under 0.25 GB resident and no compaction for HASH (D39) |
| `02-retrieval-speed.md` revision 2's C5 compaction variant (schedule 4, `fullSeriesThreshold=3` on `identity`) | round 6 | one series per incremental round, so schedule 4 leaves a live composite with 892 series a week against 56 at schedule 64 for 60 fewer binary searches per cursor open, and threshold 3 rewrites every growing `identity` index in full every 3 rounds (D41) |
| A corpus-wide Hamming band index for PDQ (8 bands) or pHash (2 bands) | round 6 (`06-stylometry-perceptual-hash.md` 3.1) | 194 GB or 49 GB at mid 3x next to S-PDQ's 54 GB; PDQ bands stay on the hot-window `media_<p>` and case databases (43 GB at 3x), the radius-31 near-dup is the S-PDQ IVF space, and PDQ supersedes pHash |
| Corpus-wide faces or images in jvector (`LSM_VECTOR`) | base design, `workstreams/vector-scale.md` | 36 to 108 B per vector of heap (the location index alone is 29 GB at 900M), 1,104 B per node of build heap, graphs rebuilt per node and not replicated; IVF keeps only centroids resident (D46) |
| Folding time or place into the embedding | research option | Breaks score comparability across partitions and makes "14 days, 200 km" un-auditable; structured allow-lists instead (D46) |
| Late-interaction multi-vector (ColPali) at corpus scale | research option | About 264 KB per page; case-scoped S-DOC only, through MUVERA or Light-ColPali (D46) |
| PhotoDNA as the general near-duplicate index | research option | Cloud service for qualified customers, "not intended for law enforcement" through the cloud; it is the CSAM reporting interface, PDQ is the near-dup index (D47) |
| JEPA prediction error as an index key | research option | Evidence thin, collapse is the main failure mode; a `novelty` scalar on `Image` instead (D46) |
| An open age-estimation model as a minor signal | research option | 2 to 3x worse than ROC on minors and unvalidated on 13 to 16; ROC age band plus human review only (D47) |
| Hosted-only decision inference (Jev) for CHT states | research option | No weights, no SLA, no reviewable MCA; required self-hosted Apache-2.0 path, hosted only for states without personal data with owner sign-off (D48) |
| A database-wide GAV for the link-chart operations | research option | 26.6 GB steady at 3x; a registry-only GAV is 11 to 19 GB and still does not fit beside the union-find on n4; the capped OLTP path needs none (D45) |
| DINOv3 before counsel's review; Hotels-50K by download | research option | Custom Meta license with an espionage clause and amendment right; no license file on Hotels-50K (owner questions 20 and 21) |
| Case-layer work product silently kept after a subject deletion | round 3 design (by omission) | Leaves the subject's keys in the board, deconfliction index, watches and alerts; redaction by default, exemption only by a signed `RetentionPolicy` (D19) |
| One 180 B centroid constant for every space | round 4 design | An inference that held only for 4-bit 256-d codes; the engine's own resident cost is 36 B plus the code plus the graph share, and S-FACE is 512-d: 356, 612 and 132 B per space (A15, D46) |
| Per-partition k-means codebooks for the IVF spaces | round 5 option | Scores would no longer compare across partitions and every partition would hold its own resident codebook; one global codebook with per-partition posting directories in index pages instead (4c.2) |
| Posting pages of one posting each | round 4 design (by omission) | At 3x a partition's posting holds about 17 items (1 KB), so a page per posting would be 16x under-filled; postings are packed about 17 per 16 KB page (4c.2) |
| `GraphBatch.withWAL(false)` for offline builds, as the engine Javadoc recommends | engine Javadoc | Retains about 8 KB of heap per record until the JVM dies (24 GB heap OOM at 5M records); every build runs WAL on at the measured 265K elements/s (6.1, R32) |
| E8 as a 4 ew non-format top-K item | round 4 design | The top-K half is done without a format change (`FullTextTopK`); Block-Max skipping needs a RID-ordered posting layout with per-block maxima, an index-format change at about 8 ew (D28) |
| Recon compare as n-way `AND` and `MINUS` over bitmaps | round 4 design | `compare.ts` runs a 12-field all-way intersection for records, per-group aggregates with pairwise overlaps for entities and sets, and `crossGroup` from the union of labels for faces; sets mode is the aggregates path over per-session refs, not saved sets (D44) |
| A Merkle root as the only record of the ids an analyst received | round 4 design | Proves inclusion to a holder of the key list, answers neither "who viewed record X" nor "what did analyst Y disseminate"; the keyed `AuditAccess` side table outside the hash (D49) |
| 7 GPU-hours/day for ingest priority scoring at 3x pace | round 4 design | 1.25B tokens/day at the stated 2K tokens/s is 174 GPU-hours (7.2 cards); at a derived 8K tokens/s prefill it is 43 (1.8 cards), so scoring is scoped to the rules fallback plus the newest 300K records/day until measured, on an 80 GB card the 49 GiB checkpoint fits (4d.3, R30) |
| `validFrom` and `validTo` on `MEMBER_OF`, ended edges kept forever as the as-of view | rounds 2 to 6 design | Puts history on the hot read path: every registry traversal (typed expand, k-hop counts, common neighbours, induced subgraph, the union-find scan) walks the dead edges and filters `validTo IS NULL`, the edge lists of every Identity grow with every merge and split (10.7 B per record per year of live-list bytes at A17), and every query author owns the as-of predicate; the engine's versioning keeps the same information in a cold store that only `history.*` reads (D54) |
| `Assertion` overlay vertices as the value history of asserted properties (`supersedes` chains the read path replays) | round 4 design | One vertex per asserted value, read and overlaid on every value read of an asserted property, with a `supersedes` walk for a correction; with versioned types the property is updated in place and `history.of` replays every value with its `_prov` and transaction time, so the overlay exists only, narrowed, for subjects whose types cannot be versioned (`Record`, shared identifiers rewritten by ingest) (D54) |
| Application (valid) time inside the engine's versioning | round 7 option | Slice 1 is transaction time only (SQL:2011 system versioning); bitemporal corrections stay as properties where a type needs them, because an engine-owned valid time would make every ingest update of a `Record` a version |

---

## Appendix A. Review log

**Round 1 (53 issues from three verifiers):** all applied; none rejected. Source claims spot-checked before applying: FTS double posting (`LSMTreeFullTextIndex.putWithStats`), purge gap without preservation (`RaftPropertiesBuilder`), election timeout defaults (5,000 / 10,000 ms), alphabetical reconcile order (`DatabaseReconciler`), serializer-wide encryption (`BinarySerializer`), `SERVER_READINESS_REQUIRES_HA` default false, `LeaderForwardContext` marker semantics, `InsertStream` target_class, Recon's `canonPhone`. Where an issue offered alternatives, the choice made:
- #1 hydrate: a `recdir` directory for `upstreamRid` (70 B per record, not 16 B, because an LSM entry must point at a document) and the period encoded in SPORK-assigned `record_id`.
- #24 and #25: FULL_TEXT moved to one derived property instead of budgeting 960 B. The other corrections are in the model, which also adds `recdir`, tattoo segments, extra composites, `storeVectorsInGraph` bytes and full-length image hashes, so totals exceed the verifier's figures.
- #29 and #44: offline rewrite at aging; warm FTS pruning moved from counted to valve 1.
- #30, #31, #40: `-Xmx` kept at 56 GB, page cache cut to 23 GB to fund GC headroom; the 384-d text embedding removed (also #32).
- #33: rule stated as newest 18 months capped at 200M, with all growth treated as hot (cap reached about month 4.7).
- #35: ROC cap raised to 45 GB (OS file cache cut to 12 GB) to hold the case and tattoo galleries; capacity 205M, mid crossing month 15.5.
- #39: W3d split into backups (W3d, 2 ew) and restore-then-join in ha-raft (W3e, 5 ew, year 2).
- #46: E6 dropped rather than shrunk.

**Round 2 (22 issues):** all applied; none rejected. Source claims checked before applying: `AutoBackupConfig` defaults (`$leader`, `./backups`, 60 min, 10 files); `HA_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT_MS` 120,000 and `DatabaseReconciler.reconcileDatabasesFromLeader` falling to `failInstallWhenNoLocalDatabases` on an empty follower; `BootstrapElection.probeAttemptTimeoutMs = 5_000L`; `HA_SNAPSHOT_INSTALL_RETRIES` 3 and base 5,000 ms; `LeaderDatabaseQuery.DatabaseInfo(name, lastTxId)`; `CSRBuilder` forward and backward offsets per type and BFS reorder; `LocalDatabase` `restoreAll` of GAVs on open; `FullTextQueryExecutor` per-MUST-term `HashMap` and `retainAll`; `LocalDatabase.checkPermissionsOnFile` early return without a bound user; `GetClusterHandler` `?presence=true` fan-out; Recon `entryHash`, FTS5 evidence columns, `RECON_FETCH_LIMIT`, `BoardTarget` fields and `boardId`. Choices where an issue offered alternatives:
- Backups: off-node mount until W3d (not W3d in Q1), with one `transient-slot` lease shared by admission, n4 builds and staging.
- n4 components: streaming union-find instead of a larger GAV slice or per-kind components (per-kind is not a true connected component, since `CO_OCCURS` crosses kinds).
- n4 RAM: a 16 GB build slice from n4's page cache (7 to 4), GC headroom (10 to 8), OLAP (24 to 19) and OS file cache (12 to 6).
- Full-text: gate on the summed df of all terms, not on dropping and verifying terms (warm bodies were then planned off node, so candidates could not be verified locally; round 3 keeps warm text on node until E17, and the df gate stands).
- Hydrate and Record: denormalize onto Record (option a, +96 B per record, +26 GB today, +80 GB at 3x) and a 32-way read pool; this moved the mid ceiling date from month 3.6 to 3.2 and the partition capacity from 86M to 80M records.
- Face backfill: burst host, blocking-only CLUSTER, separate case-gallery 1:N pass; ROC workers in their own cgroup rather than a smaller n4 gallery quarter.
- Audit: v1 genesis plus v2 commitment body; v1 entries are not redactable without a signed redaction record.
- Evidence: plaintext FULL_TEXT (option 1).
- Recon pipelines: stay in Recon; SPORK supplies footprints, leases, state and audit.
- Initial load: option (a), leader-shipped snapshots from a seeded n1; no engine change in Q1.
- Admission: retries 6 x 30 s base, not concurrency 3 (the link is the bottleneck either way); smaller admissions if the P0c stall exceeds 10 minutes. (Reversed in round 3: the retry schedule, not the link, set the stall.)
- Staffing: a third Java engineer as the plan of record; slip dates stated for the 4-engineer fallback.

**Round 3 (13 issues):** all applied; none rejected outright, two alternatives inside issues rejected with reasons. Source claims checked before applying: `INDEX_DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE` 256 KB, `BUCKET_DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE` 64 KB and `EXTERNAL_PROPERTY_BUCKET_DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE` 256 KB (`GlobalConfiguration`), `IndexBuilder.withPageSize` and `TypeBuilder.withPageSize`; `LSMTreeIndexBloomFilter` series walk; `SnapshotInstaller.downloadWithRetry` delay `retryBaseMs * (1L << (attempt - 1))` with no jitter; the `SnapshotHttpHandler` semaphore sized once at construction; `DocumentIndexer.deleteDocument` keys from `index.getPropertyNames()`; `ManualIndexBuilder` limited to LSM_TREE and HASH; `BackupTask` setting no encryption key while `BackupSettings` supports one; `ServerControlPlane.restoreDatabase` refusing a taken name; Recon `analysis/style.ts` `SIM = 0.95`, `TargetOverlap` fields, `dossier.view` and `intake.run` detail, `tools.ts` `arc` and `phone_lookup` over `ApiSource`, `LocalSource` holding only ingested records. Model re-run (`docs/spork/model/budget.py`): mid today 1,262 to 1,325 GB, mid 3x 3,384 to 3,604 GB, mid ceiling month 3.2 to 2.5 (valves 4.7 to 3.7), Q1 Java 27 to 28 ew.
- #1 and #10 page sizes (capacity, engine-feasibility): applied. 16 KB pages on the point-lookup indexes and on the Record, `recdir` and TextBody buckets (external pages too) in schema v1 (0.4, D39); hydrate restated as 131 MB and 25 to 30 ms of I/O per 2,000 ids (1.3 GB on stock pages), search rows as 25 MB; series walk, bytes per hydrate and page-cache churn added to P0a. The title stays inline on TextBody so a search row needs no external page.
- #2, #3 and #11 snapshot retry quantization: applied. `HA_SNAPSHOT_MAX_CONCURRENT=3` (one slot per follower, permanent because the semaphore is sized at startup); stalls recomputed by the model (6 to 10 minutes at 120 GB, 2 to 3.3 at 40 GB; 9.5 to 10.8 and 4.2 to 4.6 with one slot); formation no longer loses a follower; R18, D26, P0c gate on the last follower. Rejected sub-option: a 5 s base with one slot (still quantized, 13.9 minutes at 0.6 GB/s). Forming one follower at a time is unnecessary with 3 slots.
- #4 stylometric parity: applied. Exact float32 `styleVec` on Record (+72 B per record with slot reserve; the warm TextBody raw style, 48 B per warm record, is dropped as a duplicate).
- #5 shadow-diff reference: applied. Diff against `ApiSource` with record-replay; SPORK keeps the identifier rule with listed differences, and the full-text arm is owner question 14.
- #6 audit detail: applied. Recon completion appends for `dossier.view` and `intake.run` through `/audit/append`, allowlisted keys, linked by `requestId` to SPORK's pre-release entry.
- #7 ARC and phone lookup: applied. `POST /arc/search` (victim_identification with a caseRef, audited before the image leaves) and `GET /phone/lookup`, used by `SporkSource`.
- #8 deconfliction fields: applied. Full `TargetOverlap` with shared, sharedCount, anchor and assessment, sorted and capped as Recon does; withholding the anchor across cases is owner question 14.
- #9 stored `text`: applied with option (a): `text` (title + body) is the only long property, kept on node in both tiers; the 191 GB warm saving became valve 4 behind E17. Rejected sub-option: (b) a manual FULL_TEXT index, because `ManualIndexBuilder` refuses FULL_TEXT today; enabling it is E17 (3 ew, year 2, optional).
- #12 plaintext backups: applied. Encrypted backup mount with KMS-held keys as a Q1 rule (5.6, R21); 7 daily archives on the mount and no weekly copies before W3d; W3d encrypts with a KMS key.
- #13 aging: applied. Aging protocol in 3.5 (`aging` mark with lastTxId, replay list, admission as `txt_<p>_w<n>`, replay, partition-map switch, drop, then acknowledgements; R22, WS7 +1 ew).

**Round 4 (29 remaining issues from `model/round3-remaining.json`: 1 blocker, 11 major, 17 minor; plus the four research notes and three done workstreams folded in):** all 29 applied; none rejected; two sub-options inside issues rejected with reasons. Source claims checked before applying: `INDEX_COMPACTION_FULL_SERIES` default 10, `INDEX_COMPACTION_MIN_PAGES_SCHEDULE` default 10 and `INDEX_COMPACTION_RAM_MB` 300, all at database scope (`GlobalConfiguration`); `LSMTreeIndexCompactor.isFullCompactionDue` on `seriesCount >= threshold` with the dead-entries ratio as a second trigger; `DEFAULT_MAX_DATA_PAGES_PER_SERIES = 1_024` (`LSMTreeIndexCompactedStreamWriter`); `RaftTransactionBroker` and `ArcadeStateMachine` documenting `runWithCompactionReplication` as leader-only with the compacted file chunked through the WAL; `HA_STALE_FOLLOWER_LAG_THRESHOLD` 10,000 and `HA_STALE_FOLLOWER_RECOVERY_DURATION_MS` 60,000 at server scope; `EXTERNAL_PROPERTY_BUCKET_DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE` and `VECTOR_INDEX_SEARCH_CACHE_SIZE` at database scope; `ServerSecurityUser` matching database grants by exact name or `*`. Model re-run (`docs/spork/model/budget.py`): rec 1,567 to 1,595 B per record (+28 B provenance and markings), fixed 130 to 190 GB (Raft line 60 to 120), mid today 1,325 to 1,393 GB without the S1 index (face-free 1,215), mid 3x 3,604 to 3,689 GB without and 4,099 GB with the S1 index (91% of 4,520), H1 date from month 2.5 to the earlier of the face backfill and month 4.2, second-drive trigger month 19.5 at mid, backup mount 1.8 / 3.9 TB, Q1 Java 28 to 30.5 ew, total 176 to 219 ew.
- **Blocker, LSM compaction through Raft:** applied. Compaction policy in 0.4 and E0 (`indexCompactionFullSeriesThreshold=0`, dead-entries ratio 0, incremental schedule 64, leased off-peak full compactions one at a time, seal and post-admission compactions, suspension while a peer is down), Raft line 120 GB = 2 x (25 + 27 + 8), transient 27 GB under the `transient-slot` lease, dead-peer window restated (3.6 to 4.8 days, 2.0 with a compaction; alert 50, page 80), P0a and P0c measurements, R23, D41. Sub-option 64 KB pages for `recdir` kept as a P0a decision, not the plan (see the hydrate issue).
- **Audit by data class (major):** applied. 3.1, 5.1, 5.2 and 5.4 now agree: every footprint-bearing route is gated like `/intake` and appends one batched entry per request; `export.csv`, `export.brief`, `knowledge.export` and `feedback.record` added to the completion allowlist; `/knowledge/export` served from SPORK; D49.
- **Role selector (major):** applied. `role` claim in the OC asserted by the gateway per module; the plugin binds the engine user from the claim and refuses title, text and `q` under ops before the engine; three WS3 denial tests; D50.
- **Classic monitor and media routes (major):** applied. `/api/watch`, `/api/watch/scan`, `/api/alerts`, `/api/feedback`, `/api/image` added to WS4; the first three retired for the ops routes; `feedback.record`; a gateway default OC is never stored as `createdOc` (428 `declaredOcRequired`).
- **Image audit (major):** applied. One `image.view` per producing view with a Merkle root and a signed 10-minute grant; `GET /image` checks the grant and appends nothing; audit rate restated at 1.7/s for 20 analysts against 320/s per fetch (5.2).
- **Stylometric parity (major):** applied. `textHash` is SHA-256 over the exact title + body bytes; `style` and `styleVec` per record from the exact `article_body` at ingest before dedup; A5 is the measured exact-match fraction, +150 GB (3.7%) per +0.1 at mid 3x; D51.
- **Deletion and the case layer (major):** applied with option (a) as the default: step 4b redacts the subject's keys from targets, snapshots, watches, baselines, alerts, missions, feedback, set snapshots and queue items and marks `subjectDeleted`; option (b), a signed exemption, is owner question 5 and would be recorded in the certificate; D19 reworded.
- **Alert fields (minor):** applied. watchName, families[], command; watch removal cascades to alerts, audited.
- **Filtered audit totals (minor):** applied. Count pushdown on (action), (caseRef), (subjectKey), capped at 10,000; counters only unfiltered.
- **Request bounds (minor):** applied. `/intake` 25 tokens, `/records:hydrate` 5,000 ids (328 MB, 1.4% of the page cache), the per-token `limit` on social tokens.
- **Missions (minor):** applied. The mission footprint listed as an accepted shadow-diff difference with `expand=false` on `/intake`; lease-aware restart recovery in WS10.
- **importedId (minor):** applied. (tenant, importedId) index (4 B amortized in the 28 B ontology line), `/ingest` returns the id map, `imp:<id>` accepted by hydrate and intake, importedId checked before `recdir` for RID-shaped tokens.
- **7-digit deconfliction (minor):** applied. Both the raw-digit and the inferred key in `Target.identifierKeys[]` and from the footprint side.
- **SporkSource (minor):** applied. Specified as a materializing per-session cache; the seven Engine methods made async listed in WS4.
- **Replay set (minor):** applied. Object store under SSE-KMS, own retention class (cutover + 30 days), access-audited, deletion step 8b.
- **Hydrate series walk (major):** applied. Hydrate restated as 4 cold reads plus a resident walk of index bytes / (1,024 x page size) series (519 for `recdir` today, 1,609 at 3x at 16 KB; 130 and 402 at 64 KB, which is 4x fewer, not the 16x the issue stated), bloom set 1.2 to 3.8 GB budgeted in the 23 GB page cache, walk CPU 0.16 to 0.48 ms per probe; set-scoped geo and graph hydrates moved from n4 to n2/n3 until P0a; 16 KB kept on `recdir` with 64 KB as the P0a decision (229 MB per 2,000-id hydrate at 64 KB); mutable pages per probe and seal compaction added (also the last minor); D39, D52.
- **Backup mount (major):** applied with the second option: sealed databases archived once at seal and after deletion batches, 7 dailies for `identity`, `ops`, `audit`, `recdir` and the current period: 8 x 180 + 310 + 60 = 1.8 TB today, 8 x 337 + 1,195 + 60 = 3.9 TB at 3x, against 3.4 and 10.7 TB for 7 full generations; Q13 and D35 restated.
- **Install deadline (major):** applied. Two dates: before the face backfill on any node, and before the face-free crossing of 1,560 GB (month 4.2 at mid, 1.9 at high, 5.8 at low, from the model's face-free totals 1,215 / 1,383 / 1,132 GB with the new fixed line); the "valves stretch to month 3.7" claim withdrawn and the valves restated as Q2-only; the backfill waits for H1 on every node; R1, D6, WS1, critical path.
- **`searchCacheSize` (two minors, duplicates):** applied once. Database setting `arcadedb.vectorIndex.searchCacheSize=500000` per `txt_*` in E0, 1.4, 3.4, D23.
- **LUKS layering (minor):** applied. LVM on LUKS with a LUKS PV per drive, the full online sequence in 0.6 and 5.6, the doubled drive-failure exposure in R9, mirrored PVs rejected as unaffordable.
- **Stale-follower recovery during admission (major):** applied. `HA_STALE_FOLLOWER_RECOVERY_DURATION_MS=1800000` in E0, MediaJob claims and completions batched 50 per transaction (43 to 0.9 entries/s at 3x pace), P0c asserts the absence of the recovery log line, R18 restated, E16 rationale extended with the `snapshotDownloadInProgress` exemption.
- **Mission titles (major):** applied with option (1): `sourceRefs[].textHash` instead of title, `textHash` in the hydrate projection, `targeting.ts` changed in WS4, titles resolved through the console role at view time, WS10's legacy import redacted before it lands in `identity`.
- **External bucket page size (minor):** applied. `arcadedb.externalPropertyBucketDefaultPageSize=16384` per `txt_<p>` before the EXTERNAL property; 0.4 and D39 corrected.
- **Stopword analyzer (minor):** applied. One generated analyzer subclass per partition reading `${spork.analyzers.dir}/<class>.stop`, named as the index analyzer; the "file named in index metadata" wording removed (4).
- **Ops grants by exact name (minor):** applied with the first option: the ops grant on every new database is an audited step of period creation and admission, asserted by a WS3 test.
- **Auto-backup and the lease (minor):** applied. Before W3d the plugin disables the affected schedules by configuration reload around an admission; W3d's scheduler checks the lease (3.5, D35).
- **n4 build source (minor):** applied. The aging rewrite restores the partition's latest archive into n4's scratch under the lease; the slot holds source + output + archive (162 GB at the period boundary); `txt_<p>` capped at the `rec` period boundary instead of 120 GB (1.2, 3.5, D3).
- **Mutable pages per probe (minor):** applied. Forced full compaction of the point-lookup indexes at seal and after admission (part of the compaction policy); "mutable pages per probe" added to P0a.

**Round 6 (reconciliation with `02-retrieval-speed.md`, 10 conflicts found by the config-bundle review in `config/README.md`):** all 10 resolved, by read latency first and the verified engine facts second; every change made in both documents, in `model/budget.py` and in `config/`. Source facts checked: `HashIndexBucket.DEF_PAGE_SIZE` 4,096 and `DEF_VARIABLE_KEY_PAGE_SIZE` 16,384 (issue #5712), `MAX_PAGE_SIZE` 65,536, `isSupportedKeyType` (BOOLEAN to DURATION, LONG, STRING, BINARY, UUID, RID), `checkSupportedKeyTypes`, `entryTooLarge`, `splitBucket` and `doubleDirectory`, `HashIndexBucket extends PaginatedComponent`, `HashIndex.supportsOrderedIterations()` false, `compact()` and `scheduleCompaction()` false, `HashIndex.HashIndexFactoryHandler` resolving the page size through `builder.getPageSize(HashIndexBucket.defaultPageSize(keyTypes))`; `TypeIndexBuilder` validating `BY ITEM` by the LIST property type; `CreateVertexTypeStatement.java:41-53` and `CreateDocumentTypeStatement` passing PAGESIZE to `withPageSize`, `CreateIndexStatement.java:404` having no PAGESIZE clause, `IndexBuilder.withPageSize` (:317), `getPageSize()` defaulting to 256 KB and `getLsmTreePageSize()` honouring `arcadedb.indexDefaultPageSize`; `LocalDatabase.getNewEdgeListSize(previousSize)` static, reading `GRAPH_EDGE_LIST_INITIAL_CHUNK_SIZE.getValueAsInteger()` JVM-wide, called with 0 from `GraphEngine.createInEdgeChunk` / `createOutEdgeChunk`, `EdgeLinkedList` and `StripedEdgeList`; `arcadedb.fullText.topK` absent from the main tree's `GlobalConfiguration`; compiled-in defaults `queryLiteralParameterization=true`, `vectorIndex.maxConcurrentRebuilds=1`, `ha.readConsistency=read_your_writes`, `ha.autoAcquireDatabases=true`, `typeDefaultBuckets=1`, `indexCompactionMinPagesSchedule=10`, `indexCompactionFullSeriesThreshold=10`, `indexCompactionFullDeadEntriesRatio=1.0`. Model re-run: hydrate 2,000 upstream RIDs 112 MB (72 by `record_id`, 80 and 40 after M20) against 131 (round 4) and 1.3 GB (stock); Record bucket 97 / 63 / 54 GB per 77M partition at 16 / 32 / 64 KB v0 and 50 GB at 16 KB v2; HASH directories plus LSM composite tops 0.44 GB today and 1.3 GB at 3x; compaction 28 GB/week and 4 GB/day; live composite 56 series a week at schedule 64 against 892 at 4; hot-window PDQ band index 23.8 GB today and 43.2 GB at mid 3x, mid today 1,392.9 to 1,416.7 GB without the S1 index (face-free 1,215 to 1,239), mid 3x 4,099.1 to 4,142.3 GB (92% of 4,520), face-free crossing month 4.2 to 3.8 (2027-02), second-drive trigger month 19.5 to 19.1 (still 2028-06).
1. **n4 heap (02 5.2 said 46g / 42g, 1.4 says 43g):** 02 corrected to `-Xmx43g -Xms43g SoftMaxHeapSize=38g`. Only 43 sums to 126 (6 + 43 + 3 + 4 + 45 + 3 + 16 + 6; 46 gives 129), and 38 keeps n1 to n3's 89% soft target (43 x 50 / 56 = 38.4) with 8 / 43 = 19% GC headroom.
2. **OS file cache (02 3.1 and 5.1 said 12 GB):** 02 corrected to 9 GB on n1 to n3 and 6 on n4 with the encoder sidecar in both sums (6 + 56 + 3 + 4 + 45 + 3 + 9 = 126), 590K pages of 16 KB instead of 786K, and the sidecar's 3 GB cgroup added to 5.3.
3. **Compaction policy (02 C5: schedule 4, `fullSeriesThreshold=3` on `identity`, 0 on `rec_<p>`; 0.4 / 1.1 / E0: 0 and dead-ratio 0 everywhere, schedule 64, leased weekly full compactions):** 0.4's policy kept and C5, 2.0, 5.4 and risk 4 of 02 rewritten to it. Arithmetic: each incremental round appends one series, so at 3x pace a live composite (835K x 40 B = 33 MB/day) makes 8 rounds and 56 series a week at schedule 64 against 127 rounds and 892 series at schedule 4, for a saving of only 60 cached binary searches per cursor open, while the per-round rewrite of metadata, tail and root pages (about 192 KB) raises incremental Raft traffic from about 8 to 14 GB/day; `fullSeriesThreshold=3` on `identity` never fires on the tiny indexes (they do not reach 3 series) and rewrites the growing ones in full every 3 rounds (AuditEntry composites at 2.7 GB/yr of entries: every 1.7 days, 1.5 GB/day and rising), so no part of the threshold is valid under the Raft constraint. Kept from C5: the seal-time full compaction, widened to every LSM index of the sealed partition (83 GB per seal, one 3 GB index at a time), and `indexBloomFilterRate` at its default. New in 0.4: the live-partition cursor-open cost the policy accepts (about 175 cached touches, 50 to 100 us, at week's end; M2 and M6 remove it; twice-weekly compaction of the current period's composites is the P0a-gated fallback at +27 GB/week).
4. **Point-lookup index type (02 C8 HASH; 2.1, 0.4, 1.4, 2.5 assumed LSM_TREE):** HASH adopted in 2.1, 0.4, 1.4, 2.5, 3.6 E0, section 4, D39, D41, D52 and the model. Cost per probe: 3 cached touches + 1 cold read (2 with an overflow page) against 1 + 2S + 2 with S = 519 today and 1,609 at 3x for `recdir` at 16 KB (1,040 and 3,221 touches, 0.16 to 0.48 ms of CPU) or 7 touches and a 256 KB cold leaf for the 02 LSM fallback; resident set under 0.25 GB of directories (recdir 7.2M pages x 4 B rounded to 2^23 = 34 MB at 3x) against 3.8 GB of blooms and roots; no compaction through Raft (27 GB/week for `recdir` alone gone). Verified limits that do not bite: no ordered iteration (ranges stay LSM), entry must fit a page (keys under 100 B), depth 30, unique and non-unique with RID lists, `BY ITEM` by property type, growth by split and directory doubling as ordinary replicated pages. Consequence: D52 reversed, n4 takes P9c and P11 as 02 routes them (112 MB per 2,000-id hydrate is 2.8% of its 4 GB cache).
5. **Bucket page sizes (0.4 / 2.1 said Record, `recdir`, TextBody 16 KB and 131 MB per 2,000-id hydrate; 02 C9 said Record and TextBody 32 KB, `recdir` 16 KB, 160 MB):** C9's bucket sizes adopted, and C9's uniform 16 KB HASH page replaced by the engine defaults (4 KB fixed-width, 16 KB variable-width; the engine's own #5712 benchmark picked them, a cold LONG-key probe moves 4 KB instead of 16, the directory grows 4x to tens of MB). Arithmetic before M20 (v0, 8,202 B header and 2,048 slots): 609 B rows pack 13 / 40 / 94 per 16 / 32 / 64 KB page, 97 / 63 / 54 GB per 77M partition, so 16 KB costs +34 GB per partition over 32 KB and 32 KB costs +9 GB over 64 KB; a cold Record read moves 32 KB (6.5 us of bandwidth) against 64 KB (13 us), and from about 300 rows the bandwidth term wins, so 32 KB is the latency choice that capacity can afford. After M20 (256 slots, 1,026 B header): 16 KB holds 25 rows at 93.7% (50 GB) and halves the bandwidth term again, so new partitions move to 16 KB. Hydrate: upstream RID 4 + 16 + 4 + 32 = 56 KB, 2,000 ids 112 MB (72 by `record_id`), 80 and 40 MB after M20; search row 32 + 16 + 32 = 80 KB, 500 rows 40 MB, 24 MB after M20. The 0.4 byte lines keep their 12.5% slot reserve; the 25% page-header share of 32 KB v0 (+180 B per record, +14 GB per partition) is stated and left to P0a, not folded into the totals.
6. **`edgeListInitialChunkSize=2048` per vertex through a `GraphEngine` API (the claim lives in 02 2.1 and risk 9; 0.4 counts 64 B chunks):** confirmed false in source (`LocalDatabase.getNewEdgeListSize` is static and JVM-wide; `GraphEngine.createInEdgeChunk` / `createOutEdgeChunk` call it with 0 and take no size). 02 2.1, C7 and risk 9 corrected to 64 B on day 1 (a 500-record identifier costs 7 chunk hops), and the engine item added as E24 here and S18 in 02 4.2 at 0.5 to 1 ew: per-type first and second chunk sizes in the vertex type metadata, so singletons keep 64 B and any identifier needing a second chunk jumps to 2 KB (about 18 GB per partition on local vertices with 2 or more records, 0 on `identity` only), against +85 GB per partition for 2048 everywhere.
7. **SQL page sizes:** both documents now state that `CREATE [VERTEX|DOCUMENT] TYPE ... PAGESIZE n` reaches `TypeBuilder.withPageSize` (`CreateVertexTypeStatement.java:41-53`), so bucket page sizes need no Java, and that `CREATE INDEX` has no PAGESIZE clause (`CreateIndexStatement.java:404`), so FULL_TEXT and GEOSPATIAL at 64 KB need `IndexBuilder.withPageSize(65536)` (their builders ignore `arcadedb.indexDefaultPageSize`, which only `getLsmTreePageSize` reads); HASH indexes take `HashIndexBucket.defaultPageSize(keyTypes)` and need neither.
8. **`arcadedb.fullText.topK`:** marked in 1.4, the executive summary, section 4 and D28 as a key that exists only in the partial-text worktree until the Q2 merge (absent from `GlobalConfiguration` in the main tree); 02 never named it, which appendix B of 02 records.
9. **Already-default settings presented as changes:** `queryLiteralParameterization=true`, `vectorIndex.maxConcurrentRebuilds=1`, `ha.readConsistency=read_your_writes`, `ha.autoAcquireDatabases=true` and `typeDefaultBuckets=1` are the compiled-in defaults; marked "already the default" in E0, 1.1, 3.4 and in 02 C2, C4, C11 and 5.4 (where the two bookmark settings are now comments). The config bundle keeps them explicit.
**Round 7 (native versioning, `08-native-versioning-design.md`, 2026-11-03):** the owner asked for "a native way to manage versions natively rather than as an exploded mess of nodes"; a sibling session built it as engine code in the `native-versioning` worktree (branch `worktree-native-versioning`, uncommitted, 284 tests green) and this round folds it into the architecture. Source claims verified in that worktree before applying: `SQLLexer.g4` `VERSIONED` token and `SQLParser.g4` `ALTER TYPE ... VERSIONED (TRUE | FALSE)`; `DocumentType.isVersioned()` / `setVersioned()` with `LocalDocumentType` persisting `"versioned": true`, `LocalSchema` loading it and `RemoteDocumentType` exposing it; `RecordVersioning.TX_FROM_PROPERTY = "_txFrom"`, `HISTORY_TYPE = "_VersionHistory"` with properties `rid` (LINK), `txFrom`, `txTo` (LONG), `out`, `in` (LINK), `data` (BINARY) and `NOTUNIQUE` LSM indexes on `rid`, `out`, `in` with `NULL_STRATEGY.SKIP` (`prepareType`); the per-database stamp `lastStamp.accumulateAndGet(now, max(previous + 1, candidate))`; `TransactionContext.versionStamp` and `versionedRecords` (a record created in the transaction gets no history entry); the hooks in `LocalDatabase` (`versioning` field, `getVersioning()`) and `GraphEngine`; the four functions `history.of`, `history.asOf`, `history.edges`, `history.purge` in `function/sql/history/` registered in `DefaultSQLFunctionFactory`; `RecordVersioning.of(Database)`; 22 test methods in `RecordVersioningTest`. Model re-run (`docs/spork/model/budget.py`): a `_VersionHistory` line inside `identity` at A17 (0.05 Identities per record, one `MEMBER_OF` per shared identifier, 0.25 superseded versions per record per year, 200 B per Identity entry and 220 B per MEMBER_OF entry): 36.0 B per record per year at mid, 0 today, 16.0 GB at month 12, 42.9 GB at mid 3x; `identity` 279.5 to 322.4 GB at mid 3x (low 209.6 to 240.2, high 441.9 to 509.3); mid 3x total 4,142.3 to 4,185.2 GB (92% to 93% of 4,520); slope 108.0 to 109.8 GB per month with the S1 index (96.5 to 98.3 without); second-drive order trigger month 19.1 to 18.8 (2028-06 to 2028-05); `identity` crosses 120 GB at month 3.5 (2027-02) instead of about 4 (2027-03); the largest backup archive 140 to 161 GB and the mount 4.3 to 4.5 TB at mid 3x with the S1 codes (3.9 to 4.1 without); the "today" rows, the H1 dates and the RAM model are unchanged. Plan: 1 ew merge in Q2 (Java 30.5 of 30), E25c 0.5 ew in Q3 (Java 60 of 60), E25b 1 ew and the optional E25d 2 ew in year 2 (68), total about 233 ew (52 + 43.5 + 69 + 68).
1. **2.6 reversible entity resolution:** merge, split and undo delete and recreate `MEMBER_OF` edges instead of setting `validTo` and keeping them; the current view is the plain edge list (no `validTo IS NULL` filter on any traversal, stated as a retrieval-speed win for every registry procedure of 4b.2); the as-of view is `history.edges(identity, t)`, the Identity state `history.asOf` / `history.of`; `Identity` and `MEMBER_OF` are versioned types (2.1, 2.2); `ResolutionDecision` stays as the why record (rule, evidence, actor), not a version store; Q1 runs the same rule on the stock engine with the decision chain as its only as-of source until the Q2 merge; `history.purge` in the deletion saga; history not markings-checked until E13.
2. **2.7 provenance:** `Assertion` overlay vertices are no longer the value history. An assertion on a versioned object updates the property and `_prov` in place and `history.of` replays every value; `_prov` still records who and from where. The `Assertion` type is narrowed in 2.1 to one versioned document per (subject, property) for subjects whose types cannot be versioned (`Record`, shared identifiers rewritten by ingest), with no `supersedes`; `POST /assertions` (7.2) restated; D42 amended.
3. **4b.2 R13 and `graph.history(id, asOf)`:** served by `history.of`, `history.asOf`, `history.edges` plus the `ResolutionDecision` chain, the narrowed assertions and soft-edge evidence, with its cost (one `rid` lookup plus one read per version) and the OC and markings predicate applied in the procedure; D45 amended.

**Round 6, item 10 (kept for the record):**
10. **Hamming band indexes (`06-stylometry-perceptual-hash.md` 3.1, open question 4):** no corpus-wide band index. PDQ with 8 bands (radius 15) costs about 18 B per band per distinct image = 144 B, 1.35B x 144 B = 194 GB at mid 3x next to S-PDQ's 54 GB; pHash with 2 bands 36 B = 49 GB. Decision recorded in 0.4 (`media_<p>` table), 1.2, section 4, 4c.1 S-PDQ, 10.2 and the model (`HOT_BAND_B`): PDQ band keys (`Image.pdqBands BY ITEM`) only on the `media_<p>` partitions inside the hot window and on case databases, sized from the hot-tier distinct-image count (110M hot records x 1.5 = 165M images = 23.8 GB today; 200M x 1.5 = 300M = 43.2 GB at the cap), dropped when a partition leaves the window; radius-31 corpus-wide near-dup is the S-PDQ IVF space; corpus-wide pHash is not stored because PDQ supersedes it. Footprint and triggers recomputed in 0.5, 0.6, the executive summary, 1.2, 4c.1, WS1, R1, R24, Q2 and D6: mid today 1,416.7 GB (1,239 face-free), mid 3x 4,142.3 GB (92% of 4,520), face-free 1,560 GB crossing month 4.2 to 3.8 (about 2027-02, still Q1), second-drive trigger month 19.5 to 19.1 (2028-06), slope 96.5 / 108.0 GB per month. 02 does not discuss near-duplicate lookup; appendix B of 02 records the decision.

**Round 10 (fixed hardware with 4 TB drives, 2026-11-03):** the owner directed "we need to do as much as we can with the hardware we already have. Expect hardware to not change", then "Let's plan on expanding the hard drives to 4 TB" (relayed by the stakeholder write-up session). Checked in code before applying: `ha-raft` builds one Raft group per cluster (`RaftHAServer`, the group id from the cluster name, every configured peer a member), applies every entry on every peer through `ArcadeStateMachine.databaseFor`, and treats an unusable database on apply as a critical halt; `HA_AUTO_ACQUIRE_DATABASES` only governs acquisition of unseen databases; Ratis can host several groups per server (`RaftLogCacheEviction` iterates `server.getGroupIds()`). Model re-run (`docs/spork/model/budget.py`, a round-10 section; every earlier printed line unchanged): the 4 TB ceiling, runway per scenario, the option savings, combinations A to E with their trigger dates, the retention size, the RF2 layout, the ROC cap, and the CPU-only encode plan.

**Round 10 decision (2026-11-03):** the owner chose **plan C** ("Plan C", relayed by the stakeholder write-up session): combination A plus E26, warm sealed partitions on 2 of 4 nodes, over plan D's retention window. Recorded in the executive summary (replication paragraph, fixed-hardware paragraph, the 4 TB row), the status line, 0.8 (the recommendation and the combination table), section 8 (E26 committed: the P0c spike in Phase 0, in production by about 2027-07; the retention row kept only as the fallback if the spike fails or P0b measures high), R34, 9.2 Q24, D1 (replication holds for hot data only) and D56. Still open: option 4 inside A (scene and tattoo on the hot window), OCR on the daily stream only, and whether an object store exists. Model (`budget.py`): plan C without option 4 holds mid to month 26.0 (2029-01) at 96% at 3x, and E26's deadline moves from month 8.9 to 8.6, still about 2027-07, so the decision does not depend on the open option.
